# Unit 1 Knowing yourself

## Unit overview

This unit, centering around self-discovery, aims to help Ss reflect on critical moments in different life stages, probe into the process of growing up and self-development, and think about their rights and responsibilities not only on an individual level but also from a broader perspective encompassing the nation and society. The text in Active reading presents a fourth-year college student's account of how he puzzled over career choices and how his father showed him the importance of getting to know himself and finding out his own passion by taking him to catch crabs. The text in Further exploration introduces various rites of passage to mark the transition from childhood to adulthood in different cultures. Then the video explains the meaning of the eight stages in a person's development. In Project, Ss are asked to share their own personal growth stories via podcast. The text in Extended reading introduces specific ways of getting to know ourselves, including elements of self-knowledge and techniques that can help us discover our own strengths and weaknesses. By learning this unit, Ss will deepen their understanding of their inner self, appreciate the process of learning, improve critical thinking skills, develop a stronger sense of social responsibility, and acquire a global vision, which will extend to their making of informed life choices that contribute to building a prosperous, beautiful world with their wisdom and strength.

## Scenario

一档英语播客节目正在为其名为"成长与人生"的专辑征集个人成长故事。学生将参与此次征集，分享人生中的重要成长瞬间。

- **Active reading**（Catching crabs）：阅读一名美国大四学生和父亲通过抓螃蟹探讨如何选定人生之路的故事，审视内心，探寻自我，实现奋斗目标与幸福人生。
- **Further exploration – Text**（The passage to adulthood）：阅读有关不同文化中成人仪式的文章，理解世界各地成人礼的不同特征和文化内涵，反思成人礼的意义以及责任与担当的重要性。
- **Further exploration – Video**（Eight stages of development）：观看视频，了解埃里克森人生发展八阶段理论，认识人生不同发展阶段及其特点，反思不同阶段对成长的意义，不断磨砺、充盈自我，追求有意义的人生旅程。

**Project**：回顾个人成长经历，围绕人生中重要事件、重要人物对自己的影响，讲述难忘的成长瞬间，分享人生感悟。

**Extended reading**（Helpful ways to know yourself）：阅读关于自我认知的文章，学习深入认识、评估自身优缺点的方法，挖掘自身优势，获得发展的信心和动力。

**Self-reflection**：回顾整个单元的学习，反思在内容、语言、结构、交际和行为上的收获。

回顾个人成长经历，分享难忘的成长瞬间，深入理解人生的每个发展阶段及其意义，学习自我认知的方法和策略，以正确的人生观、价值观和世界观指导自己的选择。

## Scenario

The scenario in this unit can be analyzed from the following aspects:

**Setting:** Recording a story for an English-language podcast

**Topic:** A significant personal growth story

**Identity:** Storyteller – Students
                Audience – Podcast listeners

**Purpose:** To inform people of a critical moment of growth in life by telling a story

**Teaching suggestions**

T can help Ss generate ideas by asking Ss to do this exercise: First, have them take out a blank sheet of paper and draw a line down the middle. Second, instruct them to chart the formative moments of their lives on the line (e.g., any moves from one location to another, any events that have shaped or changed them, and any people who have influenced them). Third, once they have completed their timeline, ask them to choose the most formative moment that they would like to share.

Then T can help Ss structure the story about this most formative moment by listing the key elements in storytelling, i.e., setup – conflict – resolution. Ss can follow this structure and list some key ideas. For example, they might write down the time, the place, and the characters of their stories for the setup part. Next, they can share their ideas in groups and ask questions to help each other clarify the stories.

After the tryout, T can ask Ss to reflect on their difficulties in four aspects, i.e., content (how to describe the formative moment and their feelings), language (the expressions, sentence structures, and storytelling style), structure (how to organize a story), and communication (how to use vocal variety to make the story inspiring), and write them down in the table in the Student's Book.

## Active reading

In Scenario, Ss are expected to tell an inspiring story about the most formative moments in their lives. The text is a college student's memoir of such a moment. It vividly depicts the moment when his father took him to catch crabs and how it contributed to his understanding of himself and his life choices. It can help Ss better understand the theme of the unit, e.g., how one grows up and how important each growth moment is. Ss can learn the writing technique of keeping the narrative flow.

### Before you read

**Teaching suggestions**

The purpose of this warm-up activity is to encourage Ss to reflect on their personal experiences of overcoming challenges in college and explore the significance of these experiences for their personal growth. The questions are open-ended and Ss are not required to read the text beforehand. T can ask Ss to work in pairs first and then ask some Ss to share their responses with the whole class.

**Reference answers**

1   During my college life, I encountered various difficulties that tested my resilience and adaptability. One major challenge was managing a heavy workload and balancing multiple academic responsibilities. Additionally, adjusting to a new and independent lifestyle away from home presented its own set of
（页底截断，接下页）

challenges.

2   To overcome these difficulties, I implemented several strategies. Firstly, I developed effective time management skills and created a structured schedule to prioritize tasks and ensure I met deadlines. Seeking support from professors, advisors, and classmates was also crucial. Their guidance and advice helped me navigate academic challenges and provided valuable insights. Furthermore, I actively sought resources such as tutoring, study groups, and workshops to enhance my understanding and skills in challenging subjects. Engaging in self-care activities, such as exercising, practicing mindfulness, and maintaining a healthy social life, also played a significant role in managing stress and maintaining overall well-being.

### Catching crabs

**Text interpretation**

本文作者以自己大学生涯的最后一年为背景，描述了自己对于未来人生选择的迷茫，并讲述了父亲通过带领自己抓螃蟹、观察螃蟹的行为来鼓励自己找到自身优势、勇敢追寻梦想的宝贵经历。第一部分（至第二段）为故事的铺垫，讲述了作者和同学们身处毕业季，对未来的选择普遍感到迷茫。第二部分（第三至第二十七段）讲述了作者假期回家后被问到毕业后的规划时不敢说出真实想法，而父亲察觉到了自己的纠结，于是带自己出海捕捞螃蟹，并让自己通过观察螃蟹的行为审视内心，探寻自我。第三部分（第二十八至第三十五段）讲述了自己从父亲的话和观察螃蟹中得出的人生感悟。

文章通过叙述一位父亲为了帮助儿子认清自我、找到人生方向而带领儿子抓螃蟹、观察螃蟹的故事，传递了每个人都应该探寻内心、用好特长，敢于追梦的人生哲理。基于课文，教师可深入挖掘并运用思政教育元素，引导学生探讨自我认知、成长成才、职业规划、人生选择、个体社会价值等话题，帮助学生意识到人生目标有不同，职业选择有差异，但重要的是发挥自身优势、拥有真才实学、敢于创新创造，更好实现人生价值。

本文是一篇典型的记叙文，叙事线非常明晰，情节推进流畅。从写作手法上看，本文最突出的特征是以过渡句、直接引语、时间表达等的切换自然、流畅地推进故事。文章对人物语言、行为和心理活动的描写非常细腻。教师可结合理解练习1和2引导学生分析叙事结构，理解故事大意，关注细节运用。同时，结合写作技巧分析部分的讲解和练习帮助学生掌握叙事技巧，并在产出任务 Sharing your ideas 中有效使用。

**Language points**

1   **The idle days and relaxed atmosphere of the preceding summer semester had disappeared. (Para. 1)**

**Meaning:** The leisurely days and relaxed atmosphere of the previous summer semester had all been gone.

**preceding:** *a.* (*only before noun*) (*fml.*) happening or coming before the time, place, or part mentioned 在前的；在先的；前面的
*Our contact information can be found on the preceding pages.*

2   **We all started to get our heads down, studying late, and attendance at classes rose steeply again. (Para. 1)**

**Meaning:** The final year of college life usually means a busy time for students. They have to prepare for their final exams, and they may have to write their graduation papers. That's why the author said they had to study late and go back to classes and libraries.

**get one's head down:** work in a steady and quiet way 专心工作；埋头苦干
*I'm going to get my head down and finish the report before I go back home today.*

3   **Most important of course were the final exams in April and May in the following year. (Para. 1)**

**Notes:** This sentence uses inversion to place emphasis on the importance of the final exams and balance the flow of the sentence, i.e., when the subject of a sentence is too long, we can use inversion to keep the balance of the sentence. One more example:
*Very popular is the notion that intelligence is measured by your ability to solve complex problems.*

4   **No one wanted the humiliation of finishing last in class, so peer pressure to succeed was fierce. (Para. 1)**

**peer pressure:** *n.* [U] the strong feeling that you must do the same things as other people of your age if you want them to like you 同辈之间的压力
*Positive peer pressure can inspire individuals to strive for personal growth and pursue their passions with determination.*

5   **Libraries were even standing room only until the early hours of the morning. (Para. 1)**

**standing room only:** no more seats available 只有站位
*The concert was so popular that the venue was filled to capacity, with standing room only.*

**Notes:** The compound noun "standing room" means "space where people can stand to watch a performance or sports event, or when traveling on a bus or train."

6   **Most people were looking either to continue their studies ... (Para. 2)**

**be looking to do sth.:** be planning or expecting to do sth. 打算、期待做某事
*The government is looking to address the issue by offering more flexible job opportunities.*

7   **During my customary visit home for Thanksgiving, inevitably my brothers and sisters kept asking me what I was planning to do. (Para. 3)**

**Meaning:** The sentence shows that the author's family members gather together for Thanksgiving celebrations and that his family members are concerned about what he is going to do after graduation.

**customary:** *a.* (*only before noun*) sb.'s customary behavior is the way they usu. do things （人的行为）习惯性的
*He was not his customary cheerful self that day.*

8   **I instinctively braced myself for some resistance to the idea. (Para. 10)**

**brace oneself for sth.:** mentally or physically prepare oneself for sth. unpleasant that is going to happen 准备迎接（令人不快的事）
*She told me she had some bad news for me and I braced myself for a shock.*

9   **It was a small motorboat, moored 10 minutes away, and my father had owned it for years. (Para. 19)**

**moor:** *v.* fasten a ship or boat to the land or to the bottom of the sea using ropes or an anchor 系泊；（使）停泊
*Wendy had moored her boat on the right bank of the river.*

10  **There was no surf on the coastal waters at that time of day, so it was a smooth half-hour ride. (Para. 20)**

**Notes:** Both "surf" and "ride" can be used both as verbs and nouns.

11  **We waited a while, then my father stood up and said, "Give me a hand with this," and we hauled up the crab cage onto the deck. (Para. 21)**

**Notes:** This sentence vividly shows a sequence of actions that happened one right after another, which makes the writing very lively.

12  **The cage was brimming with dozens of crabs piled high on top of each other. (Para. 23)**

**be brimming with:** have a lot of a particular thing, quality, or emotion 充满（某种事物、品质或情感）
*Her eyes were brimming with tears of joy as she accepted the award.*

13  **"Just watch them for a moment. Look at that one, there! He's trying to climb out, but every time the other crabs pull him back in," said my father. (Para. 24)**

**Notes:** It's important to note that the use of "he" in this context is not meant to indicate the actual gender of the crab, as crabs do not possess inherent gender identities in the same way humans do. Instead, it serves a narrative purpose, bringing the crab to life in the reader's imagination.

14  **Several times the crab tried to escape, without luck. (Para. 25)**

**Notes:** "Several times" is used at the beginning of the sentence for emphasis. The expression nicely echoes the previous sentence and stresses the futile efforts of the crab who wanted to escape.

15  **Not only did the crab give up its lengthy struggle to escape, but it actually began to help stop other crabs from trying to escape. (Para.27)**

**Notes:** The sentence uses the structure "not only ... but (also)" to depict how the crab quit its own struggle for escape and even tried to stop other crabs from running away.

16  **Spend some time figuring out who you are and what you want in life. (Para. 28)**

**figure out:** be able to understand sth. or solve a problem 理解；明白；想出
*We had to figure out the connection between the two events.*

**Teaching suggestions**

This activity aims to help Ss grasp the main idea of the story. T can draw Ss' attention to how the author maintained the narrative flow. For example, the author uses time expressions such as "In the fall of our final year at college," and "during my customary visit home," direct speech like "Er ... so what?", and transitional sentences like "But there was something else." By analyzing these elements, Ss will be able to identify the setting, the conflict, and the resolution of the story, and grasp the main idea of it.

4) pulled it back
5) got bored with
6) the easy way of life

**Analyzing writing techniques**

**Reference answers**

1   The use of the time expression, together with the past tense, is a signal that this is a narrative containing a sequence of events.

2   This indicates that studying for the final exams was not the only concern that students had. It indicates a transition from studying for final exams to the next topic – their plans after graduation.

3   This change to direct speech opens a moment-by-moment dialog. This slows down the narration of events. It also shows how the author found it difficult to say what he wanted to do and how the father wanted to have a frank and straightforward conversation with him.

4   The topic shifts to crabs. The sentence also arouses readers' interest – we expect some fascinating new information about crabs and how the topic of crabs is connected with the son's future plans.

5   The conclusion is quite effective. The statement does not tell what really happens next, but the implication is fairly clear. The father would support the son's choice to become a writer. Not stating it clearly leaves more room for personal interpretation.

Young people inevitably face numerous choices. We need to understand not only what fascinates us but also what society expects from us, and let our choices be guided by a correct worldview, outlook on life, and sense of values. The life experiences of preceding generations suggest that young people who choose to endure hardships will be rewarded, and those who choose to make contributions to society are the ones who deserve respect. In the new era, Chinese youth are fortunate to witness the best period of development for the Chinese nation. We should be determined to become better versions of ourselves and shoulder the responsibilities of the times.

**Sharing your ideas**

**Reference answers**

• It happened when I was in junior high school.
• My shyness became an increasingly frustrating aspect of my life. Every time I was asked to read aloud or speak in front of others, my heart would pound, and I couldn't help but tremble with nervousness.
• I felt hopeless and powerless. My shyness was as if a wall stood between me and the world, preventing me from fully participating in social and academic settings.
• My Chinese teacher suggested that I join the school debate club. She told me that I should step outside my comfort zone and face my fears head-on. I hesitantly agreed, although my nerves were still running high during the initial training sessions. But eventually, I fell in love with it. I started to come out of my shell and began improving the other areas of my life. I was no longer a shy student.
• From that experience, I learned that growth and self-improvement often lie on the other side of our fears. By embracing new challenges and pushing ourselves beyond our comfort zones, we can unlock hidden potentials and transform ourselves into stronger individuals. Looking back, I am grateful for the opportunity to join the debate club and for the encouragement I received from my teacher. It changed my perspective on shyness and showed me that with determination and the right support, we can conquer our fears and discover the amazing person we are meant to become.

**Suggestions for evaluation**

For this activity, Ss' story plan can be evaluated according to the following criteria:

• whether it provides enough vivid details about what happened and how they experienced personal growth
• whether it is organized clearly

## Further exploration

The task in Scenario asks Ss to record a podcast story sharing their personal growth moments. The text in this part can help Ss better understand the different features and symbolic meanings of coming-of-age ceremonies in various cultures. The video introduces Erikson's theory of eight stages in an individual's psychosocial development. The input material in this part provides more information about the process of learning and growing up, which can prepare Ss for telling a personal growth story. T can help Ss integrate the main ideas of Active reading and Further exploration by asking Ss to compare and contrast the significance of passage to adulthood in different cultures and reflect on their own developmental stages.

### The passage to adulthood (Text)

**Culture notes**

1   **Rite of passage**
A rite of passage is an official ceremony or informal activity that marks an important stage or occasion in a person's life, e.g., becoming an adult. "Rite" means a traditional ceremony, while "passage" here means a transition or journey from one stage or status to another, such as adolescence to adulthood, student to graduate, or singlehood to marriage.

2   **As You Like It**
*As You Like It* is a comedy by William Shakespeare, written and performed in about 1598 - 1600, and it revolves around themes of love, identity, and the complexities of human relationships. The most famous speech in *As You Like It* is the Seven Ages of Man, which begins "All the world's a stage, and all the men and women merely players; they have their exits and their entrances, and one man in his time plays many parts, his acts being seven ages." The seven ages are the infant, the whining schoolboy, the lover, the soldier, the justice, the lean and slippered pantaloon, and second childishness and mere oblivion.

3   **The Book of Rites**
*The Book of Rites*, also known as *Liji*, is a collection of texts describing the social forms, administration, and ceremonial rites of the Zhou Dynasty as they were understood in the Warring States and the early Han periods. *The Book of Rites* contains a comprehensive and systematical record and interpretation of Confucian thoughts and occupies an important position in the Confucianism system, so it was upgraded to one of the "Five Classics."

4   **Capping ceremony**
The capping ceremony is called *guan li* in Chinese. In ancient China, when a young man came of age, at 20, an important ceremony was held to mark the event. During the ceremony, he would wear different types of caps, hence, the name of the ceremony. Such a ritual suggested that a young man came of age, not only in terms of his physical constitution but also in terms of the moral standing required of him as an adult. Having gone through this rite, a man was considered qualified to shoulder his responsibilities as an adult in life and take part in important ceremonies or activities.

5   **Hair-pinning ceremony**
Hair-pinning ceremony is called *ji li* in Chinese. The character *ji* refers to a kind of bamboo-made hairpin. Before the ceremony, a girl would tie her hair in a bun on the top of her head. During the ceremony, the master of the ceremony would put a hairpin on a girl's beautifully decorated bun, and that signaled she was now an adult. The master was often a highly respected married woman and the girl's relative. Often the ceremony was held when a girl turned 15 and was engaged, but sometimes the function was postponed to her wedding day.

6   **The prom**
The prom is an end-of-year dinner dance for senior (final-year) high school students in the U.S. The prom originated in the 1920s, following the 19th-century European tradition of "a promenade ball" at which young people could dance and have the opportunity to merge themselves into society. American parents thought the prom would be a good formal social occasion to teach school leavers about etiquette. On prom night, the boys generally wear black suits or tuxedos and the girls wear formal gowns or long dresses. There is the crowning of the Prom Queen and King, who are chosen by fellow students.

7   **Pentecost Island**
Pentecost Island is a mountainous, tropical island in the Republic of Vanuatu in the South Pacific. There are no towns on Pentecost – most of the islanders live in small villages and grow their own food in small gardens. Parts of Pentecost remain relatively untouched by Western influences, and local traditions are strong. The island is the home of the *nanggol* (land diving) – the original bungee jump – which is known throughout the world.

8   **The walkabout**
The walkabout is a rite of passage undertaken by adolescent Australian aboriginals, who set off alone into the wilderness for several months. On walkabout, they come to appreciate the spiritual ties of the land, the natural world, the wildlife, and their ancestors.

9   **Songlines**
Songlines, also known as dreaming tracks, are pathways that exist in the beliefs of native Australian people. These paths, found on the land or even in the sky, represent the routes taken by spiritual beings during the sacred time of creation. The songlines are preserved through traditional songs, stories, dances, and artwork, and they play a significant role in native Australian ceremonies. They are crucial for native Australian culture as they connect people to their ancestral lands.

**Language points**

1   **The phenomenon of rites of passage in almost every society confirms that we prefer to think of life in terms of stages such as childhood, adulthood and old age. (Para.1)**

**Meaning:** Nearly every society has rites of passage, which proves that we like to think about life in terms of different stages, like childhood, adulthood and old age.

2   **A rite of passage is a formal recognition of a move from one stage to another, the most universally recognized one being the transition between childhood and adulthood. (Para. 2)**

**Meaning:** The sentence specifically highlights the widely recognized rite of passage that occurs when an individual moves from childhood to adulthood and suggests that this transition is universally celebrated.

**universally:** *ad.* by everyone in the world or everyone in a particular group 全体地
*Her talent and achievement as an artist have been universally recognized.*

**Notes:** In this sentence, the phrase "the most universally recognized one" functions as a noun phrase modifying "rite of passage." The phrase "being the transition between childhood and adulthood" functions as a participial clause modifying the noun phrase "the most universally recognized one." It indicates that the transition between childhood and adulthood is the specific characteristic that makes the rite of passage universally recognized. A sentence with the same structure is "A graduation ceremony is a formal celebration of completing one's academic journey, the most anticipated one being the attainment of a university degree."

3   **To this day, shouldering responsibilities remains a symbol of adulthood in Chinese culture, which values an individual's contribution to the family and to the wider community. (Para. 3)**

**Meaning:** Responsibility is one of the most treasured traditional values in China. The Confucian theory of virtue stresses loyalty, faith, benevolence, righteousness, filial piety, etc. All the virtues emphasize the responsibility of individuals to others and society, such as filial piety highlighting the responsibility of children to their parents, loyalty highlighting the responsibility of their own behavior, faith highlighting the responsibility of their friends, and so on.

4   **One of the most dangerous coming-of-age ceremonies might be the land diving ceremony on Pentecost Island. Bungee jumping is considered to have originated from it. (Para. 5)**

**Meaning:** The expression "coming-of-age" refers to the transition from childhood to adulthood.

**originate:** *v.* come from a particular place or start in a particular situation 发源；开始；起源
*If one day we discover life on Mars, it may be difficult to tell whether it originated there or drifted over from another planet.*

5   **In so doing, they would penetrate to the heart of native Australian culture and, in the process, discover themselves, too. (Para. 6)**

**Meaning:** By completing the walkabout, the young people would understand the essence of native Australian culture, and meanwhile, they would learn more about themselves.

**penetrate:** *vt.* study or investigate sth. in order to understand it 洞察；了解；深入研究
*The scientist aimed to penetrate the mysteries of the universe through rigorous research and exploration.*

6   **Although different cultures hold different forms of coming-of-age ceremonies, the ceremonies are similar in that they symbolize the transition from childhood to adulthood, after which the person is no longer considered a child and is granted the full rights and responsibilities of an adult. (Para. 7)**

**Meaning:** Different cultures have their own unique coming-of-age ceremonies, but they all serve the same purpose of representing the change from being a child to becoming an adult. After this transition, people are no longer seen as children and are given all the rights and responsibilities that come with being an adult.

**symbolize:** *vt.* if sth. symbolizes a quality, feeling, etc., it represents it 象征；代表
*The dove is often used to symbolize peace and harmony.*

**Reference answers**

1
B

2
1   It is a ceremonial event that marks and signifies a person's transition from one stage of life, status, or identity to another.
2   In ancient China, there were specific rites of passage that marked the transition to adulthood. Young men would participate in a capping ceremony at the age of 20, while girls would take part in a hair-pinning ceremony at the age of 15.
3   For Chinese youth, shouldering responsibility means making contributions to the family and to the wider community, and integrating their life goals with those of the nation and the society.
4   To mark the start of their journey toward college or the world of work.
5   They may value qualities such as hard work, wisdom and courage.
6   They spend about six months walking alone through the wilderness and following the paths of their ancestors along the "songlines."

**Additional activity**

The text introduces different rites of passage across different cultures. But Ss might not fully understand the deeper meaning of these ceremonies to a person's development. This additional activity can help Ss recall the various formal or informal rites of passage they have experienced and draw their attention to the real purpose of these rituals.

T can ask Ss to discuss the following questions:

1   What is the most memorable rite of passage you have experienced?
2   What do you think is the importance of having rites of passage?

**Reference answers:**

1   To me, the "A Letter to My Future Self" ritual held at junior high school has been most unforgettable.
2   Rites of passage, formal or informal, big or small, can highlight the transitions in our life stages and give us a special opportunity to reflect upon our own development and guide us to treasure each stage. For example, when I wrote a letter to my future self, I spent lots of time thinking about what kind of person I wanted to become and how I can become that person. That motivated me to treasure every day at school and become my better self.

## （续）

### Eight stages of development (Video)

### Scripts

1 Erikson's theory of psychosocial development identifies eight stages which a healthy individual should pass through from birth to death. At each stage, we encounter different needs, ask new questions and meet people who influence our behavior and learning.

2 Stage 1: basic trust versus mistrust. As infants, we ask ourselves if we can trust the world and we wonder if it is safe. We learn that if we can trust someone now, we can also trust others in the future. If we experience fear, we develop doubt and mistrust. The key to our development is our mother.

3 Stage 2: autonomy versus shame and doubt. In our early childhood, we experience ourselves and discover our body. We ask: Is it OK to be me? If we are allowed to discover ourselves, then we develop self-confidence. If we are not, we can develop shame and self-doubt. Both parents now play a major role.

4 Stage 3: initiative versus guilt. In preschool, we take initiative, try out new things, and learn basic principles, like how round things roll. We ask: Is it OK for me to do what I do? If we are encouraged, we can follow our interests. If we are held back or told that what we do is silly, we can develop guilt. We are now learning from the entire family.

5 Stage 4: industry versus inferiority. Now we discover our own interests and realize that we are different from others. We want to show that we can do things right. We ask if we can make it in this world. If we receive recognition from our teachers or peers, we become industrious, which is another word for hard-working. If we get too much negative feedback, we start to feel inferior and lose motivation. Our neighbors and schools now influence us the most.

6 Stage 5: identity versus role confusion. During adolescence, we learn that we have different social roles. We are friends, students, children, and citizens. Many experience an identity crisis. If our parents now allow us to go out and explore, we can find identity. If they push us to conform to their views, we can face role confusion and feel lost. The (key) to our learning are (is) our peers and role models.

7 Stage 6: intimacy versus isolation. As younger adults, we slowly understand who we are and we start to let go of the relationships we had built earlier in order to fit in. We ask ourselves if we can love. If we can make a long-term commitment, we are confident and happy. If we cannot form intimate relationships, we might end up feeling isolated and lonely. Our friends and partners are now central to our development.

8 Stage 7: generativity versus stagnation. When we reach our forties, we become comfortable, use leisure our time creatively, and maybe begin contributing to society. Our concern is generativity. If we think that we are able to lead the next generation into this world, we are happy. If we did not resolve some conflicts earlier, we can become pessimistic and experience stagnation. People at home and at work are now who influence us the most.

9 Stage 8: ego integrity versus despair. As we grow older, we tend to slow down and begin to look back over our lives. We ask: How have I done? If we think we did well, we develop feelings of contentment and integrity. If not, we can experience despair and become grumpy and bitter.

### Culture notes

**Erik Erikson**

Erik Erikson (1902-1994) is one of the most influential psychoanalysts of the 20th century. He was born in Frankfurt-am-Main, Germany. Erikson's best-known work is his theory that each stage of life is associated with a specific psychological struggle, a struggle that contributes to a major aspect of personality. Each phase was the potential root of later health and pathology. By focusing on the social as well as the psychological, Erikson's stages represented a quantum leap in Freudian thought.

### Language points

1 **Erikson's theory of *psychosocial* development identifies eight stages which a healthy individual should pass through from birth to death.** (Para. 1)

*psychosocial*: *a.* relating to both sb's mind and how they behave with other people 社会心理的
*Psychosocial factors significantly impact human development.*

2 **At each stage, we encounter different needs, ask new questions, and meet people who influence our behavior and learning.** (Para. 1)

**Meaning:** When we progress through different stages of life, our needs change, new questions arise, and we encounter individuals who have an impact on our behavior and learning. The sentence highlights the idea that growth and learning are ongoing processes influenced by various factors people encounter at each stage of life.

3 **In preschool, we take *initiative*, try out new things, and learn basic principles, like how round things roll.** (Para. 4)

*initiative*: *n.* [U] the ability to make decisions and take action without waiting for sb. to tell you what to do 自主决断的能力；主动性
*If you notice that something is going wrong, take the initiative to fix it.*

**Notes:** There are other verbs that can be used in combination with "initiative," e.g., "show / exercise / demonstrate / embrace / encourage." For example:
*The company encourages its employees to embrace the initiative and take ownership of their projects.*
*John consistently exercises initiative in his work by taking on additional responsibilities.*

4 **If we get too much negative feedback, we start to feel inferior and lose motivation.** (Para. 5)

**Meaning:** Too much criticism may lead to our loss of confidence and a decrease in our drive to endeavor.

5 **If they push us to *conform to* their views, we can face role confusion and feel lost.** (Para. 6)

*conform to*: behave in the way that most other people in your group or society behave 从众；顺应
（页底截断，接下页）

（接上页）
*In order to maintain harmony within the group, it is often expected to conform to the established norms and rules.*

6 **As young adults, we slowly understand who we are and we start to *let go* of the relationships we had built earlier in order to fit in.** (Para. 7)

**Meaning:** As young adults, we slowly get to know more about ourselves and start to give up old relationships that we had developed in order to be accepted by our peers.

*let go (of sb. / sth.)*: stop holding sb. or sth. 放开；松手
*In order to find inner peace and move forward, it is essential to let go of past sadness and resentments.*

7 **If we did not resolve some conflicts earlier, we can become pessimistic and experience *stagnation*.** (Para. 8)

**Meaning:** If we did not solve previous problems, we can become pessimistic and stop development.

*stagnation*: *n.* [U] the fact of no longer developing or making progress 停滞；不发展；不进步
*Due to the lack of innovation and progress, the company experienced stagnation, leading to a decline in its market position.*

8 **If we think we did well, we develop feelings of contentment and integrity.** (Para. 9)

**Meaning:** If we have done well in a particular task, or aspect of our lives, we experience a feeling of satisfaction and a sense of moral wholeness. The word "integrity" means a sense of completeness felt by the senior people when they look back at their own lives.

### Sharing your ideas

**Reference answers**

• It is meaningful to me because it highlights the importance of embracing challenges and pushing ourselves beyond our comfort zones. It demonstrates that by confronting personal fears and taking risks, I can unlock my hidden potential and achieve personal growth.

• whether Ss can clearly summarize the value of the moment

## Project

**Sample:**
(*Words and expressions in red are from the input materials.)

In my junior high school, I struggled with overwhelming shyness. Every time I was asked to speak or read aloud in front of others, my heart would pound, and I couldn't help but tremble with nervousness.

One day, during a particularly nerve-wracking class, my Chinese teacher, Mrs. Li, noticed my struggle. She approached me with a compassionate smile and said, "You have so much potential. Have you ever considered joining the school debate club?"

I looked at her in surprise, my mind racing with doubts and fears. "Debate club?" I stammered. "I don't know if I can do it."

Mrs. Li nodded. "I understand that it's challenging, but sometimes, facing our fears head-on is the best way to grow. You have a unique perspective to share, and I believe that the debate club can help you gain confidence and discover your true abilities."

Hesitant yet intrigued, I agreed to give it a try, unsure of what lay ahead. As the weeks went by, I attended the debate club's training sessions, feeling a mixture of excitement and nervousness. During one particularly intense practice debate, my nerves got the best of me, and I faltered in my arguments.

After the debate, my debate partner, David, approached me with a reassuring smile. "Don't worry, we all started out feeling like that," he said. "It takes time to get comfortable, but you'll get there."

His words resonated with me, and I realized that the journey to overcoming my shyness was not a quick fix but a gradual process. I decided to spend more time practicing and preparing for debates, determined to improve and overcome my fear of public speaking.

Months passed, and as I actively participated in debates and engaged in discussions with my fellow club members, I started to feel a shift within me. The once overwhelming fear began to fade, replaced by a newfound sense of confidence and self-assurance.

During a debate tournament, as I confidently presented my arguments, Mrs. Li watched from the sidelines, a proud smile on her face. Afterward, she approached me, congratulating me on my progress. "You've come a long way," she said. "I always believed in you. Remember, growth and self-improvement often lie on the other side of our fears."

Her words struck a chord in my heart, marking the start of a deeper realization. I had always assumed that my shyness was an unchangeable part of my personality. However, by embracing new challenges, I discovered my hidden potentials and transformed into a stronger individual.

Looking back on that formative moment, I realized that it wasn't just about becoming a better debater. It was about embracing new challenges, pushing beyond my comfort zone, and ultimately, discovering the amazing person I was meant to be.

**Suggestions for evaluation**

| Components | Criteria / Traits |
| --- | --- |
| **Content** | • Relevance: Addressing the assigned task by telling a personal story that focuses on a formative moment in life as well as its gains and by explaining the value of this moment to one's growth<br>• Appeal: Developing setting, characters and plot properly, using details and tension, showing a sense of audience |
| **Organization** | • Structure: Using the setup – conflict – resolution pattern<br>• Coherence: Using a consistent first-person narration<br>• Technique: Making appropriate use of dialogues, direct speech and transitional sentences to make the story each to follow |
| **Language** | • Diversity: Using an adequate range of vocabulary and sentence structures<br>• Vividness: Using stylistic devices, adding lively details<br>• Acquisition: Using as many as possible of the new words, expressions, and grammatical structures learned in the unit |
| **Delivery** | • Fluency: Presenting in an effortless, smooth and clear way with a natural colloquial flow<br>• Engagement: Using suitable speaking rate and volume, varying the pitch, pausing properly, and getting rid of unnecessary fillers |

## Extended reading

In the previous parts, Ss learned to share their own personal growth stories. This part provides an essay to help Ss make further exploration about specific ways of getting to know oneself. The essay first stresses the importance of self-knowledge and then introduces specific techniques of getting to know oneself. Ss can make informed choices based on deep analysis of their own strengths and weaknesses.

### Helpful ways to know yourself

### Culture notes

**Socrates**, (470 B.C. – 399 B.C.), was an ancient Greek philosopher whose way of life, character, and thought exerted a profound influence on Western philosophy.

### Language points

1 **These famous words are often incorrectly *attributed* to the Greek philosopher Socrates.** (Para. 1)

**Meaning:** People often attribute these words to Socrates, but he did not actually say or write them.

*attribute*: *v.* (~ sth. to sb. / sth.) if people in general attribute a particular statement, painting, piece of music, etc. to sb., they believe that person said it, painted it, etc. 认为……是……所说（所作等）
*Many people attribute the famous quote to Albert Einstein, believing that he was the one who first uttered those words.*

2 **Research suggests that adopting *intrinsic* values such as social responsibility, a desire for personal growth, and meaningful relationships can lead to greater happiness and overall life satisfaction compared to *extrinsic* values like financial success, physical attractiveness, and social recognition.** (Para. 2)

**Meaning:** According to research, compared with external values such as wealth, physical charm and social recognition, internal values such as social responsibility, personal growth desire and meaningful relationships can make one feel happier and more satisfied with life.

*intrinsic*: *a.* being part of the nature or character of sb. or sth. 内在的；本质的；固有的
*Encouragement and recognition are an intrinsic part of our school culture.*

*extrinsic*: *a.* (*fml.*) coming from outside or not directly relating to sth. 外在的；无直接联系的
*Extrinsic motivation is something driven by external rewards, like money and promotion.*

3 **Psychology experiments demonstrate that worthwhile values can provide you with the necessary motivation to keep going even when you are exhausted.** (Para. 3)

**Meaning:** Psychology experiments show that meaningful values motivate you to keep going even when you are extremely tired.

4 **You might be an introvert, which means you *restore* your energy from being alone. Or you might be an extrovert, and restore your energy from being with other people. Maybe you make decisions based on analysis. Or perhaps you prefer to follow your feelings. Maybe you love details. Or you may prefer big ideas.** (Para. 5)

*restore*: *vt.* make sth. return to its former state or condition 使恢复
*Measures have been taken to restore the public's confidence in the company.*

**Notes:** In this paragraph, the author uses "maybe" and "or" to start a series of simple sentences that describe one's inborn preferences. The string of sentences makes the writing rhythmical.

5 **When you have a strong curiosity toward something, it can give you clues to your deepest passions.** (Para. 10)

**Meaning:** Following and exploring one's curiosity can lead to a better understanding of their deepest passions in life.

6 **The more you know about these areas of your life and character, the more energy and confidence you will have. You'll feel more motivated to appreciate life, grow as a person, improve yourself, and connect with others.** (Para. 11)

**Notes:** This sentence summarizes the main points stated in previous paragraphs and effectively reinforces the thesis of the text.

**Reference answers**
1) A
2) B
3) C
4) D
5) D

## Self-reflection

In this part, T can ask Ss to refer back to Try it out in the Student's Book and reflect on what they have learned to complete the task. Ss can categorize their gains according to the same four aspects, i.e., content, language, structure, and communication. They can also reflect on how learning the unit may influence their view on personal growth and development and how they make important decisions in life. Then they can write down their specific gains in the self-reflection table in the Student's Book.

## Translation of the texts

### Active reading

#### 抓螃蟹

1 大学最后一年的秋天，我们的心情变了。上个夏季学期的闲暇时光和轻松气氛一去不复返了。我们开始埋头学习，苦读到深夜，课堂出勤率再次急剧上升。我们都觉得在校时间不多了。我们意识到以后再也不会有这样的学习机会了，所以都下定决心不再虚度光阴。我们知道这个阶段的任何闪失都会让自己付出不菲的代价。最为重要的是下一年四五月份的期末考试。我们谁都不想考全班倒数第一，那也太丢人了，因此同学们之间的竞争压力特别大。图书馆甚至在凌晨时分都还座无虚席。

2 还有别的事情让大家心情焦虑。每个人都在心里盘算着过几个月毕业离校之后该找份什么样的工作。大多数同学不是准备继续深造，就是想在银行、地方政府或其他单位当个白领，薪水足够多，让他们得以在二十出头的时候过上舒适的生活，然后就娶妻生子，贷款买房，期望升职，过安稳日子。

3 感恩节的时候我照例回了一趟家，兄弟姐妹们免不了不停地问我毕业后有什么打算。我想他们要是知道我的计划也许会批评我，所以只对他们说了别人都准备干什么。

4 父亲看着我，什么也没说。夜深时，他把我叫去书房，给我们俩各倒了杯饮料。

5 "怎么样？" 他问。

6 "啊，什么怎么样？"

7 "你毕业后到底想做什么？" 他问道。

8 父亲是一名律师，我一直都认为他想让我去法学院深造，追随他的人生足迹，所以我有点儿犹豫。

9 过了会儿我回答说："我想旅行，我想当个作家。"

10 我想这不是他所期待的答案。旅行？去哪儿旅行？当作家？写什么？我本能地做好了可能会遭到反对的准备。我确信他会把这一时冲动而予以否定。

11 接着是一阵长长的沉默。

12 "这想法有点意思，" 最后他开口说道。

13 接着又是一阵长长的沉默。

14 "我有点希望自己在你这个年纪时也能做这些事儿。"

15 我在等他把话说完。

16 "你还有很多时间，不必马上进入一个报酬还不错的行业。你现在要搞清楚自己真正喜欢什么，如果你弄不清楚，以后就不可能开心。"

17 "那我该怎么弄清楚呢？"

18 他想了一会儿。然后他说道："瞧，现在太晚了。我们明天早晨乘船出海去，就我们爷儿俩。也许我们能抓点螃蟹当晚餐，我们还可以再多谈谈。"

19 那是一艘小小的机动船，停泊在离我们家约十分钟路程的地方，是些年前父亲买的。次日清晨，我们沿着港湾出发。

20 在这个时候沿海水域没什么风浪，船平稳地航行着半个小时就到了。"咱们在这儿试试运气吧，" 父亲一边说，一边抓起一个系着绳子的网篓抛到海里。

21 我们等了一会儿，父亲站起来对我说："来帮我一把。" 于是我们一起将蟹篓子抛上了甲板。

22 螃蟹吸引了我，它们太容易抓了。它们不仅爬进了一个再明显不过的陷阱，而且即便你把盖子打开，它们似乎也懒得从里面爬出来。

23 篓子里挤满了十只螃蟹，一只压着一只，堆得老高。"它们为什么不逃走呢？" 我满腹疑惑地问父亲。

24 "你先观察一下。看那只螃蟹，那儿！它想爬出去，但每次都被同伴拽了回去，" 父亲说。

25 我们接着观察。那只螃蟹顺着网眼向顶盖攀援，每当它爬到顶盖时，就会有另一只螃蟹举起蟹钳夹把它拽下来。这只螃蟹尝试了好几次想挣脱，但都没能成功。

26 "快看！" 父亲说。"它开始对这场游戏感到不耐烦了。"

27 这只螃蟹不仅放弃了漫长的逃跑挣扎，而且还帮着把其他想逃跑的螃蟹拽下来。这是个信号，说明它接受了安逸的生活。

28 我忽然明白了父亲的用意。他看着我说："你可别被别人拽下来。花点时间想想你是谁，你这一生希望得到什么。想想什么真正使你感兴趣，你有什么技能。尝试弄清楚你想在哪里生活，你想去哪里，以及想做什么样的工作。如果你现在不能回答这些问题，你就得花点时间去找出答案。因为如果你不弄清楚的话，你永远都不会感到快乐和满足。"

29 他停顿了一下。

30 "你想去旅行？" 他接着问我。

31 "对，" 我回答说。

32 "那就去申请护照吧。你想当作家？"

33 "我想是的。"

34 "有趣的选择，我们家还没出过作家呢，" 他说。

35 父亲启动了发动机。我们启程回家了。

### Further exploration

#### 成人仪式

1 莎士比亚在他的戏剧《皆大欢喜》中指出，人的一生要经历"七个阶段"。几乎每个社会都存在的成人仪式也证明，我们往往把生命分为几个阶段来看待，比如童年、中年和老年。

2 重要仪式是对个人从一个阶段走向另一阶段的正式认可，其中被广泛认同的是由少年步入成年时举行的成年礼。成年礼有多种形式。在中国古代，人们举行大型仪式来纪念"成年"。这一仪式至少可以追溯到 2000 多年前的周朝。据《礼记》记载，青年男子应该在 20 岁时行冠礼，而女孩则在 15 岁时行笄礼。仪式还表明年轻人应该承担起家庭和社会责任。时至今日，在重视对家庭和社会贡献的中国文化中，肩负责任仍然是成年的象征。中国人民坚信，当年轻人将自己的人生目标与国家和人民的目标相结合时，他们的生活会更加充实。

3 在当代美国，成人仪式也很隆重。高中毕业时举办的正式舞会就是一个例子。学生们身着正装（许多学生平生第一次这么穿），而且通常会租车往返。毕业舞会标志着学生们步入大学校园或迈入职场的旅程的开始。

4 在某些文化中，成人仪式的形式很不寻常。最危险的成人仪式之一可能是瓦努阿图彭特科斯特岛上的"陆地潜水"仪式。人们认为该仪式是蹦极的起源。在仪式之前，年轻人要花费几个星期的时间用藤蔓和原木搭建一座高大的"跳塔"。仪式当天，他们从高台上跳下，以证明自己的男子气概。正确而安全地跳跃需要高超的技巧。村里的人们会放声高歌，以表示他们对这些青年男子勇敢行动的支持。

5 世界上最有趣的成人仪式之一或许就是澳大利亚原住民的"徒步旅行"了。还处于青春期的少年必须在野外独自行走六个月，他们需要沿着划定疆域的古老"歌之版图"追寻祖先的足迹。通过这样的仪式，他们深入到澳大利亚土著文化的精髓之中，并在这一过程中发现自我。

6 尽管不同的文化举行不同形式的成人仪式，但这些仪式的相似之处在于，它们象征着从童年到成年的过渡。此后一个人将不再被视为儿童，同时也被授予了成年人的全部权利和责任。

### Extended reading

#### 认识自己的有益方法

1 "认识自己。" 这句名言经常被错误地认为是古希腊哲学家苏格拉底说的。但是，当你"认识自己"时，你究竟在认识什么？让我们来看看我们生活中的几个不同领域，并思考我们可以从这些领域中认识自己什么。

2 第一个也是最重要的领域是价值观。价值观是你认为在生活中应该遵循的重要原则和做法。生活中有许多不同的价值观，而且并不是所有的价值观都相互兼容。采用一套特定的价值观是否会使你感到更快乐、更健康、更满足，并给予你的生命更大的意义和目的感？研究表明，与追求外在的价值观例如财务成功、外表的吸引力和社会认可等相比，追求社会责任、个人成长和有意义的人际关系等内在价值观，可以带来更大的幸福感和整体生活满意度。

3 心理学实验表明，即便你已经感到精疲力竭，有意义的价值观也可以为你提供前进的必要动力。因此，树立并维护有意义的价值观而且采取正向的行动很重要。

4 人生中另一件重要的事情是理清你的人生目标，或者说是人生使命。当你年轻的时候，你可能认为自己的人生目标是创造出伟大的艺术作品，所以你决定成为一名音乐家。后来，你意识到你的人生目标是帮助他人，所以你接受培训成为一名医生或急救医疗技术员。那么，如何更准确地确定你的人生目标呢？方法之一是思考对你最有意义的经历。这些经历为你的人生目标提供线索。你对自己人生目标的了解可以指引你的职业选择和其他重要决策。

5 除了价值观和目标外，你还需要了解自己的气质。你的气质涵盖你的诸多特质。这些特质有一个共同点，它们是你天生的偏好。你可能是一个内向的人，这意味着你通过独处来恢复能量。或者你可能是一个外向的人，通过和他人交流互动重获能量。也许你擅长基于分析做决策。或者
（页底截断，接下页）
（接上页）你更喜欢跟随感觉。也许你喜欢细节。或者你更喜欢宏大的想法。这些都是你天生气质的一部分。了解自己的偏好可以帮助你做出最适合自己的决定。

6 在了解气质之后，你可以考虑自己的优点。你的优点不仅限于你的天赋、技能和能力。你最伟大的优点还包括你的性格优点，比如忠诚、公正、高情商和对他人的尊重。了解自己的优点是提高自信心的一种方式。

7 其他人可以帮助你辨别自己的优点。试着听听别人的赞美，并注意那些你拥有的技能，它们可能就是你优点的线索。例如，如果有人说你的声音很舒缓，也许你可以成为有声读物的配音演员或者电台主持人。同样，意识到自己的弱点可以让你坦承正视自己不擅长的领域。你可以选择克服这些弱点，或者减少其对个人生活或工作的负面影响。

8 你还需要了解自己的生物节奏。一个简单的问题是，你是"早起的鸟儿"（早起活力满满的人）还是"夜猫子"（晚上精力充沛的人）？有些人在早上工作效率最好，而有些人在晚上更有精力。在错误的时间（对你来说）做所有的工作会让你感到疲倦、情绪低落，并且没有成就感。

9 找一个与自己生物节奏相似的室友会给你的生活带来巨大的快乐。你们可以轻松地协调好睡眠时间并一起起床。如果你们两个都是早起的鸟儿，你们可以计划一起去散步，这可以帮助你们维护良好的关系并保持健康。虽然与重要的价值观相比，这可能微不足道，但当你与自己的生物节奏同步时，你的日常生活会更加愉快。当你不去浪费精力假装成为别人时，你更容易欣赏生活的方方面面。

10 最后，考虑你的兴趣，包括你的各种爱好，尤其是那些让你在长时间内充满热情的事物。如果你非常幸运，你或许能把最浓厚的兴趣转化为职业。为了确定自己的兴趣领域，你可以考虑一些关键问题：我专注于什么？我痴迷哪些话题？我被什么问题困扰？当你对某些事有强烈的好奇心时，它可能会给你提供线索，让你发现最深层的热情。

11 你对你的生活和性格的这些领域了解越多，你就会拥有更多的能量和自信。你会感到更有动力去欣赏生活，获得成长，提升自己，并更好地与他人互动沟通。

# Unit 2 Beyond the horizon

## Unit overview

This unit focuses on the different ways and deeper meanings of travel. The text in *Active reading* is an account of a British writer's five-month stay in Shanghai lanes. The text in *Further exploration* introduces adventures into different places across the world. Then the audio explains how travel can help us improve our inner selves. In *Project*, Ss are expected to develop a descriptive speech about their most unforgettable trip. The essay in *Extended reading* tells a humorous story of a man who prefers to stay at home and read travel books rather than go out to see beautiful places. By learning this unit, Ss will gain a deeper understanding of the true meaning of travel and become a conscious traveler.

## Scenario

学生就读的大学为庆祝世界旅游日将举办主题为"一次难忘的旅行"的英语演讲比赛，鼓励参与者分享旅行故事，相互交流，拓宽视野。学生将参与此次比赛。

- **Active reading**（Life in the lanes）：阅读一位英国作家在上海弄堂的旅居生活，了解在旅行中融入当地文化与生活的乐趣，提升在旅行中开拓视野、提升自我、充盈人生的意识。
- **Further exploration – Text**（Adventures of a lifetime）：阅读有关让人终生难忘的、充满挑战的旅行方式的文章，加深对不同旅游方式的理解，培养敢于探索未知的精神，提升应对困难的自信。
- **Further exploration – Video**（The point of travel）：观看视频，反思旅行的深层意义，理解旅行是疗愈身心、让内心获得平静的重要方式。

**Project**：回顾个人旅行经历，围绕一次最难忘的旅行发表演讲，描述此次旅行的所见所闻，讲述旅行背后的意义。

**Extended reading**（The armchair traveller）：阅读关于 George 决定外出旅行，但是考虑到种种不便在最后关头选择放弃的幽默故事，思考旅行的利与弊。

**Self-reflection**：回顾整个单元的学习，反思在内容、语言、结构、交际和行为上的收获。

讲述一次难忘的旅行经历，阐释旅行对于感受当地风土人情，拓展文化视野，探索未知世界，促进个人成长的意义，形成正确的旅行观念。

## 本单元课程思政元素备选清单

| 素材 | 课程思政参考点 | 二十大精神 |
|---|---|---|
| **Active reading**<br>(Life in the lanes) | • 通过旅行了解当地风俗和历史传统文化，充实自我；<br>• 与人交流，开阔视野，增长见识；<br>• 尊重世界文明多样性，践行文明交流和文明互鉴。 | 通过本单元的学习，学生能充分理解党的二十大所提出的"坚持以文塑旅、以旅彰文，推进文化和旅游深度融合发展"的精神，培养通过旅行提升自身人文素养的意识，践行智慧旅游、绿色旅游、文明旅游。此外，学生也将更深入地了解中国的文化底蕴，提升文化自信，坚守中华文化立场，学会在旅游活动中发现美、欣赏美、提炼展示中华文明的精神标识和文化精髓，培养爱祖国、爱家乡的家国情怀；而且乐于传播美，对外宣传中国的悠久历史、灿烂文化、壮美山川、多样风情，讲好中国故事、传播好中国声音，展现可信、可爱、可敬的中国形象。 |
| **Further exploration – Text**<br>(Adventures of a lifetime) | 敢于迎接挑战，探索未知； | （同上，与上格合并） |
| **Further exploration – Video**<br>(The point of travel) | 理解旅行的深层意义，从旅行中获得智慧和力量。 | |
| **Extended reading**<br>(The armchair traveller) | 客观看待旅行的利与弊，思考读书和旅行的关系，倡导"读万卷书，行万里路"，培养积极的旅行观念。 | |

## Scenario

The scenario in this unit can be analyzed from the following aspects:

**Setting:** A university English public-speaking contest

**Topic:** My unforgettable trip

**Identity:** Speaker – Student contestants
                Recipient – teachers and students in the university

**Purpose:** To describe the experiences of an unforgettable trip and share the insights gained from it

**Teaching suggestions**

T can help Ss generate ideas about how to describe a memorable trip by asking them to do a quick impromptu speaking exercise. T can show such topics on the slide: a scene at sunset, hiking in the mountains, walking in an ancient town, sipping in a special teahouse, boating in the lake, watching the birds, cooking with the locals ... Then T can ask Ss to work in groups of four or five and take turns making a 1-minute speech on their chosen topics. Then T can choose one S from each group to talk to the whole class.

After the tryout, T can ask Ss to reflect on their difficulties in four aspects, i.e., content (how to introduce a trip and share their gains from it), language (what difficulties they have in expressions and sentence structures), structure (how to organize a speech clearly and logically), and communication (how to engage the audience), and write them down in the table in the Student's Book.

## Active reading

In *Scenario*, Ss are expected to describe an unforgettable trip. The text is about how a British writer made the choice to live in a Shanghai-lane house and experienced the pleasures of mixing with local people and getting to understand Chinese culture. It illustrates how one can get the most out of a traveling experience. It can help Ss better understand the theme of the unit, i.e., the different ways and meaning of travel. Ss can learn the writing technique of making vivid descriptions.

### Before you read

**Reference answers**

1   I would like to go to the Lake District in the U.K. and stay in a cottage for five months.

2   I would choose this place for two reasons. First, the Lake District is famous for being "the most beautiful corner of England." It's highlighted by beautiful mountains, valleys, dark woods, and great stretches of water. Second, it is also a place infused with history and culture. It was once the home of Beatrix Potter, the writer of Peter Rabbit. Near the village of Sawrey at the north end of Lake Windermere, there is the cottage of Beatrix Potter. I love her stories.

3   The Lake District is one of the most stunning destinations in the U.K. There is a wide range of things for me to do, and it will be a great chance to refresh myself. First, the Lake District is an ideal place for fun outdoor activities, and I will enjoy boating, kayaking, paddle boarding, hiking, and biking. Second, the Lake District is home to several cultural attractions, including historic houses, castles, and museums, and I will spend time learning about the rich history and culture of the region. I'll learn more about Beatrix Potter, one of the Lake District's most famous residents. I'll visit the Beatrix Potter Gallery in Hawkshead, and look at the original sketches and watercolors she made for her books. I will also visit nearby Hill Top, the house where she once lived. Third, the beauty and silence of the Lake District will allow me to listen to my own thoughts, and therefore I plan to finish a creative art project I've been
（页底截断，接下页）

working on during my stay there.

### Life in the lanes

**Text interpretation**

本文作者是一名旅居上海的英国作家，以上海弄堂为背景，记录了自己在弄堂居住五个月的所见所闻，所思所想。第一部分（第一至第三段）为背景介绍，讲述了作者放弃现代化公寓，租住在弄堂的原因。第二部分（第四至第七段）详细描述了作者对弄堂居民饮食、起居、娱乐等各方面生活的观察。第三部分（第八段）总结了作者旅居生活的收获，表达了作者对安恬简约的生活、亲密融洽的邻里关系的热爱。

文章作者通过自己的旅居体验，展示了自己如何融入当地居民生活，深入了解中国文化的经历。基于课文，教师可有机融入思政目标，引导学生回忆并讲述个人的旅行经历，同时探讨旅行的深层意义，思考如何让旅行助力个人成长，如何通过旅行感知当地文化，如何通过旅行拓宽视野，如何将旅游与文化深度融合发展，如何彰显中国的文化底蕴等话题，帮助学生树立正确的旅行观念。

这也是一篇夹叙夹议的游记类文章，采用了总分总的结构。从写作手法上看，本文最突出的特征是讲述细致生动，具有强烈的画面感。作者擅长使用例证，也善于使用生动具体的语言。教师可结合写作技巧部分的讲解和练习引导学生分析文中例证与具体细节描写，并在产出任务中有效应用。

**Photo interpretation**

教材 34 页关于本文的配图有两张，上方为弄堂的实景，下方为石库门，富含中国文化特色。教师可以在读前活动中充分利用配图，让学生围绕图片提问或者教师设计问题，引导学生关注旅游的人文价值，隐性融入育人元素。比如，教师可以就弄堂照片设计以下问题：

1) Use three adjectives to describe the place.
2) How do you like living in this neighborhood?

教师可以就石库门照片设计以下问题：

1) What are the architectural features of this gate?
2) What do you know about its history?

**Culture notes**

*Shikumen*
*Shikumen*（石库门）is a unique style of housing that emerged in the late 19th century and early 20th century in Shanghai. It combines Western architectural structure and traditional Chinese decorations. The doorframe of shikumen is made of stone, and therefore many people also call *shikumen* stone gate houses. These houses are attached to each other and are located in alleys, also called *longtang*（弄堂）. The alleys are often long, narrow, and winding.

**Language points**

1   **Finding somewhere to live as a newcomer to Shanghai can be a daunting task, as the city has many options to offer.**（Para. 1）

**Meaning:** As a newcomer to Shanghai, it is very difficult to find a place to live, because there are many choices.

**daunting:** *a.* sth. that is daunting makes you worried because you think it will be very difficult or dangerous to do 吓人的；令人气馁的

*Planning a solo trip to a foreign country can be daunting, but it can also be an incredibly rewarding experience that pushes you out of your comfort zone.*

**Notes:** The verbs that often go before "options" are "choose," "select," "take," "offer," etc.

2   **You can choose to live in a tall building with newly renovated modern flats, or you can opt for a place that speaks from Shanghai's past.**（Para. 1）

**Meaning:** Shanghai has gone through drastic changes in the past decades. You have the choice to live in either modern buildings that represent modern Shanghai or places like lane houses that represent Shanghai's past.

**renovate:** *vt.* repair a building or old furniture so that it is in good condition again 修复，翻新（建筑物或家具）

*They spent a fortune renovating the upstairs bedrooms.*

**Notes:** In the phrase "a place that speaks from Shanghai's past," the word "speak" is being used metaphorically to imply that the place has the ability to convey or communicate the essence, history, and cultural heritage of Shanghai. Just as words can express ideas or stories, the choice of the word "speak" suggests that the place has a voice that reflects the historical significance and character of Shanghai.

3   **This traditional style of accommodation once was said to house about 60 percent of Shanghai's downtown population.**（Para. 2）

**house:** *vt.* give sb. a place to live 给……提供房子

*The company plans to house its employees in a newly built accommodation facility.*

**downtown:** *a.* (only before noun) to or in the main business area of a town or city 在城镇中心区的；在闹市区的

*The couple moved from a downtown apartment to a rural house.*

4   **I was greeted by subtropical weather, humid and hot, the likes of which I had never before experienced in the U.K.**（Para. 3）

**Meaning:** The first thing I noticed in Shanghai was its hot and humid subtropical weather. It is something that I had never experienced in the U.K.

**greet:** *vt.* if you are greeted by sth., it is the first thing you notice in a particular place 最先被……感觉到；映入……眼帘；呈现在……面前

*As I stepped off the plane, I was greeted by a cool ocean breeze that instantly refreshed me.*

**the likes of:** people or things of a particular type 那样的人或物；那种人或物

*The singer's powerful voice and incredible stage presence were the likes of which I had only heard in legendary performances.*

5   **Tucked away from the hustle and bustle of the busy streets, the lanes allowed me to find some calm just a few steps away from the chaos of the main thoroughfares.**（Para. 3）

**Meaning:** Located away from the noise and crowd of the busy streets, the lanes are quiet. By walking just a few steps away from the chaotic main roads, I was able to find some calm in these lanes.

**be tucked away:** if a place is tucked away, it is in a quiet area （某处所）位于安静之地

*The secret garden was tucked away behind a tall stone wall, creating a peaceful retreat.*

**hustle and bustle:** noise and activity 喧闹嘈杂

*As I stepped into the city center, the hustle and bustle of people rushing by filled the air.*

6   **Many of my neighbours seemed to spend most of their days moving around and doing various things in the lane, whether it be hanging laundry, chopping vegetables, or just having a chat – it was all done outside their front doors.**（Para. 4）

**Notes:** The structure "whether it be hanging laundry, chopping vegetables or just having a chat" is an adverbial clause containing the "be" subjunctive (be 型虚拟式), which is often used in formal, written English. It has become a formulaic expression to present two or more alternatives or possibilities. The "be" subjunctive used in adverbial clauses can be introduced by "if / though / whatever / whether," etc. For example:

*If the rumor is true, everything is possible.*

*Whether it be on the beach, in the park, or even in the office, people love to free their feet.*

7   **I was fascinated to think of the long and rich life my neighbour had experienced growing up in Shanghai, along with the vast changes to the city she would have witnessed.**（Para. 5）

**Meaning:** My neighbor has had a rich long life, and has witnessed the dramatic changes of Shanghai ever since she was born. I find her experience so interesting and attractive.

**along with:** used for mentioning additional people or things that are also included or involved in sth. 与……一起

*The restaurant offers a diverse menu with a wide selection of vegan options, along with delicious desserts.*

8   **Life in the lane provided such a rich insight into Chinese culture.**（Para. 6）

**Notes:** This sentence serves as an effective topic sentence of Paragraph 6. It sums up the specific details provided in this paragraph.

9   **... to wash and prepare their vegetables and meat for the evening meal while chatting away.**（Para. 6）

**chat away:** (also chat) talk in a friendly informal way, especially about things that are not important 闲谈；聊天

*Despite the long journey, the two friends chatted away in the car, keeping each other entertained.*

10  **Every day around 6 p.m. they would begin cooking, and delicious fragrant smells of garlic, ginger, and chili would travel across the lane.**（Para. 6）

**Notes:** The word "travel" is used in a personified way. It vividly shows that the strong pleasant smell of all kinds of spices moves through the air or floats over the lane when people cook at the same time.

11  **I observed that on warm, sunny days, the lane would quickly become full of colour, in the form of drying clothes and bed sheets.**（Para. 7）

**Meaning:** For years, it has been a way of life for many Shanghai residents living in lane houses to dry wet laundry in the sun, as the humidity prevents washing from drying for days inside. On warm, sunny days, people living in the lanes would hang out their clothes and bed sheets, and the lanes thus would become very colorful.

12  **Neighbours would unfold chairs and sit outside their doors, drinking green tea and people watching.**（Para. 7）

**Notes:** The part "drinking green tea and people watching" is a present participle clause acting as an adverbial phrase. The main clause is "Neighbours would unfold chairs and sit outside their doors." The use of the present participle clause adds vivid details about what people drink and do when they sit outside. Here is another example:

*The old lady was asleep, holding a book in her hand.*

13  **Each is a tiny neighbourhood that represents a very personal story of China.**（Para. 8）

**Meaning:** Each lane is a tiny community. Each lane tells personal stories of Chinese people and Chinese culture.

14  **Living in the lane for a few months taught me about the strong sense of community that is so intrinsic to Chinese society.**（Para. 8）

**Meaning:** Chinese culture values friendliness and harmony between people. This is something fundamental to Chinese society.

15  **On a simple level, I also learned that pleasure can be found in the small things in life: food, people and sunshine.**（Para. 8）

**Meaning:** At a basic level, I also discovered that joy can be gained from simple and everyday things in life like food, companionship, and the warmth of the sun.

**Getting the idea**

**Reference answers**

1
Part I - b
Part II - c
Part III - a

2

2   lane houses; Western and Chinese elements
3   long and rich life; vast changes
4   Chinese musical instrument; their vegetables and meat (for the evening meal); aid digestion; people watching
5   the richness of the local culture; sense of community; the small things

My favorite aspect of the holiday was hiking to the peak of the mountain in the early morning and watching the beautiful sunrise. Many of my friends were amazed and celebrated the moment in various ways, whether it be singing a cheerful song, dancing happily, or posing for photos with the sun.

**Your sentences:**
I observed that in the early mornings, the residents from the nearby neighborhood would gather in the park and do various exercises, some practicing taichi, some jogging, and some kicking the shuttlecock.

It's a journey into China's ancient history and cultural richness. You will be greeted by the wonderful ancient historical sites and the blend of diversified cultures. •   I traveled to Lijiang, a town in Yunnan Province, China.
•   I went there in the summer of 2018.
•   I stayed in a small inn near the town center.

The narrow lanes and old architecture are reminders of how people lived in ancient times. It is a very relaxing place to enjoy a slow pace of life.

They also have a number of traditional songs and dances, many of which are still performed today. Another thing that impressed me was their cuisine. The Naxi people have a cuisine that is heavily influenced by the local climate and geography. One of the most unique and interesting aspects of Naxi cuisine is the use of herbs and spices. ## Further exploration

The task in *Scenario* asks Ss to present a 3-minute prepared speech describing their most unforgettable trip. The text in this part introduces several adventurous activities in many different parts of the world and guides the students to think about different ways of travel. The video guides the students to consider the meaning of travel and how one can get the most out of a trip. The input material in this part can prepare Ss for telling their most unforgettable trip. T can help Ss rethink what different ways of traveling can benefit them and how they can grow into better versions of themselves through traveling.

### Adventures of a lifetime (Text)

**Culture notes**

1   **McMurdo Station**
It is a U.S. research and support facility that was established in the 1950s at the southernmost point of Antarctica where bare ground is accessible by ship. The station is the largest community in Antarctica, capable of supporting over 1,200 residents in summer and 300 in winter.

2   **Sydney Harbour Bridge**
The Sydney Harbour Bridge is one of Australia's most famous landmarks. Opened in 1932, it stands at a height of 134 meters and has a total length of 1,149 meters. It is the tallest steel arch bridge in the world. The Sydney Harbour Bridge climb is an iconic and thrilling experience that offers a unique perspective of Sydney and its stunning harbor. During the climb, participants are provided with special equipment, including a protective suit and a harness, and are accompanied by experienced guides.

**Language points**

1   **Are you looking for something completely out of the ordinary to do when you're on vacation? Here are some adventures that you can try.**（Para. 1）

**out of the ordinary:** unusual or different 不寻常的

*The art exhibition showed a collection of paintings that were out of the ordinary, with abstract and thought-provoking pieces that challenged traditional artistic conventions.*

2   **During your adventure, you'll see curious rock formations left behind by the last volcanic eruption.**（Para. 2）

**Meaning:** During your adventure, you will come across interesting rock formations that were formed as a result of the most recent volcanic eruption.

3   **Bring a sense of adventure for this unusual trip, and it helps if you are not afraid of the dark!**（Para. 2）

**Notes:** The expression "a sense of adventure" means a natural ability or quality that people have when doing something new, different, or exciting. For example:

*Kevin has no sense of adventure. There's no way he'll climb to the top of that waterfall with me.*

4   **You don't need to be a working scientist or to apply for a research grant to go to the icy continent of Antarctica.**（Para. 3）

**Meaning:** You don't have to be employed as a scientist or go through the process of applying for a research grant in order to visit the very cold continent of Antarctica.

**grant:** *n.* [C] an amount of money given to a person or organization, especially by the government, for a particular purpose （尤指政府发给的用于特定目的的）补助金，拨款

*The project was supported by a research grant from the National Institutes of Health.*

5   **This glass path, which is 430 meters long, 6 meters wide, and 300 meters high, is set against dramatic landscapes in Zhangjiajie Grand Canyon in China's Hunan Province.**

**set:** *vt.* (usu. passive) if sth. is set somewhere, it is in that place or position 位于；处于

*The magnificent Sydney Harbour Bridge is set against the stunning backdrop of the Sydney Opera House, creating an iconic and picturesque scene.*

6   **So next time you are in the mood for excitement, try it – if you dare!**

**dare:** *vi.* be brave enough to do sth. 敢于；胆敢

*I don't dare to touch the flower for its tenderness.*

**Notes:** The expression "if you dare" means if you have the courage to do so. For example:

*Jump off the cliff into the crystal-clear waters below, if you dare, and experience the exhilaration of a daring adventure.*

**Reference answers**

1
1)  mysterious
2)  explore the wonders
3)  icy
4)  landscapes and seascapes
5)  wildlife
6)  Mailing out postcards
7)  challenge
8)  360-degree view
9)  between two cliffs
10) dramatic

2
1   I think tourists enjoy adventurous trips for two reasons. Firstly, such trips allow them to escape from dull routines or stress of life and give them a break to recharge and refresh themselves. Secondly, these trips provide a sense of achievement. Adventures like exploring a dark cave or waking on a dazzling glass bridge are exciting and challenging. When a person completes such a daunting task, they will get a great sense of fulfillment.
2   If I could do any of the adventures above, I would like to explore a dark lava cave in Iceland. I favor
（页底截断，接下页）

this adventure for a few reasons. First, the darkness and mystery of the cave can create a sense of adventure and exploration. As I navigate through the cave, there is only the headlamp to guide me. This is especially exciting for me as I am always in a mood for excitement. Second, exploring the cave can provide a window into Iceland's geological history and the forces that shape its landscapes. I am very interested in geology, and I believe the trip will be a fascinating and educational experience.

**Additional activity**

The text discusses some unusual ways of traveling, aiming to encourage students to approach each experience with an open mind and urging them to step out of their comfort zone to explore the unknown. T can trigger Ss' interest in learning about different modes of travel. Considering the financial capabilities of Ss, T can also guide them to think about how they can travel comfortably and safely on a limited budget.

Ask Ss to discuss the following questions.

1   Travel can be experienced in various ways, such as engaging in a working holiday in a foreign country. Could you please share your personal experiences or stories of individuals who have traveled in unconventional ways?
2   When one travels on a small budget and tries to save money by hitchhiking, backpacking, or couch surfing, how can one ensure safety? Share your own tips for traveling safely.

**Reference answers**

1   Jake, a 19-year-old who took a college break to travel, had a life-changing experience through Workaway in Guatemala. Workaway is a cultural exchange program where participants work a few hours a day for food and accommodation. Jake researched and achieved his travel goals: volunteering, living with a local family to learn Spanish, and meeting travelers worldwide. In Guatemala, he lived with international volunteers and a local family, teaching English to fourth graders at a local school for 6 weeks. Workaway enabled him to connect with locals, and make an impact, and living with a Guatemalan family enriched his experience. Now he still maintains contact with his host family and fellow volunteers.

2   While traveling on a small budget through hitchhiking, backpacking, or couch surfing, it's crucial to prioritize safety. Here are some essential tips to ensure a safe journey. Firstly, trust your instincts. Always listen to your gut feeling and trust your intuition. If a situation or person feels unsafe, it's better to remove yourself from the situation. Secondly, do thorough research. Before embarking on your journey, research your destinations extensively. Learn about potential risks, safety concerns, and local customs. Stay updated with travel advisories and warnings from reliable sources. Thirdly, stay connected. Maintain regular communication with someone you trust back home. Share your travel plans, and check in with them periodically. This provides an extra layer of safety as someone will be aware of your whereabouts and can assist you in case of emergencies.

### The point of travel (Video)

**Scripts**

1   What's the point of travel? It's to help make us into better people. All of us are, in one way or another, on what could be termed an inner journey of self-development. The point of travel is to go to places that can help us in our inner evolution. The outer journey should assist us with the inner one.
