## （续）

2 Every location in the world contains qualities that can support some kind of beneficial change inside a person. Take these two-hundred-million-year-old stones in the deserts of America's state of Utah. It's a place. But looked at psychologically, it's also an inner destination, a place of calm and a comforting reminder of the vast history of which we are a part.

3 In an ideal world, travel agencies would be staffed by travel agents who are also trained psychotherapists. They'd take care not just of the flights and hotels, they'd start by finding out what was wrong with us and how we might want to change. The anxious might be sent to see the majestic and immemorial waves crashing into the cliffs on the west coast of Ireland. Those who are too concerned with being admired and famous might be sent to contemplate the ruins of Detroit. Someone out of touch with their body might be recommended a trip to Bahia, Brazil.

4 Nowadays, too often we head off on our travels without fully knowing what we need or precisely understanding how our chosen destination is meant to help us. We should become more conscious travelers, searching for places that possess qualities we need, like calm and comfort. We should try to evolve our characters according to the suggestions offered up by the places we've been to. We need to relearn how to be ambitious about travel, seeing it as a way of helping us grow into better versions of ourselves.

### Culture notes

**1 Detroit**
The city of Detroit, Michigan, U.S. was once a booming industrial city, the home of the American motor industry and the birthplace of Motown. Since the 1960s, however, the city has faced a prolonged period of decline which culminated in Detroit becoming the largest U.S. city to ever file for bankruptcy in 2013. Abandoned buildings are now an ever-present feature of the cityscape, with some even going as far as labeling it the Abandoned City. The ruins of the city serve as a reminder of Detroit's troubled past and have become a subject of fascination for urban explorers and photographers.

**2 Bahia**
One of the largest states in Brazil, Bahia is home to a wide variety of sceneries and cultures. Its southern coast is particularly rich in experiences. Brazilians nicknamed Bahia "the land of happiness." Southern Bahia is the place to be if people are looking for a tranquil place to unplug.

### Language points

**1 All of us are, in one way or another, on what could be termed an inner journey of self-development.** (Para. 1)
**Meaning**: To some extent, all of us are on what could be called an inside journey of self-growth.

**2 The point of travel is to go to places that can help us in our inner evolution.** (Para. 1)
**evolution**: n. [U] the gradual change and development of an idea, situation, or object 演变，逐步发展
*The evolution of technology has gone beyond our wildest expectations.*

**3 Every location in the world contains qualities that can support some kind of beneficial change inside a person.** (Para. 2)
**Meaning**: Every place in the world can offer something that supports a person's healthy inner change.

**4 In an ideal world, travel agencies would be staffed by travel agents who are also trained psychotherapists.**
**Meaning**: Ideally, travel agencies would employ travel agents who are also trained in the field of psychotherapy.
**staff**: vt. be or provide the workers for an organization 在……工作；为……配备职员
*The community center was staffed mainly by dedicated volunteers.*

**5 The anxious might be sent to see the majestic and immemorial waves crashing into the cliffs on the west coast of Ireland. Those who are too concerned with being admired and famous might be sent to contemplate the ruins of Detroit. Someone out of touch with their body might be recommended a trip to Bahia, Brazil.**
**Notes**: These three sentences follow a similar pattern. They begin with a subject ("The anxious," "Those who," "Someone out of touch"), followed by a verb phrase ("might be sent," "might be recommended"). The sentences then present the destination or scenario ("to see the majestic and immemorial waves ...," "to contemplate the ruins of Detroit," "a trip to Bahia, Brazil"). The use of vivid imagery and contrasting ideas adds depth and evokes a strong emotional response.

**6 Nowadays, too often we head off on our travels without fully knowing what we need or precisely understanding how our chosen destination is meant to help us.**
**Notes**: "Nowadays" and "too often" are used at the beginning of the sentence for emphasis. By doing so, the author stresses that most of the time modern people are not aware of what they need and why they travel.

**7 We should become more conscious travelers, searching for places that possess qualities we need, like calm and comfort.**
**conscious**: a. being aware, thoughtful, and deliberate 有意的；刻意的
*He made a conscious decision to disconnect from social media and focus on building meaningful relationships in real life.*

**8 We need to relearn how to be ambitious about travel, seeing it as a way of helping us to grow into better versions of ourselves.**
**Meaning**: We need to get rid of our old ideas and learn how to be ambitious about travel. We need to see travel as a way of improving ourselves.

**Reference answers**
1
C

2
1) comforting reminder
2) anxious
3) being admired and famous
4) out of touch
5) what we need
6) meant to help us
7) possess qualities we need
8) better versions

### Sharing your ideas

**Reference answers**
- When I decided on the destination, I put cultural richness as the No.1 factor. Lijiang is just such a place. I wanted to learn more about the history of the ancient town, and I was also eager to know more about the customs and traditions of the Naxi ethnic group.
- Lijiang was a place full of cultural flavor. It was a trip that left me with memories that will last a lifetime. The journey brought about two significant changes in me. Firstly, it sparked a strong interest in learning about the history of different ethnic groups. Secondly, it instilled greater confidence in my social interactions.

**Suggestions for evaluation**
For this activity, Ss' sharing can be evaluated according to the following criteria:
- whether Ss can clearly state their points
- whether Ss can back up their points with evidence (e.g., examples, details)

## Project

**Teaching suggestions**
Some Ss may feel that this is a difficult activity because they haven't traveled much for pleasure or for work. If so, put Ss with little travel experience with other pairs to form groups so that they can share experiences. T can ask Ss to talk about how they travel from their hometowns to the university or guide them to recall a memorable journey.

**Sample**
(*Expressions and sentences in red are from the input materials.)

Last summer, I embarked on an extraordinary journey to a place filled with enchantment and cultural richness. As I ventured through winding paths and secret alleys, I found myself in the world-renowned 800-year-old Old Town of Lijiang. Its ancient architecture and tranquil waterways captivated my senses, transporting me to a bygone era.

Every step I took in Lijiang revealed the perfect blend of tradition and heritage. The architectural style, unique to this region, acted as an insight into the rich history that shaped this town. Strolling along the bridges and lanes, I was greeted by breathtaking views, the likes of which I had never before experienced. Each corner turned provided me with a fascinating insight into the local life, inviting me to observe and absorb the essence of this cultural gem.

One aspect that held me spellbound was the presence of the Naxi ethnic group, the indigenous people of this land. I found myself particularly drawn to their customs and traditions, which showcased a rich and unique culture. Engaging in conversations with the locals, including restaurant and shop owners, I eagerly delved into their world, eager to learn more about their way of life. Venturing beyond the Old Town, I embarked on a tour to Yuhu village, where I had the privilege of experiencing the daily rituals and routines of the Naxi community.

The Naxi people's deep-rooted musical heritage fascinated me. Their traditional instruments, such as the lute, flute, and dong-dong drum, echoed through the narrow lanes, infusing the air with captivating melodies. I had the pleasure of witnessing their vibrant performances, an enchanting display of their musical prowess. Additionally, the Naxi cuisine left an indelible impression on my taste buds. The richness of their culinary creations, shaped by the local climate and geography, introduced me to a tantalizing world of flavors. The skilled use of herbs and spices, like basil, added a unique and aromatic touch to their dishes, awakening my senses with each bite.

Every time I stepped into a new realm of Lijiang, I was amazed by the profound depth of its culture. It was not merely the stunning landscapes that captured my heart but also the warm interactions and genuine hospitality of the local people. This journey gifted me with more than just memories; it instilled in me a newfound appreciation for heritage, diversity, and the beauty of human connection. Lijiang, with its cultural tapestry and captivating stories, will forever hold a special place in my heart, reminding me of the transformative power of travel and the treasures waiting to be discovered in every corner of our remarkable world.

**Suggestions for evaluation**

| Components | Criteria / Traits |
| --- | --- |
| Content | • Relevance: Addressing the assigned task by describing a most memorable trip and explaining the personal gains out of this trip<br>• Appeal: Including examples and details, showing a sense of audience, being descriptive and thought-provoking |
| Language | • Variety: Using an adequate range of vocabulary and sentence structures<br>• Style: Using clear and vivid language<br>• Acquisition: Using as many as possible of the new words, expressions, and grammatical structures learned in the unit |
| Organization | • Structure: Including well-organized introduction, body, and conclusion<br>• Technique: Giving examples to help the audience visualize the scene |
| Communication | • Physical delivery: Having good eye contact and posture, using adequate gestures and facial expressions, and being confident<br>• Vocal delivery: Having good pronunciation and natural intonation, using proper volume, pauses and speaking rate, creating vocal variety, and conveying enthusiasm |

## Extended reading

> In the previous parts, Ss have learned to describe unforgettable trips and state their gains of these trips. The focus is on how to plan a trip and how to make the most out of travel. This part provides a story to help Ss make further exploration of the theme of this unit. This is a humorous story about a man called George, who imagines the possible inconveniences of travel and decides to stay at home. The style combines description with voicing the main character's thoughts and speculations. The humor is mainly in the idea that George's imagination is double-edged: It tells him how wonderful his planned journey might be, but it also tells him that the actual visit may not match the wonderful things he has imagined about the south of France. Ss can better understand the advantages and disadvantages of traveling through reading the passage. T should make it clear to Ss that it's important not to overemphasize the negative effects of exploring the outside world.

### The armchair traveller

### Culture notes

**1 Flat**
A flat (AmE apartment) is a set of rooms, usually on one floor of a building. It can be a small one with two rooms or a luxury one. It may be rented or owned by those living there.

**2 Marcel Pagnol (1895-1974)**
Marcel Pagnol was a popular French writer and film director. He directed films for cinema and TV as well. His work centers on human dramas and comedies in Provence. He also wrote lyrical and humorous memoirs of his family and life in the south of France. Two of his most successful novels, which were also made into films, are *Jean de Florette* and *Manon des Sources*. He was the first film director to be elected to the Académie Française.

**3 Pablo Picasso (1881-1973)**
Picasso was a Spanish painter and sculptor, considered by many as the greatest artist and innovator of styles and techniques in 20th-century art. During his nearly 80 years of art making, Picasso produced over 20,000 paintings, prints, drawings, sculpture, ceramics, theatre sets and costumes.

**4 Paul Cézanne (1839-1906)**
Cézanne was a French painter, known as one of the greatest Post-Impressionists. His works and ideas informed many 20th-century artists. He lived for years in Aix-en-Provence in the south of France.

**5 Vincent van Gogh (1853-1890)**
Van Gogh was a Dutch painter. Influenced by the Impressionists, he had his own unique style. Some of his best-known works were painted in Arles in the south of France during one of his most creative periods.

**6 Graham Greene (1904-1991)**
Graham Greene was a prolific English writer of novels, short stories, plays and film scripts. His works often deal with moral issues, with adventure and suspense. He lived for long periods in Nice, in the south of France.

**7 Ernest Hemingway (1899-1961)**
Ernest Hemingway was a U.S. novelist and short story writer, who was awarded the Nobel Prize for Literature in 1954. His work is noted for the masculinity of his writing and a succinct, clear, apparently simple style. He worked for some years as a journalist in Paris.

**8 Émile Zola (1840-1902)**
Émile Zola was one of the most prominent French novelists of the late 19th century and also a journalist. He carried out detailed research and observation for many novels, aiming to show the lives of ordinary people working in factories and mines.

### Language points

**1 He lived alone by choice, because he easily felt overwhelmed by other people.** (Para. 1)
**Meaning**: He chose to live alone to avoid other people.

**by choice**: if you do sth. by choice, you do it because you want to do it and not because you are forced to do it 自己选择地；自愿地
*He said that he lived in the countryside by choice.*

**2 So he stayed in his flat most of the time, especially in wintertime, when his drab, gray neighbourhood was made drabber and greyer by the seemingly continuous rain.** (Para. 1)
**Meaning**: There is a general conception that England is a rainy country. And there is much more rain in wintertime. When it rained nearly every day, George felt the neighborhood was more boring, and therefore he stayed indoors most of the time.

**3 His lifestyle was modest and his financial circumstances allowed him to spend his days... reading great works of literature.** (Para. 2)
**Meaning**: George didn't spend very much, and had enough money to ... read literature all day (he did not need to work).

**4 From the warmth of his armchair, he could travel in his mind to the farthest reaches of the world ...** (Para. 2)
**Meaning**: George didn't need to go traveling to see the outside world. He could stay in his armchair and travel to even the farthest places in the world by reading works of travel writing and using his imagination.

**5 Set against the dramatic background of the Alps and the deep blue of the Mediterranean Sea, Provence has a tradition of farming and local crafts.** (Para. 3)
**Notes**: "Set against the dramatic background of the Alps and the deep blue of the Mediterranean Sea" is a past participle clause serving as adverbial of the whole sentence. Provence is its logical subject.

**6 It was home to painters such as ...** (Para. 4)
**be home to sb. / sth.**: be the place where a person, animal, or plant lives 是……的居住地；是……的产地，是……的生长（栖息）地
*The wetland is home to a variety of wildlife, especially birds.*

**7 Unable to contain his curiosity, he got dressed and went to a travel agency.** (Para. 5)
**Meaning**: George couldn't control his curiosity, so he got dressed and went to the travel agency to find travel information.

**Notes**: Verbs that often go before "curiosity" are "have / arouse / awaken / spark / satisfy, etc."

**8 He was astonished that a journey so rich in ideas, colours, and impressions could be made with such little effort — a train ride of a mere six hours.** (Para. 6)
**Meaning**: He was astonished that such a great and meaningful journey could be done so easily: He could make it with only a six-hour train ride.

**mere**: a. used to emphasize how small or unimportant sth. is 仅仅；只不过
*There is more to good health than the mere absence of disease.*

**9 It was early, and George had time to spare before the train left.** (Para. 7)
**time / money / room, etc. to spare**: if you have time, money etc. to spare, there is some left in addition to what you have used or need 余暇（余钱、余地等）
*We had time to spare and visited some of the shops at the airport.*

**10 ... and fruit and vegetables coloured with the sun.** (Para. 7)
**Meaning**: ... and brightly colored fruits and vegetables that have been ripened under the sun.

**11 ... and ordered a meal the likes of which he would have found in the Old Port of Marseille.** (Para. 7)
**Meaning**: He ordered a meal. It was the kind of meal that he would have found in the Old Port of Marseille.

**12 And when he arrived, he would have to find a hotel, sleep in an unfamiliar bed, and look for the sights, sounds, and smells which his dreams had already been so effective in describing to him.** (Para. 8)
**Meaning**: Before traveling to the south of France, George had read about it, vividly imagining what it would be like. He thought he had already known what it was like from his reading, so he began questioning whether it was worth the trouble of the journey. This sentence marks a turn in George's decision to make a journey to Provence.

**13 In his imagination, he was already there.** (Para. 8)
**Meaning**: He thought he was already there. In his imagination, he already knew what it was like. The questions at the end of the paragraph suggest that George was hesitating and trying to find reasons for persuading himself to cancel the journey.

**Reference answers**
1 D
2 D
3 A
4 B
5 C

## Self-reflection

> In this part, T can ask Ss to refer back to Try it out in the Student's Book and reflect on what they have learned to complete the task. Ss can categorize their gains according to the same four aspects, i.e., content, language, structure, and communication. They can also reflect on how learning the unit may influence their action in daily life. Then they can write down their specific gains in the self-reflection table in the Student's Book.

## Translation of the texts

### Active reading

**弄堂生活**

1 对初到上海的人而言，找个地方住可能是一项艰巨的任务，因为选择太多。在这里，游客们会感受到现代与传统的完美融合。你可以选择住进一栋装修一新的现代化高层公寓楼里，也可以选择住在一个有历史感的地方。对我这样一位希望记录普通中国人生活的作家来说，上海的独特之处是那些老弄堂的存在。

2 这些弄堂房屋被称为石库门。它们代表了上海传统的建筑风格，融合了西方和中国元素。石库门房屋被布置成密集的小巷网络，称为弄堂，让人很容易在其中迷路。据说这样的传统住宿方式容纳了大约60%的上海市中心人口。现在，石库门是透视中国传统文化的地方。

3 当我去年8月第一次来到上海，计划旅居五个月时，弄堂对我有特殊的吸引力。那时迎接我的是湿热的亚热带气候，这是我在英国从未经历过的。远离繁忙街道的喧嚣，这些小弄堂使我在离主干道的混乱仅几步之遥的地方找到了一些宁静。这些弄堂也让我有机会观察当地居民的日常生活，并让我深刻认识到人与交往的重要性。

4 我最喜欢弄堂生活的部分是邻里关系。我的许多邻居似乎每天大部分时间都在弄堂里走来走去，做各种各样的事情，晒衣服、切菜或者只是闲聊——这一切都发生在他们的前门外。

5 我的邻居大多是70岁以上的老人，他们似乎都在弄堂里享受着平静的退休生活。一位邻居都快百岁高龄了。尽管年事已高，她仍然每天在弄堂里散步，给猫喂食，面带让人愉悦的笑容。她告诉我她就出生在这条弄堂里，而且她对我很好奇，问我很多问题：来自哪里？做什么工作？今天吃饭了吗？习惯吃中国菜吗？虽然我们语言不通，但年轻的曾孙女会把我的回答翻译成上海话。我很喜欢这种独特的交流方式。我的这位邻居在上海土生土长，经历了丰富而漫长的人生，目睹了这座城市的巨大变化，这让我很感兴趣。她也对我的文化很感兴趣，想了解更多。我们谈论了她在这条弄堂里遇到的各种各样的人。

6 弄堂生活让我充分了解了中国文化。每天早上，一位年长的男性邻居会坐在大门外的小竹椅上弹奏琵琶。琵琶是一种传统的中国弦乐器，有梨形的木制琴身。听到他弹出活泼而富有表现力的曲调，我赞叹不已。其他邻居下午会聚在一起，一边聊天，一边清理、准备晚餐所需的蔬菜和肉类。每天下午6点左右，邻居们都会开始做饭，整条弄堂里都飘荡着大蒜、生姜和辣椒的香味。晚餐结束后，他们会在门口给猫留下一些米饭和碎肉，然后开始散步消食。

7 弄堂里晾衣绳上琳琅满目的衣物也很吸引我。我注意到，在温暖和煦、阳光充足的日子里，弄堂会晾满衣服和床单，并很快变得色彩斑斓。弄堂里的每扇门外都摆放了生机勃勃的绿植。邻居们会打开折叠椅，坐在门外，喝着绿茶，看看来来往往的人。

8 上海的每条弄堂都让人感觉亲切。每条弄堂都是一个迷你社区，讲述着中国老百姓自己的故事。

（接上页）

9 作为一名外国游客，住在弄堂里的独特体验让我更贴近当地丰富的文化。每次走进弄堂，我都感觉这座庞大的城市突然变成了一个小村庄，大家彼此熟稔。在弄堂里住了几个月，我体验了中国社会特有的浓浓人情味。简而言之，我也认识到生活的快乐就蕴含在美食、友邻和阳光这些细微的小事中。

## Further exploration

**终生难忘的冒险旅行**

1 当你度假时，你是否想有不同寻常的体验？那么你可以尝试以下这些刺激冒险的活动。

**探索熔岩洞穴**

2 你想拥有独特的体验吗？如果是这样，计划去冰岛吧，那是个冰与火交融的国家。一旦你到了冰岛，一定得去参观瑞法赫火山岩洞，它可是一个完美的冰岛熔岩洞穴。探索多彩而神秘的地下世界是一次真正非凡的体验。在探险中，你会看到上次火山喷发留下的奇特岩层。穿戴好所有必要的安全装备后，你可以爬到地下探索洞穴的奇迹。带着冒险的心态开始这次不同寻常的旅行吧。如果你不怕黑的话，会很有帮助哦！

**坐邮轮去南极洲**

3 你不需要成为一名在职科学家，也不需要申请研究基金，就可以前往冰冷的南极洲。虽然大多数研究者在南极洲科研中心麦克默多站度过整整三个月，但普通人可以坐邮轮在那里进行短暂的旅行。南极洲是一个非常美丽的地方，有着非同寻常、在任何别的地方都看不到的地貌和海景。在这片冰天雪地里，生活着大量独特的野生动物。旅行期间，将有经验丰富的冰雪和野生动物专家指导你。你可以选择参观洛克罗伊港，这是一个英国的小型研究基地。这里还有一个邮箱，你可以邮寄明信片。

**攀爬悉尼海港大桥**

4 在澳大利亚旅行时，你可以参加很多惊险的项目，包括攀134米高的桥。没错！年轻和年老的游客都曾接受过攀爬悉尼海港大桥的传奇挑战，这是一项在澳大利亚大都市游览时很受欢迎的活动。你需要穿上安全装备，然后爬上这座著名的大桥，这座位于悉尼的美丽海港将会360度毫无遮挡地呈现在你眼前。

**走过令人目眩的玻璃栈道**

5 如果你足够勇敢，你可以在世界上最长、最高的玻璃桥上行走。这座桥悬挂在两个悬崖之间。这条玻璃栈道长430米，宽6米，高300米，位于景色壮观的中国湖南省张家界大峡谷。桥底铺有透明玻璃，因此你可以从空中漫步并欣赏到令人惊叹的景色。但请注意：如果你往下看，你可能会感到头晕。所以下次如果你想找点刺激，而且胆子够大的话，就来试试这座桥吧！

### Extended reading

**坐在扶手椅上的旅行者**

1 乔治独自一人住在伦敦南部的一套公寓里。他是自己选择独居的，因为其他人总让他感觉紧张无措。确实，很多事情都让他感到紧张无措。他感觉最好待在家里，因为家里很少有事情让他感到紧张无措。所以他绝大多数时间都待在自己的公寓，尤其是在被看似永远下不完的阴雨搞得更加索然无味、灰蒙蒙的冬季里。

2 他的生活很简朴，经济状况也还可以。他可以穿着睡袍，终日坐在一张古老又舒适的扶手椅里，阅读文学名著。尽管他生性多疑、悲观，他特别喜欢阅读经典游记。这样，即便是舒舒服服地坐在温暖的扶手椅上，他也可以神游到天涯海角：神秘的亚马孙丛林，壮美的喜马拉雅顶峰，遥远的南太平洋海岛，冰天雪地的北极圈以及无与伦比的撒哈拉沙漠。他还可以在神游中体验旅行的诸多乐趣；在月光笼罩下的热带湖泊中畅游，沿着某个国家公园的徒步路线行走，在春季欣赏满树的樱桃繁花，在棕榈树下小憩。

3 一天早上，他翻看着马塞尔·帕尼奥尔写的一本书。帕尼奥尔记述的有关法国南部地区普罗旺斯的见闻曾经激发了几代人去旅行的热情。普罗旺斯背靠壮观的阿尔卑斯山脉，面朝地中海蔚蓝清澈的海水，有着农耕和民间手工技艺传统。在帕尼奥尔看来，普罗旺斯地区既富有乡村特色又很保守，天生就不太信任陌生人。

4 不过，乔治从别的书中也得知，普罗旺斯是一个对富人和艺术家都很有吸引力的地方。很多艺术家都在此居住过，如画家毕加索、塞尚和凡·高，作家格雷厄姆·格林、欧内斯特·海明威、埃米尔·左拉。圣特罗佩的海滩、戛纳的电影节、格拉斯的餐馆以及尼斯的大饭店都给普罗旺斯增添了令人难以想象的魅力。

5 然而，真的有那么想象吗？乔治越往下读，就越急切地想要亲眼去见识一下普罗旺斯。他有一种强烈的冲动，想去体验普罗旺斯的生活——在长满薰衣草的田野中漫步，欣赏古老的建筑，到当地的葡萄酒园品尝各式顶级美酒，坐在餐馆的阳台上观察形形色色的人。他再也难以抑制自己的好奇心，于是换上衣服就去了旅行社。经过一番仓促的讨论，他得知他只要自己到火车站，就可乘坐火车直达普罗旺斯的中心——阿维尼翁。

6 令他感到惊奇的是，这趟激起他无尽遐思、色彩缤纷、让他充满想法的旅行，只要坐6个小时的火车就能轻而易举地实现。于是，他直奔火车站，买了张票，订好了座位。

7 乔治到火车站时时间尚早，离火车还有一段时间。火车站旁有个法国餐馆，为顾客提供具有浓郁地中海风情的食物：从市场直接买回的鲜鱼、奶酪、葡萄酒、橄榄、香草，还有吸收了大量阳光、色彩鲜艳的水果和蔬菜。他挑了一张靠窗的桌子坐下，要了一份可能在马赛古港才能吃到的饭菜。

8 可是，随着出发的时间慢慢临近，乔治渐渐感到疲倦了，部分原因是刚才那顿大餐使他有些困倦，还有就是他意识到这趟旅行可能会很累人。他感觉到他得急急忙忙地穿过月台找到他订好的座位，在熙熙攘攘的旅客中挤来挤去，还得很不舒服地在那儿坐上6个小时——至少，和他在家坐在扶手椅上比是这样。等他到站下了车，他还得找旅馆，在陌生的床上睡觉，四处寻找那些在他的梦想中已经熟悉的风景名胜、声音和气味。真有必要到普罗旺斯去一趟吗？在他的想象中，他已经去过了呀。如果他看到的实景和他想象的不一样，那他起码肯定会感到失望。既然坐在扶手椅上就能畅游，离开扶手椅去一趟有什么好处呢？也许出国旅行根本就是一件麻烦事。就待在伦敦的家里不是更简便、更安全吗？

9 所以，乔治付了餐费，乘公共汽车又回到了他位于伦敦南区的寓所，并且决定以后再也不再离开家了。

# Unit 3 Informed or misinformed?

## Unit overview

This unit approaches the topic of media in the new era. It aims to help Ss develop an understanding of the multifaceted influence media exerts on people's lives, awareness of the role every common citizen plays as a media consumer and content creator, and skills for using media wisely. The text in *Active reading* discusses social media's possible negative influences and positive effects, highlighting the importance of developing social media literacy. The text in *Further exploration* explains the five key concepts to be included in a media literacy framework, presenting the means for improving social media literacy. The audio then tells how to figure out what is more true or untrue when dealing with information and opinions in the present world where everyone can be a content producer and distributor. In *Project*, Ss are asked to create a vlog on social media literacy, explaining how to be a smart user of social media. The essay in *Extended reading* further explores the criteria for media to decide on their headline stories. By learning this unit, Ss are expected to better understand the influence of media, reflect on their attitude and behavior concerning social media, and develop skills in using social media smartly and responsibly.

（本页为单元结构流程图，按方框层次转录如下）

**Scenario**
联合国教科文组织发起促进媒体与信息素养的项目，邀请全球博主制作视频博客，宣传社交媒体素养。学生将参与此次活动，制作视频博客。

↓

**Active reading**（Is social media good for us?）
阅读讨论社交媒体影响的文章，理解社交媒体可能存在的正面和负面影响，了解具备社交媒体素养的重要性。

→ **Text**（Social media literacy: The five key concepts）
阅读关于社交媒体素养五个基本概念的介绍，分析其具体含义，认识到公民作为信息传播者的责任。

→ **Audio**（How to choose your news）
聆听音频，了解在网络世界中对各种信息辨别真假、去芜存菁的方法，学会做理性、明智的媒体使用者。

↓

**Project**
思考社交媒体的正负面影响，分析使用社交媒体所面临的挑战，提炼提升媒体素养与责任感的方法，呼吁大众做智慧的媒体使用者。

↓

**Extended reading**（Making the headlines）
阅读关于媒体头条故事特点的说明文，了解媒体选择头条报道的影响因素，理解媒体在信息传播中的作用。

↓

**Self-reflection**
回顾整个单元的学习，反思在内容、语言、结构、交际和行为上的收获。

↓

了解和反思社交媒体对个人与社会的影响，认识公民在使用媒体时面临的挑战和承担的责任，学习成为明智而负责任的社交媒体使用者。

本单元课程思政元素备选清单

| 素材 | 课程思政参考点 | 二十大精神 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Active reading**<br>(Is social media good for us?) | • 认识网络在人们生活中的作用；<br>• 分析社交媒体可能带来的负面影响；<br>• 思考如何健全网络综合治理体系，推动形成良好网络生态，发挥网络的正面作用。 |  |
| **Further exploration – Text**<br>(Social media literacy: The five key concepts) | 了解面对"全世界在观看，二十四小时在线，人人都有麦克风"的现状，"全员媒体"是大势所趋，内容生产的门槛看似变得很低，但实际上要实现高品质、高价值的内容生产并不容易；要坚持底线思维，加强网络法治建设和舆论引导，确保网络信息传播秩序和国家安全、社会稳定，为经济社会各项事业发展提供稳定的舆论环境。 | 通过学习本单元，学生能够更全面地认识到互联网已经深度融入经济社会各领域，成为人们生产生活、求知求美、创新创造的重要平台。建立风清气正的网络生态环境，推进互联网内容建设，弘扬新风正气，令网络空间更加清朗，人人有责。当代青年应该积极规范个人网络行为，增强对网络虚假信息、错误行为的鉴别能力，为营造良好网络生态贡献力量。 |
| **Further exploration – Audio**<br>(How to choose your news) | 了解网络所具有的虚拟性、开放性、跨时空性，用户在网络上的言行更加随心所欲，容易逾越法律和道德的限度。了解网络背后承载着各种类型的文化价值观念、意识形态与政治倾向等。培养在网络世界里辨别真假、去芜存菁的意识和能力。 |  |
| **Extended reading**<br>(Making the headlines) | 了解不同的媒体形态能够满足人们不同场景下的信息需求。无论什么媒体形态，有一些价值是永远值得追求的：真实、快速、客观、公正。探索结合新媒体的力量，满足并放大人们对这些价值的需求。 |  |

（注：第三列"二十大精神"的内容为第二至第四行合并单元格。）

## Scenario

The scenario in this unit can be analyzed from the following aspects:
**Setting:** A UNESCO project to promote media and information literacy
**Topic:** How to increase social media literacy (how to be a smart social media user)
**Identity:** Speaker – Vloggers
Audience – The general public
**Purpose:** To inform the audience of the influence of social media and provide suggestions on using social media smartly

**Teaching suggestions**
T can help Ss generate ideas by thinking about the three w-words: what, why, and how – specifically, what to be discussed about social media, why it's necessary to talk about it, and how to deal with it. For example, some may feel social media is profoundly involved in their own lives and would like to approach the topic from a more personal perspective. They may begin by sharing their own experience of using and being influenced by social media and then share some tips on how to gain more benefits from social media. Ss can create short video clips, upload them to a platform, and vote for the most informative or educational ones.

After the tryout, T can ask Ss to reflect on their difficulties in four aspects, i.e., content (what they can express about the topic and what should be added), language (the expressions and sentence structures they have used and feel hard to use), structure (how to organize ideas for their vlogs) and communication (how to speak with proper body language), and write them down in the table in the Student's book. T can also briefly analyze the most challenging aspects based on Ss' reflection.

## Active reading

In *Scenario*, Ss are expected to explain how social media influences people's lives and how to be a smart social media user. The text objectively addresses social media's effects with points and examples. It introduces some worries about social media, discusses how it can be applied for beneficial purposes, and points out that it is not social media itself but those who are using it to decide whether more good or harm will be done. By learning this text, Ss will form a comprehensive understanding of social media. Ss can also learn the writing technique of engaging the audience.

### Before you read

**Teaching suggestions**
This is an activity aiming to prepare Ss for reading by activating their own experience, so it should be finished before Ss begin to read the text. The first question is more factual and Ss only need to report based on their behavior of using social media. The second question requires some reflection and thinking and therefore may need a little more time. Pair discussion can also help Ss generate ideas on the second question. T can encourage Ss to note down the main points and categorize them into positive or negative influences.

**Reference answers**
1 One app I often use is WeChat. It is an all-in-one platform for communication, socializing, and conducting daily activities for users. I use it for connecting with friends, joining group chats, sharing moments and posts, playing games, and accessing various third-party services.

2 The use of social media has had a profound positive influence on my life in various ways. Firstly, it has made it easier for me to stay in touch with friends and family, regardless of geographical distances. I can share updates, photos, and videos with them. Secondly, I use it as a primary source of news. I can get access to the latest news events, important causes, and viral trends. Thirdly, I join online communities centered around various interests, hobbies, and causes, which provides me with a sense of belonging, support, and opportunities for collaboration. However, I must say I have noticed some negative influence of social media on my life. For example, social media platforms often collect and store my personal data, which makes me worry about the release of my private information. And I have found myself targeted for advertising sometimes.

**Positive influences of social media:**
- Connectivity and communication: Social media has revolutionized how people connect and communicate with each other. It has made it easier to stay in touch with friends and family, regardless of geographical distances. It allows people to share updates, photos, and videos, maintaining and strengthening relationships over time.
- Information sharing: Social media has transformed the way we consume and share information. It has become a primary source of news and has enabled people to disseminate information quickly and widely. This has facilitated the rapid spread of news events, important causes, and viral trends.
- Online communities and support: Social media has facilitated the formation of online communities centered around various interests, hobbies, and causes. These communities provide a sense of belonging, support, and opportunities for collaboration. People can connect with like-minded individuals, share ideas, and find support for personal challenges.
- Business and marketing: Social media has become an essential tool for businesses and marketers. It allows them to reach a vast audience, promote their products or services, and engage with customers directly. Social media platforms provide valuable insights and data, helping businesses refine their strategies.

**Negative influences of social media:**
- Decreased privacy: Social media platforms often collect and store users' personal data, which can raise concerns about privacy. This information can be used for targeted advertising, and sometimes, it may even be misused or compromised.
- Cyberbullying and online harassment: Social media can provide a platform for cyberbullying and online harassment, where individuals may face abusive comments, threats, or anonymous hate messages. This can negatively impact one's self-esteem, mental health, and overall well-being.
- Comparison and self-esteem issues: Seeing carefully idealized versions of others' lives on social media can lead to feelings of inadequacy and lower self-esteem. Constantly comparing one's own life to others' highlight reels can create undue pressure and unhappiness.
- Addiction and time-consuming nature: The addictive nature of social media can lead to excessive use and time consumption. People may find themselves spending significant amounts of time scrolling, which can interfere with productivity, relationships, and personal well-being.

## Is social media good for us?

### Text interpretation

本文探讨当今人们网络生活最为常见的载体——社交媒体，从正面和负面两个视角讨论其属性，得出关于社交媒体利弊的结论。文章第一部分（第一至第三段）是关于社交媒体的负面影响。开头（第一段）点明社交媒体在人们日常生活中深度渗透的特质，引出关于社交媒体负面作用的第一个观点——破坏人际关系，并通过具体举例说明社交媒体可能导致人际焦虑（第二段），接着列举了关于社交媒体负面影响的第二个观点，即利用社交媒体进行不负责任的虚假信息传播以及实施网络暴力（第三段）。文章第二部分（第四至第七段）聚焦社交媒体的正面效应，包括拓展人际网络获取社会资本（第四至第五段）以及促成团结合作（第六段），同时分析了社交媒体能够产生巨大效应的原因（第七段）。文章结尾（第八段）正面回答本文标题所提出的问题"社交媒体对我们有益吗？"，指出社交媒体的效应取决于使用者的意图和行为，因此有必要重视和提高大众使用社交媒体的素养。

本文尽管从正面和负面进行了分析，但核心观点是强调社交媒体使用者才是社交媒体效应的决定者，只要使用者以积极的态度加以善用，则社交媒体是有益的。作者的总体态度也是偏正面的。对社交媒体负面影响的讨论，源自所引用的观点，如第一段"There is an argument that social media ..."；第三段"Another concern is that social media gives people ..."。对正面效应的讨论则更直接地展现作者自己的认同，如第四段"there is growing scientific evidence that social media can actually be beneficial for us"；第六段"Social media also makes it easier to collaborate"。

文章在写作上的突出特点包括用具体事例对观点进行阐释和佐证，以及运用对比、设问等技巧吸引读者。这二者可以在教学中分别结合理解练习2和写作技巧分析练习加以学习。

在学习课文的同时，教师可以进行隐性课程思政。在理解课文第一部分有关社交媒体负面影响时，结合第二段关于利用社交媒体进行不负责任的虚假信息传播以及实施网络暴力的内容，引导学生基于真实的事例分析这类现象对社会、国家和人民生活的危害，探讨如何深化网络综合治理，打造良好网络生态。

在理解课文第二部分社交媒体的正面效应时，可以结合第四、五段内容探讨如何发挥社区新媒体的沟通优势，畅通社情民意表达渠道，建立新型邻里关系。多种社交媒体为居民提供了公开讨论、协商、解决社区公共事务的平台，拓宽了社情民意收集渠道，也为多元主体的沟通交流带来更多机会。居民借助网络空间参与投票、表决、议事等，可有效提高参与社区治理的积极性，培育公共意识和公共规范。社区居民从事着多元的职业、有着不同的生活圈子，虚拟空间的沟通帮助居民互相解答问题、对接供需、解决冲突，实现了线上线下交往的同频共振，同时对加快塑造新型邻里关系，塑造共同体意识，强化居民对社区的认同感和归属感，形成社区治理合力有着积极意义。还可结合第六段关于社交媒体促成团结合作的内容，探讨面向新时代新征程，如何团结一切可以团结的力量，创新传播手段方式，实现正能量拥有大体量、大声量、大流量的目标。

### Culture notes

**Social capital**
Social capital is a concept in social science that refers to a set of shared values or resources that allows individuals to work together in a group to effectively achieve a common purpose. Social capital can also be thought of as the potential ability to obtain resources, favors, or information from one's connections. The idea is generally used to describe how members can band together in society to live harmoniously.

### Language points

1 **Nowadays, it seems that many people are constantly scrolling through social media feeds when they are at home with family or out with friends. (Para. 1)**
**Notes:** The expression "social media feeds" means the updated list of new content available on various social media platforms.

2 **There is an argument that social media, rather than cementing our relationships, is actually driving us apart. (Para. 1)**
**cement:** *vt.* to make a relationship between people, countries, or organizations firm and strong 加强，巩固（关系）
*It is believed by many that trade can well cement the friendship between countries.*

3 **But we are bombarded with news about their lives, the details of which usually show their finest moments. (Para. 2)**
**bombard:** *vt.* do sth. too often or too much, for example criticizing or questioning sb, or giving too much information 轰炸般对待（如大肆批评、连珠炮似地提问、大量提供信息）
*With ill intention, the man bombarded the boy with questions to which he should have known the answers.*

**Notes:** By saying "But we are bombarded with news," the author expresses a negative attitude since this structure embodies the meaning of "too much."

4 **This often makes us feel uneasy, because we worry that our lives are inferior as we compare them to others'. (Para. 2)**
**Meaning:** We often feel unhappy when we see a large number of posts showing other people's great lives. We tend to compare them with our own lives and find that our own lives are not as good as other people's.

5 **It can be a negative experience, resulting in low self-esteem. (Para. 2)**
**Notes:** One's "self-esteem" is how one feels about oneself. For example, if you have low self-esteem, you do not like yourself, you do not think that you are a valuable person, and therefore you do not behave confidently. Low self-esteem can be related to many difficulties in relationships.

6 **Internet trolls post malicious and often fake information, with the intention of hurting and disrupting online communities, or provoking hate campaigns. (Para. 3)**
**Meaning:** Internet trolls intentionally post harmful and often untrue information in order to harm and disrupt online communities, or to provoke hate campaigns.

**disrupt:** *vt.* prevent sth. from continuing in its usual way by causing problems 扰乱；使混乱
*Rail services have been disrupted as part of a coordinated general strike.*

**provoke:** *vt.* cause a reaction or feeling, esp. a sudden one 激起；引起
*The writer hopes her article will provoke readers into thinking seriously about the negative impact of social media.*

**Notes:** "Hate campaigns" refer to coordinated efforts by individuals or groups to spread and promote messages of hate, prejudice, or discrimination against a particular person, group, or community. These campaigns typically involve the dissemination of offensive and inflammatory content with the aim of causing hostility and animosity toward the targeted individuals or groups.

7 **It involves the potential of individuals to secure benefits and invent solutions to problems through membership in social networks. (Para. 4)**
**Meaning:** When individuals are part of social networks, they have the opportunity to obtain advantages and come up with solutions to various issues.

**secure:** get or achieve sth. that will be permanent, esp. after a lot of effort （尤指经过大量努力）获得，得到
*Her determination and efforts secured her position in the final round of the competition.*

8 **Social media, however, helps the establishment of relationships with people with whom we have weak ties, ... (Para. 4)**
**Meaning:** However, social media helps us build relationships with people we are not familiar with and don't have much connection with.

9 **Even ephemeral relationships can be unexpectedly powerful sometimes. (Para. 5)**
**Notes:** In this sentence, "ephemeral relationship" means a relationship that only exists for a very short period of time. The expression "unexpectedly powerful" means showing a lot of power that is far greater than expected. The contrast between "ephemeral relationships" and "unexpectedly powerful" can help readers realize that social media can be beneficial in aspects that people may not have considered before.

10 **It can bring together like-minded people with a common aim or purpose. (Para. 6)**
**Meaning:** It can make people who think similarly gather and make efforts together.

11 **... a Kenyan television journalist called for aid via social media sites, alongside traditional forms of media. (Para. 6)**
**Meaning:** A television journalist from Kenya used both social media platforms and traditional forms of media to appeal for assistance or help.

**call for sth.:** say publicly that sth. must happen 呼吁；要求
*The environmental activists are calling for immediate action to combat climate change.*

**alongside:** *prep.* used to say that people or things do sth. or exist together at the same time 与……一起
*Alongside her academic studies, she actively participates in extracurricular activities.*

12 **... it can also be used as a collaborative tool to do good, given its reach and influence. (Para. 8)**
**given:** *prep.* taking sth. into account 鉴于；考虑到
*Given the difficult circumstances, the students have done really well.*

**reach:** *n.* [sing.] the number of people that see or hear an advertisement, television program, etc. 覆盖面
*After three years of development, the network had a reach of about 5 million homes.*

13 **Social media is a blank canvas, and we are the ones painting on it. (Para. 8)**
**Notes:** A metaphor has been used in this sentence. Social media is compared to a blank canvas and the users are compared to painters who are painting on the canvas. The author argues in a vivid way that just like a canvas, social media itself is not good or bad. It is the users or painters who can decide whether the result is good or not.

### Getting the ideas

**Reference answers**
1
B

2
1) social media feeds
2) lists of friends
3) inferior
4) malicious
5) disrupt online communities
6) severe consequences
7) secure benefits
8) membership in social networks
9) we have weak ties
10) innovative ideas
11) a common aim or purpose
12) swift and overwhelming

**Additional activity**
When Ss have completed the table of Activity 2, T could ask Ss to further discuss in pairs what can be done when faced with the possible negative influences of social media. Ss could first vote on whether they share similar experiences listed under the three aspects of possible harm. Then, they could discuss specific suggestions for avoiding common harm.

**Reference answers**
To avoid the common negative influences of social media mentioned in the text, consider the following suggestions:
1 Mindful social media use: Be aware of how much time you spend on social media and set limits to prevent excessive scrolling. Focus on meaningful interactions with close friends and family in real life.
2 Quality over quantity: Evaluate your social media connections and prioritize authentic relationships. Consider unfollowing or filtering out accounts that make you feel inadequate or anxious.
3 Self-comparison awareness: Recognize that social media often showcases the best moments of people's lives. Avoid comparing yourself to others and instead, focus on your own growth and achievements.
4 Promote positivity: Refrain from engaging in or sharing hurtful content. Report and block cyberbullies and trolls to maintain a positive online environment.
5 Critical consumption: Verify information before sharing it. Avoid spreading rumors or fake news that can harm others or disrupt online communities.

3
**Teaching suggestions**
For the two questions in Activity 3, the answers can be found based on the comprehension and analysis of the text, specifically Paragraph 7 and Paragraph 8 respectively. T could also ask Ss to share their own opinions about the two questions, including whether they agree with what the text presents and any additional thoughts they have.

**Reference answers**
1 There are different kinds of applications that are all linked together to enable people to communicate. Each of these applications has a variety of tools that let people interact in different ways. When a celebrity posts a picture, there may be thousands of shares, likes, and so on. That is a sign of a community forming around this particular person. When someone has millions of followers, their influence can become significant.
2 Social media can be good if used with positive intent. To ensure positive effects, we should become more literate in our use of social media. Instead of waiting for people to figure things out themselves, we should teach them social media skills.

### Analyzing writing techniques

**Reference answers**
1 Our growing lists of friends on social media often include people we barely know. But we are bombarded with news of their lives ... 【a】
2 ... your neighbors might help dig you out of a snowdrift or a group of friends provide you with emotional support. 【a】
3 Even ephemeral relationships can be unexpectedly powerful sometimes. 【b】
4 ... with the equivalent of about 10 million dollars collected within just two weeks. 【b】
5 So is social media good for us? The answer is that it can be – if used with positive intent. 【c】

### Building your language

**Reference answers**
1
1 are bombarded with
2 low self-esteem
3 share their opinions anonymously
4 provoke anxiety
5 enlightening pieces of advice

**information**
*a.* up-to-date information, new information, useful information, malicious information, fake information
*v.* post information, provide sb. with information, share information, give information

**relationship**
*a.* unreal relationship, inauthentic relationship, interpersonal relationship, close relationship, ephemeral relationship
*v.* cement the relationship, reveal the relationship between ..., build a relationship, establish a relationship, disrupt the relationship, harm the relationship, develop a relationship

**Making sentences**
information
1) I can provide you with up-to-date information on the technological advancements in artificial intelligence.
2) Educators play a crucial role in promoting information literacy by teaching students how to critically evaluate sources and discern reliable information from misinformation on the Internet.

relationship
1) Sarah realized that most of her social media connections were merely inauthentic relationships, as she barely knew many of the people on her friends' list.

3
Social media has undergone considerable development in China, reaching more than one billion users. It's not uncommon that users have accounts on multiple social media sites. They actively engage in different types of interactions, share personal lives and perspectives, and always receive swift reactions from others. There is a tendency of increasing younger social media users now. The influence social media exerts on young users is unexpectedly powerful. Given the situation, experts call for measures to be taken to make young users become more literate in their use of media and information and guide young people to use social media with positive intent.

### Sharing your ideas

**Reference answers**

Social media has become an integral part of our daily lives, shaping how we connect, communicate, and consume information. Personally, I use social media platforms daily. People use social media throughout the day, with peak usage during leisure time.

Social media serves various purposes, allowing us to stay connected with friends and family, share updates and photos, and express ourselves creatively. It has also transformed into a powerful tool for accessing news, trends, and opinions on various topics.

The functioning of social media revolves around user-generated content, network connections, and algorithms. Users create and share content, which is distributed to their network. Algorithms analyze user behavior to deliver personalized content and ads, creating a cycle of engagement.

Social media's influence on daily life is significant. It has revolutionized communication, making it easier to connect globally. However, excessive usage can lead to addiction and isolation. Social media's impact on mental health is evident, as comparisons with others can breed feelings of inadequacy. It has also transformed marketing, providing new avenues for businesses to reach customers. Overall, social media has both positive and negative effects, necessitating mindful usage.

**Suggestions for evaluation**
For this activity, Ss' sharing can be evaluated according to the following criteria:
- whether Ss show their understanding of social media with logical reasoning
- whether Ss include examples from Ss' own experience

## Further exploration

The task in *Scenario* asks Ss to create a vlog about social media literacy. The text in this part introduces a framework of social media literacy, explaining the five key concepts in detail. It offers insights into how to be a responsible content creator and distributor when using social media. The audio talks about what to pay attention to when faced with information and opinions from the media. It provides principles to follow when striving to be a smart user of social media.

T can connect *Active reading* and *Further exploration* by pointing out that while the former argues that the users decide how beneficial social media can be and that improving social media literacy is pressing, the latter offers guidelines for better social media literacy of users. T can ask Ss to think about how the negative influences discussed in *Active reading* can be avoided if the principles mentioned in *Further exploration* are applied.

## Social media literacy: The five key concepts (Text)

### Language points

1 **When creating our own media, we should be aware of what outside influences might be shaping how we create and share original content online. (Para. 2)**
**Meaning:** When we produce and share our own media content on the Internet, we should be conscious of external factors that might be influencing or affecting the way we create and share that content.

**shape:** *vt.* influence sth. such as a belief, opinion, etc., and make it develop in a particular way 形成（某种信仰、看法等）；影响（某事物的发展等）
*Understanding how the author's life experience shaped her can help with the interpretation of her works.*

**Notes:** Here "might be" is used as hedging, an important feature of academic writing. Hedging is the use of cautious language to modify the strength of one's claims.

2 **The media is responsible for the majority of the observations and experiences from which we build up our personal understanding of the world. (Para. 3)**
**Meaning:** How we understand the world is decided by what we see and experience, and it mostly comes from the media.

3 **Such messages have been pre-constructed and contain built-in views, interpretations, and conclusions. (Para. 3)**
**Meaning:** These messages have been constructed beforehand. They unavoidably include opinions, explanations, and conclusions from the people who constructed these messages.

4 **How might the audience expand over time to include potential friends, colleagues, employers, or significant others? (Para. 4)**
**Notes:** Here a question form is used to prompt the reader to consider the potential consequences and implications of posting content online. By posing this question, the author wants to draw attention to the fact that the audience of online content can grow and evolve beyond its initial intended recipients. Questions are a powerful rhetorical device that invites the reader to actively participate in the content and consider the implications of the statements being made.

5 **... but there is a price we pay for the use of these "free" services. (Para. 5)**
**Notes:** Here the quoted "free" expresses two meanings. One is that the services are believed to be free by many social media users, and the other is that the services are not truly free, because the users have to pay in another way.

6 **All of the content we post online is used to build a profile of us as potential consumers. (Para. 5)**
**Meaning:** What we post online is all used as useful information for analyzing and describing our characteristics that indicate possible buying behavior.

7 **We often edit out what is undesirable about our world and our lives. (Para. 6)**
**Meaning:** When we choose to post the good things from our world, we are at the same time taking away the unpleasant part of our lives.

**edit out:** remove sth. when you are preparing a book, piece of film, etc. for printing or broadcasting （在编辑、剪辑等过程中）删除，去掉
*During the post-production phase, the film editor will carefully edit out any unnecessary scenes to ensure a smooth and concise final cut.*

8 **We should all hold ourselves responsible for the messages we post. (Para. 7)**
**Notes:** Here the expression "hold somebody responsible (for something)" means to make someone become in charge of or look after something. It also has another meaning – blame someone for (an accident, mistake, crime, etc.), which is more frequently used. For example:
*The company will hold the employees responsible for the project's failure due to their negligence and poor decision-making.*

**Reference answers**
1
1) create and share
2) built-in views
3) unintended audience
4) lie beyond our intentions
5) potential commercial purposes
6) edit out

## （续）

2
**Teaching suggestions**
When Ss finish their pair work for Activity 2, they can share their lists with the whole class. By gathering all the listed dos and don'ts, a questionnaire can be designed with a Likert scale of importance and conducted on Ss to find out which of the items are the most important ones as agreed upon by Ss. Then Ss can discuss why they are the most important.

**Reference answers**
1  We should not take media messages as completely reflecting reality, since they contain built-in perspectives.
2  We should think twice about the content we construct and share, as it could reach audiences beyond those we intended.
3  We should be careful with private information when posting content, for it may be used for commercial purposes.
4  We should not assume that others' posts are a complete or accurate representation of their lives, because we all tend to edit out what we don't like.

### How to choose your news? (Audio)
### Scripts
1  Compared with the old days, the Internet has multiplied the amount of information and opinions available thanks to social media, blogs, and online videos. However, different sources may disagree not only on opinions but on facts themselves. So how do we find out the truth or something close to it?
2  One of the best ways is to get the original news directly. Instead of reading articles that interpret a scientific study or an expert's speech, we can find the original material and make our own judgments. During major events, people can post updates and recordings in the midst of the chaos. We need to keep in mind that these versions often combine the facts with the opinions of the contributors.
3  Bear in mind that the more chaotic a situation in a news story, the more cautious we should be about reaching a conclusion while it's being broadcast. During unfolding events like violent attacks and natural disasters, the media attempts to provide continuous coverage even when no reliable new information is available. This sometimes leads to incorrect information or false accusations against innocent people. So allow time for complete details to emerge and false reports to be refuted.
4  Although objectivity is a key principle of quality journalism, sometimes media bias cannot be avoided. Turning to various sources and noting the differences between them enables us to put the pieces together and form a more complete picture for ourselves. It's also crucial to separate facts from opinions. Words like "think," "likely," or "probably" mean that the reporter is being careful or making assumptions.
5  We should also watch out for reports that rely on anonymous sources. These could be people who have little connection to the story or have an interest in influencing coverage. Their（页底截断，接下页）

（接上页）anonymity makes them unaccountable for the information they provide.
6  Finally, and most importantly, try to check if news is true before spreading it. While social media has enabled the news to reach us faster, it has also allowed rumors to spread and false information to survive. So before sharing a questionable piece of information, do a web search to find additional information or context that might have been missed.
7  Today more than ever we are able to receive and pass on information, but with this ability comes a responsibility. We need to ensure that as we allow information to flow without barriers, it does not become a flood, leaving us less informed than before the digital media era started.

### Language points
1  **However, different sources may disagree not only on opinions but on facts themselves.** (Para. 1)
**Meaning:** However, when we read messages provided by different sources, we may find them to be different not only in what they believe but also in what they report to have happened.

2  **During major events, people can post updates and recordings in the midst of the chaos.** (Para. 2)
**in the midst of:**
1)  if you are in the midst of an event or situation, it is happening around you 在……的进行过程中；正值……发生的时候
    *Although it's challenging, we should try and find hope in the midst of despair.*
2)  in the middle of a place or a group of things or people 在……当中；在……中间
    *We finally found her in the midst of a group of people dancing and singing merrily.*

3  **During unfolding events like violent attacks and natural disasters, the media attempts to provide continuous coverage even when no reliable new information is available.** (Para. 3)
**Meaning:** During events that are still in the process of developing, such as violent attacks and natural disasters, the media tries to keep reporting the development even when they don't have believable new information.

4  **This sometimes leads to incorrect information or false accusations against innocent people.** (Para. 3)
**accusation:** n.[C] a statement saying that sb. is guilty of a crime or of doing sth. wrong 控告；指控；指责
    *It has been reported that the new administration is facing accusations of corruption.*

5  **Words like "think," "likely," or "probably" mean that the reporter is being careful or making assumptions.** (Para. 4)
**Notes:** This sentence means when the reporter uses words like "think," "likely," or "probably," it（页底截断，接下页）

（接上页）is very likely that either they are not very sure about the source of the facts they are reporting or they are making inferences that are subjective. This sentence reveals the behavior of some reporters who claim something to be true even if they lack proof. Teachers can guide students to think about the purpose for the reporters to behave like this.
6  **Their anonymity makes them unaccountable for the information they provide.** (Para. 5)
**Meaning:** Their identities are not known, so they don't feel responsible for the information they give.

**Reference answers**
1
1) directly
2) our own judgments
3) being broadcast
4) complete details
5) refuted
6) various sources
7) opinions
8) anonymous sources
9) spreading it
10) context

2
1  While information is more easily accessed, it is also more easily created and distributed. When every individual can be a content creator and distributor, there is less guarantee on the quality of the content, leading to the situation that we may be exposed to more unreliable information and are therefore more misinformed or less informed.
2  To ensure information does not become a flood, we need to adopt a critical and discerning approach when consuming and sharing information. Firstly, get information directly from original news sources whenever possible to form our own judgments and avoid interpretations or biases from secondary articles. Secondly, be cautious about reaching conclusions during unfolding events, and allow time for complete details to emerge and false reports to be refuted. Thirdly, differentiate between facts and opinions in media reports and be mindful of language indicating assumptions or cautiousness. What's more, be wary of relying on information from anonymous sources.

### Sharing your ideas
**Reference answers**
•  There are several challenges we face when using social media and I'll just name a few there. First, a considerable amount of misinformation is distributed by individuals anonymously, sometimes for the purpose of spreading hurt. Second, other people's posts do not usually reflect their true lives and can be misleading if we want to fully know a person based on their posts. Third, there are reports about unhealthy use of and even addiction to social media, which（页底截断，接下页）

（接上页）severely harms a person's normal life.
•  In order to cope with these challenges, we need to learn some tips for using social media smartly. First, when exposed to information, be careful not to make a hasty judgment. Analyzing the source of the information and turning to multiple sources to compare may help. Second, we need to understand that posts are usually created with the intention of constructing a certain image. They do not necessarily reflect the true selves of the posters but help infer how the posters want other people to see them. Third, if there is a tendency to overuse social media, we need to develop our own awareness and set rules for ourselves about how often and how long we can use social media.

**Suggestions for evaluation**
For this activity, Ss' sharing can be evaluated according to the following criteria:
•  whether Ss can clearly state the challenges of using social media
•  whether Ss can provide relevant suggestions for coping with the stated challenges

## Project
**Sample:**
(*Expressions and sentences in red are from the input materials.)

Let me get started with a very little question. What was the last time you visited a post or talked to someone on a social media site? Several minutes ago? Or just now as you are listening to me? You see, social media has become an inseparable part of our life. It is so deeply and naturally involved in our daily life that we tend to take for granted that everyone is born knowing how to use social media. Actually, we are not. Social media can bring a lot of convenience, but it can also cause harm. It is crucial that we know some basics about it and how to be a smart user of social media.

Now, do we gain something from social media? Of course. We use social media to express our identity and opinions and to **build social relationships** – to stay in touch with our family and friends, to share information, to **provide emotional support**, and to feel connected. Social media also enables individuals to participate in social activities in distant communities. Many people from various backgrounds can collaborate without knowing one another. They can gather to **form unimaginable power and influence** for important courses.

However, we can't deny that there are also challenges when using social media. When we are connected to friends via social media, we may also feel uneasy when we are **bombarded with the posts showing their finest moments**. We may worry that our own lives **are inferior** in comparison and **are subject to low self-esteem**. When we enjoy easy access to information, we are also facing the threat of **being misinformed**. Since everyone can share their opinions anonymously, some may **spread fake information with the intention of hurting and disrupting online communities**. When we post something private about ourselves to share with our friends, some days later we may be annoyed to find that it is known by someone we never want it to reach or that it is used for analyzing consumer behavior.

So does it mean that social media is bound to be both beneficial and harmful and we have to accept its harm if we want to enjoy its benefits? I don't think so. **Social media itself is a blank canvas, and we are the ones painting on it.** How we use social media is **the key to making it beneficial**. Here are some tips which can help us become a smart user. First, stop comparing our own lives with others' posts. As someone posts an online meme or a selfie, they are often **following unstated instructions** for what these images should look like and say. These images do not **reflect their true lives or true selves**. Therefore, there is no need for us to take these as a source of peer pressure. Second, double check a piece of information before taking it as truth and **spreading it**. Turn to various source, note the differences between them, **put the pieces together** and try to **form a more complete picture**. Even news reports also have **built-in views** and perspectives. Finally, be careful with what we post online. It can **reach unintended audiences** and be interpreted beyond our intentions. In addition, while we seem to enjoy the service of social media sites **free of charge**, the content we post online is used to **build a profile** of us as potential consumers.

Social media has provided us with easy access to numerous messages from all kinds of sources. It（页底截断，接下页）

（接上页）has also enabled us to **reach a global audience**, with increasing **ability to persuade and influence**. There is pressing need that we improve our **social media literacy** and become smart and responsible content consumers, **creators, and distributors**. It is a prerequisite for social media to play a positive role in our life and society.

**Suggestions for evaluation**

| Components | Criteria / Traits |
| --- | --- |
| **Content** | • Relevance: Addressing the assigned task by talking about the benefits and possible harms of social media and offering suggestions for becoming a smart social media user<br>• Appeal: Including relevant messages, using body language properly to enlarge the effect of the content |
| **Organization** | • Structure: Organizing ideas following a situation – analysis – suggestion pattern<br>• Coherence: Using effective signpost expressions to move on from describing situation to analyzing causes, and to offering suggestions<br>• Clarity: Arranging the ideas with a clear logic, paying special attention to the connection between challenges and solutions<br>• Technique: Using techniques to engage the audience |
| **Language** | • Diversity: Using an adequate range of vocabulary and sentence structures<br>• Acquisition: Using as many as possible of the new words, expressions and grammatical structures learned in the unit |
| **Delivery** | • Fluency: Presenting in an effortless, smooth and clear way with a natural colloquial flow<br>• Engagement: Keeping the language clear and simple |

## Extended reading
In the previous parts, Ss learned to address the topic of social media literacy. They mainly take the perspective of a common social media user when analyzing the gains and challenges of using social media and providing suggestions for becoming a smart and responsible content creator and distributor. In this part, Ss will read an expository essay talking about how media makes decisions when selecting headline stories among numerous incidents taking place all over the world every day. It helps Ss understand the underlying guidelines for media to function and realize every news story being released conveys the journalists' or editors' viewpoints and preferences. By exploring the topic from another perspective, Ss are expected to be able to think critically and become a more rational user of media.

### Making the headlines
### Language points
1  **It isn't very often that the media leads with the same story everywhere in the world … But this is exactly what occurred on April 15, 2019 …** (Para. 1)
**Meaning:** There aren't many stories that come top in newspapers all around the world, but what happened on April 15, 2019 was one of them.
**Notes:** For some nouns ending with "-um," the plural form is to change "-um," into "-a," such as medium – media, datum – data, memorandum – memoranda. The word "media" can be used with both singular and plural verbs.

2  **Such an event would have to be of enormous international prominence.** (Para. 1)
**Notes:** The expression "be of + n." equals "be + adj." in meaning. For example, be of importance = be important, be of significance = be significant, be of value = be valuable, be of benefit = be beneficial, etc. But "be of + n." is more formal and is therefore typically used in academic writing.

3  **So shocking, so remarkable was the news that many people across the globe …** (Para. 2)
**Notes:** This is an inverted sentence. To put it in the normal structure: "The news was so shocking and so remarkable that …" The inverted structure used is for the purpose of emphasizing. Another example is: "So excited was she that she cried."

4  **It was also very human, in the sense that the cathedral is regarded as a valuable historical reflection of culture, and an amazing piece of architectural engineering and craft.** (Para. 4)
**Notes:** The expression "in the sense that" is used when we can see that our use of a certain phrase may cause misunderstanding. In this context, to say such a story of destruction is very human may seem odd, but the author explains he is referring to how closely connected the incident is to human culture and architectural achievements.

5  **Take the news story about a mouse holding up a flight from Vietnam to Japan.** (Para. 5)
**hold up:** delay sb. or sth. 耽搁某人或阻延某事
    *His train was held up and he was late for the conference.*
**Notes:** Here "take" means "take … for example."

6  **By the time it took off, the plane was more than four hours delayed.** (Para. 5)
**take off:**
1)  if an aircraft takes off, it leaves the ground and starts flying （飞机）起飞
    *I was very excited when the plane took off, as it's the first time I had ever traveled by air.*
2)  have a particular amount of time away from work 从工作时间抽出（时间）；休假
    *I have to take several days off to take care of my father who hasn't been feeling very well.*

7  **… they have to use to frame a news story …** (Para. 7)
**frame:** v. (fml.) carefully plan the way you are going to ask a question, make a statement, etc. （小心措辞以）表达
    *He paused for a moment, carefully framing his answer to my question.*

8  **Similarly, an event which is about to happen ("today," "this evening," or "tonight") may also be newsworthy, …** (Para. 7)
**Notes:** The suffix "-worthy" is added to a word and forms an adjective, meaning being suitable for or deserving to receive a particular thing. Examples include "noteworthy" and "trustworthy."

9  **… or at least following set guidelines …** (Para. 8)
**Notes:** The expression "set guidelines" means rules and principles which have been decided upon.

10  **… the power to influence the public, more or less secretly.** (Para. 8)
**Notes:** By saying "secretly," the author emphasizes that media usually exerts its influence in an implicit way which is hard to be realized or detected. This is also why ordinary people need to improve media literacy.

11  **… this power is being eroded, or at least transferred to ordinary people.** (Para. 9)
**Meaning:** The power of the media is reducing, or at least ordinary people are having more influence.

12  **… they are going to turn increasingly to the Internet for their sources, …** (Para. 10)
**turn to:**
1)  start to do or use sth. new, esp. as a way of solving a problem 改用；开始使用
    *More and more people are turning to the Internet for news rather than to newspapers.*（页底截断，接下页）

（接上页）2)  try to get help, advice, or sympathy from sb. 求助于；求教于
    *You can turn to your teacher for help when you have difficulty understanding the book.*

**Reference answers**
a-7
b-10
c-4
d-8
e-9
f-4
g-5
h-1
i-2
j-8

## Self-reflection
In this part, T can ask Ss to refer back to Try it out in the Student's Book and reflect on what they have learned to complete the task. Ss can categorize their gains according to the same four aspects, i.e., content, language, structure, and communication. They can also reflect on how learning the unit may influence their action in daily life. Then they can write down their specific gains in the self-reflection table in the Student's Book.

## Translation of the texts
**Active reading**
**社交媒体对我们有益吗?**

1  如今，许多人无论在家和家人一起还是在外和朋友一起，总是习惯于刷手机。有种观点认为，社交媒体不仅没能巩固我们的关系，反而使我们越来越疏远。果真如此吗?
2  好吧，有些人可能会认为，在某种程度上，社交媒体正在用不真实的关系取代我们亲密的友谊。我们社交媒体上不断增长的朋友列表中常常包括我们几乎不认识的人。但有关他们的生活的信息却轰炸着我们。这些信息的细节通常展示的是他们最好的一面。这常常会让我们感到不安，因为担心自己活得比别人差。当我浏览在社交网站上所关注的人精心营造的身份时，我常常开始怀疑自己的吸引力、工作、经历，甚至自己的家人和朋友。这可以是一种非常消极的体验，导致低自尊。
3  另一个担忧是，社交媒体给人们提供了一个匿名分享看法的平台，有时它会传播伤害性的内容。键盘侠会发布恶意的、往往是虚假的信息，以故意破坏和扰乱网络社区，或挑起仇恨运动。网络暴力对青少年的影响日益增强，在一些案件中它已经导致了严重后果。
4  然而，越来越多的科学证据表明，社交媒体其实可以为我们带来益处。一些研究揭示了社交网站和"社会资本"这一社会学概念之间的关系。社会资本是指个人凭借社会网络身份获利和解决问题的潜力。例如，社会资本可以是一群雪中送炭的邻居，或是一群为你提供情感支持的朋友。认为社交媒体和社会资本之间存在关系背后的理念是，各种形式的沟通都有利于建立人际关系。面对面交流常见于非常亲密的关系中，比如我们与父母或最好的朋友之间。而社交媒体则帮助我们与较为疏远、互动较少的人建立联系，比如与同事或偶然相识的人。这很重要，因为尽管我们可能与这些人并不亲密，但他们可以为我们提供有用的信息、新颖的观点和未来的机遇。
5  即便是短暂的关系有时也会有意想不到的作用。有些网站能将邻居们联系起来。比如，人们可以分享当地关于保洁、园丁或其他服务人员的最新消息。还有一些网站能让大学生们分享知识、彼此提问并回答问题。你可能花费数周时间查阅课本和笔记，但仍然没法解开一道统计题。最后，你在一个网站上发布了问题，怀着渺茫的希望期待收到相关回复。然而，几分钟后，你惊讶地发现一个或许你永远都不会见到的陌生人提供了一条启发性的建议，帮助你解决了这个问题。
6  社交媒体也使合作变得更加容易。它可以将志同道合、有共同目标或目的的人聚集在一起。例如，当肯尼亚遭受严重旱灾，几千人性命垂危时，一名肯尼亚电视记者通过社交媒体网（页底截断，接下页）

（接上页）站以及更传统的媒体寻求援助。该求援迅速得到广泛响应，仅在两周内就募集了相当于约1000万美元的款项。
7  "肯尼亚人支持肯尼亚"倡议标志着非洲的普通人首次通过社交媒体共同拯救生命。那么，社交媒体上的帖子如何引起这样惊人的反响呢? 这么说吧，我们不应该将社交媒体视为单一事物。它包含各种互相联系的应用能让人们进行交流。其中每一个应用都有一系列工具让人们实现多样互动。当某个名人发布一张照片，就可能会带来成千上万的分享、点赞、评论等等。这标志着围绕这个特定人物形成了一个社群。当某些人拥有以百万计的关注者时，他们的影响力就会变得特别大。
8  那么，社交媒体对我们是有益的吗? 答案是，它可以有益——如果我们出于积极正面的目的使用它。尽管社交媒体有可能破坏人际关系、引起不安，但鉴于其覆盖面和影响力，它也可以成为大家共行善事的合作工具。许多人认为，为了确保这一点，我们必须提高使用社交媒体的素养。大众尤其是年轻一代，往往以为他们天生就知道如何使用社交媒体。实际上，我们应该教会人们使用社交媒体的技能，而不是等着大家自己解决问题时陷入困境。毕竟，使社交媒体变得有益的关键在于我们如何看待和使用它。社交媒体就好像一块崭新的画布，而我们才是绘画的人。

**Further exploration**
**社交媒体素养：五个关键概念**

1  在线数字技术以20年前无法想象的方式赋予了用户权力。社交媒体网站让我们能够接触到全球的观众，也提升了普通用户的说服力和影响力。如何建立一个媒体素养框架，帮助我们成为负责任的内容创作者和传播者? 以下是五个应该考虑的关键概念。

**所有媒体信息都是被"构建的"**
2  当我们在网络上发布模因或自拍照片时，关于这些图像应该呈现为什么样子、表达什么信息，我们往往都遵循着心照不宣的规则。从不那么自然的笑容到镜头前的故作姿态，我们总是在构建自己的媒体形象，而这些形象并不反映我们的生活或真实的自我。在打造自己的社交媒体时，我们应该意识到哪些外部因素可能会影响自己在网上发布和分享原创内容的方式。

**媒体信息塑造了我们对现实的认知**
3  媒体是我们大部分观察和体验的来源，我们在此基础上建立对世界的理解。我们对现实的认识大部分都基于媒体所提供的信息。这些信息都是预先构建的，包含预设的观点、解读和结论。因此我们对现实的感知很大程度上由媒体决定。

**不同受众对相同信息有不同理解**
4  当我们在网上发布内容时，我们对其意想不到的受众有多了解呢? 随着时间的推移，这些受众如何扩散至潜在的朋友、同事、雇主或其他重要的人? 互联网内容的传播范围很容易超出目标受众，即便是我们所认为的一对一私人交流也是如此。我们应该花时间考虑到超出预料的解读和后果。

**媒体信息具有潜在商业影响**
5  媒体受到商业因素的影响。大多数媒体运营都是门生意，所以必须盈利。我们创作的许多内容都是免费发布和传播的，但我们为使用这些"免费"服务付出了代价。我们在网络上发布的所有内容都被用于构建我们作为潜在消费者的个人资料。我们在社交网络上所分享和发布的关于其他人的许多内容，可能被用于潜在的商业目的，且并未经本人同意。

**媒体信息包含观点**
6  所有媒体信息都包含价值观。我们发布度假的照片，而不是反映日常无聊生活的图片，我们是在如何传达一个关于世界上什么是美好和有价值的信息呢? 我们经常裁剪掉自己的世界和生活中不受欢迎的东西。
7  虽然媒体环境已经发生了根本性的变化，但评价内容的标准却没有变。我们不再只是媒体的消费者，而是内容的创作者和传播者，同时也是编辑、评论员和新闻记者。我们都应该对自己发布的信息负责。

**Extended reading**
**制造头条新闻**

1  世界各地的媒体都以头条报道同一新闻的情形并不很常见。这样的事件具有巨大的国际影响力。但2019年4月15日就出现了这样的情形。当时，一场大火摧毁了巴黎圣母院教堂的大部分。这座12世纪哥特式地标建筑的尖顶、时钟和中央架构都被烧毁了。成千上万的人聚集在教堂周围的街道上，默默地看着燃烧的火焰。不夸张地讲，全世界都十分震惊。
2  但是，使这场火灾成为值得铭记的事件的不仅仅是它的历史和国际影响，还有严重和震撼程度。这一极其令人震撼极其引人注目的消息让全世界各地的许多人冲动地去把这一消息告诉其他人，从而证实了那句老话："坏事传千里"。事发多年以后，许多人依然清楚地记得他们第一次听到这一消息时身在何处，以及当时正在做什么。
3  一切重大新闻事件都是如此。我记得上小学的时候，老师脸色煞白地向一班吃惊的七岁孩子通报说，肯尼迪总统死了。我并不知道肯尼迪总统是谁，但是我听到这一消息后非常不安，后来就跑回家去告诉了父母（当然，他们已经知道了）。事实上，这是我最原始的记忆之一。
4  那么，新闻到底是什么? 一个事件光有客观重要性显然还不够——世界上有大量全球性的问题，从贫困问题到全球变暖，这些都会造成惊人的后果——但由于它们都是进行中的，并不都会在同一天成为头条。对比之下，圣母院大教堂的火灾不仅具有国际影响，而且奇特怪异、出人意料，还极具人性，因为教堂是公认的人类宝贵文化的历史反映，是建筑和工艺的绝世之作。
5  奇特怪异并不意味着重大。就拿一只老鼠延误了一架从越南飞往日本的航班这条新闻为例。在河内机场有人发现那只老鼠在一架飞机的过道里逃窜。它最终被12名技术人员合力逮住。他们怕它会咬坏电线，造成短路。起飞时，这架飞机已经晚点了四个多小时。
6  你也许会说，这并不是具有重大国际影响的事件。但是这一事件在全球却颇具反响，从亚（页底截断，接下页）

（接上页）洲到美洲，甚至是苏格兰，在线报纸都有刊登。
7  新闻价值的另一个元素是"即时性"。这是指事件发生在近期。一周前发生的事件一般来说就不是新闻了——除非你刚刚读到它。"何时"是受训记者常被教导用以勾勒新闻故事的五个"何"问题之一（其余四个问题分别是"何人""何事""何地"和"何故"）；"今天""今晨""昨天"这几个词可能是新闻报道最为常用的时间副词。同样，即将发生的事件（"今天""今晚"或"今夜"）也可能具有新闻价值，虽然从定义上讲，这种事件不出人意料，因此不那么耸人听闻。
8  说到即时性，能够实时播报新闻的媒体，如电视、广播和互联网，就比报纸的优势大多了。眼看着事件在你眼前展开与次日早餐时在报上读到它的感觉大不相同。但是，电视新闻未必比报纸报道更客观或更可靠，因为你在屏幕上看到的图像是经记者或编辑根据特殊的目的，或至少是按照既定方针筛选的，并且以一个独特的视角展现给观众。如果把摄像机镜头移到别的地方，你就会看到另一番景象。这就是为什么人们通常会说"媒体霸权"，即媒体或多或少暗中影响公众的能力。
9  但也许在21世纪，这种权力正在减弱，或至少转移给了普通民众。个人博客的大量出现，通过网站自我广播的可能性以及权限开放网页的增长都意味着任何人如果有任何话要说——或者有任何东西要展示的话——现在都能立刻让全世界的观众看到。
10  当然，这并不意味着报纸和电视即将在一夜之间消失。但是，在永不休止地搜寻有趣新闻——奇特怪异、出人意料和具有人性的新闻——的过程中，报纸和电视将越来越多地借助这些网站来收集资料，为全球信息网络提供稀奇的地方视角。

# Unit 4 City

## Unit overview

This unit explores the topic of cities and their development from various perspectives. It aims to help Ss identify the characteristics of cities and reflect on how a city becomes attractive, which will in turn lead to Ss' deeper understanding of the relationship between cities, their inhabitants, and culture. The text in *Active reading* depicts the musical city of Vienna by telling the story of the masterpiece *The Blue Danube* and discussing the factors that turned Vienna into a center of classical music. The text in *Further exploration* introduces a charming Chinese town, Wuzhen, revealing its history and modern development as a cultural and technological attraction. The video then explains the basic principles for creating an attractive city. In *Project*, Ss are invited to give a presentation introducing their own cities. The essay in *Extended reading* reviews the process of urbanization which caused the birth of cities and also envisions the future of cities. By learning this unit, Ss are expected to better understand cities in general and their respective cities, develop closer bonds with their cities and better ability to promote them.

（本页为单元结构流程图，按方框层次转录如下）

**Scenario**
世界文化旅游论坛举办城市展。参展城市将设立推介展台，吸引游客。学生作为自己城市展台的志愿工作者，将要发表一场演讲介绍自己城市的特点、历史、传统、发展等。

↓

**Active reading**（The beautiful blue Danube）
阅读音乐之城维也纳的相关故事，了解这一古典乐之都的特色与成因，认识城市与人文特点之间的关系。

→ **Further exploration – Text**（Wuzhen: Past and present）
阅读关于中国水乡古镇乌镇的介绍，了解其历史底蕴，以及当代文化、科技方面的发展，学习从历史与当今发展双视角推介一个城市。

→ **Further exploration – Video**（How to make an attractive city?）
观看视频，了解从城市规划的角度增加城市魅力的基本原则，探索挖掘城市吸引力的途径。

↓

**Project**
了解自己的城市，思考其吸引人的主要特色，选定推介的核心要点，讲好家乡故事。

↓

**Extended reading**（Cities: Past and future）
阅读关于城市发展的说明文，理解城市的成因，分析未来城市的发展方向。

↓

**Self-reflection**
回顾整个单元的学习，反思在内容、语言、结构、交际和行为上的收获。

↓

深入了解和理解自己的城市，讲述家乡故事，将中国介绍给世界。
