## Scenario

The scenario in this unit can be analyzed from the following aspects:
**Setting:** Participating in a city exposition at the World Culture and Tourism Forum
**Topic:** My city
**Identity:** Speaker – A volunteer at the exposition
Audience – Visitors to the exposition
**Purpose:** To inform the audience of a city and persuade them to visit the city

**Teaching suggestions**
T can help Ss generate ideas by asking three questions: What are the impressive characteristics of your city? What is the focus of your introduction? What should be included with that focus? For example, some may find their cities distinguishable with a long history and a rich historical heritage. They may decide to focus on the importance of their cities in Chinese history and the attractions that can help visitors relive the glorious times. They can make presentations in groups and comment on how attractive each other's introduction turns out to be.

After the tryout, T can ask Ss to reflect on their difficulties in four aspects, i.e., content (what they can produce for introducing their cities and what is lacking), language (the expressions and sentence structures they have used and feel hard to use), structure (how to organize their presentation) and communication (how to use visual aids to help with their speech), and write them down in the table in the Student's Book. T can also briefly analyze the most challenging aspects based on Ss' reflection.

## Active reading

In *Scenario*, Ss are expected to introduce a city and a very important step is to identify the characteristics of the target city. The text introduces the city of Vienna with a focus on it being the center of classical music. It starts with the description of the most well-known waltz *The Blue Danube*, which leads to the introduction to the spirit of the people as reflected by this masterpiece and many other famous musicians related to this city. It also analyzes what is special about the city that contributes to its distinguished position in music. Ss can learn the writing technique of highlighting features when introducing a city.

### Before you read

**Teaching suggestions**
This is an activity concerning the associated meaning of the title. It should be finished before Ss begin to read the text. The title will trigger some background knowledge from Ss, which would help with their understanding of the text. All four selections are related to the title in one way or another and will be approached by the text. T can invite Ss to briefly share what they know about each of the four selections.

**Reference answers**
When I look at the title "The Beautiful Blue Danube," the first thing that comes to mind is the famous river known as the Danube. One reason is that the title mentions the specific name of the river, "Danube." This triggers my knowledge and familiarity with the Danube River, as it is one of the longest and most well-known rivers in Europe. Additionally, the adjective "beautiful" in the title further strengthens the association with the Danube. This adjective suggests that the article might explore the scenic and picturesque aspects of the river, reinforcing the connection between the title and the actual river.

When I look at the title "The Beautiful Blue Danube," the first thing that comes to mind is actually a famous musical composition called "The Blue Danube" composed by Johann Strauss the Younger. The reason for this association is because "The Blue Danube" is a widely recognized waltz that has become immensely popular. The waltz features a serene and melodic tune that evokes images of a flowing river and captures the beauty and grace associated with the Danube. Furthermore, the use of the word "beautiful" in the title further reinforces the connection to the musical piece, as it aligns with the sentiment and emotions typically associated with the waltz.

When I look at the title "The Beautiful Blue Danube," the first thing that comes to mind is "Austria." The Danube River flows through Austria, and Vienna, the capital city, which is known for its rich musical heritage and as a hub for classical music.

When I see the title "The Beautiful Blue Danube," the first thing that comes to my mind is Johann Strauss the Younger, the famous composer. Because "The Blue Danube" is one of Johann Strauss the Younger's most well-known compositions. It is a waltz that has become iconic and is strongly associated with the Danube River.

## The beautiful blue Danube

### Text interpretation

本单元介绍了音乐之城维也纳。不同于惯常的介绍性文章那样开头就简述城市的基本信息，本文从一首耳熟能详的圆舞曲《蓝色多瑙河》切入，引出维也纳在古典乐发展中的重要作用、音乐之都的主要成因，以及直至今日在乐迷心中的地位，从而丰富多维度地展现了维也纳这座城市最突出的特征——音乐之城。

文章第一部分（第一至第二段）描述了与维也纳联系最紧密的乐曲《蓝色多瑙河》及其在西方古典乐中举足轻重的地位。第二部分（第三至第五段）讲述了《蓝色多瑙河》从创作到举世闻名的过程，并解释了乐曲如何充分体现维也纳人的个性特点。此外，它作为一种根深蒂固的传统，已深深融入了维也纳人的文化生活之中。第三部分（第六至第八段）进一步展现维也纳这座城市在古典乐发展史上不可替代的地位，探讨其古典乐中心的成因。文章的结尾（第九段）将读者从历史拉回到今天，介绍维也纳音乐传统对现今的深刻影响，并呼应开头，以美丽的蓝色多瑙河比拟人们对维也纳这座城市的畅想。

文章以优美的笔触，采用描写、记叙与议论结合的多维写作方式，将维也纳的音乐特性表达得淋漓尽致，令读者不禁心生向往。本文最突出的特点是，它并不试图全面而平铺直叙地描述一座城市，而是针对城市最显著的特性进行深入剖析。通过具体的事例（一首全球知名的乐曲），文章进行了深入的探讨，充分描绘和渲染了维也纳的特点。教学中可以结合理解练习2，引导学生分析在使用具体事例时如何选取最相关的细节，以及如何通过事例说明所要展现的城市特点。

在学习课文的同时，教师可以引入隐性课程思政，引导学生参考维也纳的音乐性特征深入挖掘自己家乡城市的文化内涵、精神载体，思考城市历史文化的保护传承：维也纳的最典型特征是其古典乐发展中的重要作用，你家乡城市最典型的城市文化特点是什么？《蓝色多瑙河》是维也纳音乐性的代表作，你家乡的文化特点最具代表性的象征是什么？维也纳的音乐传统对今天仍有深刻影响，游客仍可沉浸其中，你家乡的文化特点是如何传承发展的？在一系列的问题讨论后，可以结合本单元开头配图，探讨城市的历史传承与现代化发展。

### Photo interpretation

本单元开头配图是湖北省武汉市的照片。作为一座有着丰富历史遗迹和故事的现代化大都市，武汉的古今对话在这张图片中表露无遗。"天下第一楼"黄鹤楼与鳞次栉比的摩天大楼、现代化的电视高塔（曾经是亚洲第一高塔）隔江相望，中间流向天际的长江见证了历史上无数风流人物、重大事件，以及这座城市的风貌变迁与蓬勃发展。教师可以设计以下问题，引导学生基于图片思考、讨论城市的历史传承与现代化发展：
1) What scenes in the picture have caught your attention?
2) What historical significance do you know about Wuhan?
3) What modernization development of this city do you know?
4) What do you think should be done to both protect the history and enhance modernization development of a city?

### Language points

1 **The famous song perfectly summarizes the longing to visit this most musical of cities that is felt by so many. (Para. 1)**

**Notes:** Here, "that is felt by so many" is used to emphasize that the longing to visit the city is experienced by a large number of people. It implies that the desire to visit the musical city is not limited to just a few individuals, but is widespread and shared by many.

2 **However, few of us have heard the piece as it was originally intended. (Para. 2)**

**Notes:** This sentence is an effective transition. Although "The Blue Danube" is a masterpiece familiar to most people, it is rare that people know what it was originally like. The following paragraph will therefore talk about its origin. In addition, the contrast between what you know and don't know can well arouse the readers' interest.

3 **Strauss was commissioned to write a choral work by the Vienna Men's Choral Society in 1865, but the composer couldn't start work because of other commitments. (Para. 3)**

**commission**: *vt.* formally ask sb. to write, make, or create sth. or to do a task for you 正式委托（谱写、制作、创作或完成）

*The government commissioned a team of scientists to conduct research on renewable energy sources.*

**commitment**: *n.* [C] sth. that you have promised you will do or that you have to do 承诺的事

*Many parents do not get involved in schools because they have too many other commitments.*

**Notes:** Here "the composer" refers to Strauss mentioned in the first clause of the sentence. This is a strategy to connect the two clauses without repetition.

4 **He wanted to create a musical piece expressing his attachment to them and took his work's title from a line in a poem ... (Para. 3)**

**attachment**: *n.* [C, U] a feeling that you like or love sb. or sth. and that you would be unhappy without them 喜爱；爱慕；依恋

*Sarah has a strong attachment to her childhood home and often visits it to relive cherished memories.*

5 **But when Strauss adapted it into an orchestral version and introduced it to the world later that year in Paris, it caused a sensation. (Para. 4)**

**sensation**: *n.* [C, usu. sing.] extreme excitement or interest, or sb. or sth. that causes it 轰动；激动；引起轰动的人（事）

*Out of everyone's expectations, the series created by an unknown designer became a sensation overnight.*

**Notes:** Verbs that often go before "sensation" are "cause," "create," etc.

6 **The tune's joyful melody, possibly the catchiest piece of classical music ever written, won over the public. (Para. 5)**

**catchy**: *a.* a catchy tune or phrase is easy to remember （曲调或话语）悦耳易记的

*The catchy chorus of the song had the crowd singing along and dancing at the concert.*

**win sb. over**: get sb.'s support or friendship by persuading them or being nice to them （通过劝说或示好以）获得（某人的支持或友谊）；把（某人）争取过来

*Both presidential candidates are busy trying to win over this class of voters given the importance of the states where they reside.*

7 **The music remains deeply rooted in Viennese life and invariably features in any classical musical performance in the city. (Para. 5)**

**rooted in sth.**: developing from or being strongly influenced by sth. 根源于；由……产生

*Even the most enlightened beings who have inhabited our world have provided explanations rooted in their cultural experiences.*

**feature**: *vi.* include or show sth. as a special or important part of sth. else, or be included as an important part 是……的特征；是……的重要组成部分

*Jazz and Blues feature prominently in the magazine for music lovers.*

8 **As the chimes of midnight fade away on New Year's Day across Austria's capital, ... (Para. 5)**

**fade away**: gradually disappear 逐渐消失

*After a sleepless night of discussion and debate, the last of his doubts had faded away.*

9 **... members of the aristocratic court of the time saw it as their duty to encourage and support musicians. (Para. 7)**

**Meaning:** People from the royal court and of a higher social class believed that they had the responsibility to encourage and support musicians.

10 **In addition, Vienna's unique setting served as a source of inspiration for its creative people. (Para. 8)**

**Notes:** Here "served as a source of" is used to explain a factor that makes Vienna a place for classical music to develop. A number of expressions are used for this purpose in Paragraphs 7 and 8, including "play a role," "contribute to," and "have a significant effect on."

11 **Today, visitors to Vienna can enjoy its musical legacy by taking in a concert at one of the numerous music venues both traditional and modern. (Para. 9)**

**take in sth.**: go to see sth., such as a movie, play, etc. 观看（电影、戏剧等）

*Sarah decided to take in the movie at the local theater to relax and escape from her busy schedule for a few hours.*

12 **... delighting listeners and allowing them to remember the glorious age of classical music. (Para. 9)**

**delight**:
*vi.* give sb. great satisfaction and enjoyment 使愉快；使高兴
*We are delighted to have you as a guest author and submit posts to share your experience.*
*n.* [U] a feeling of great pleasure and satisfaction 高兴；快乐
*Being smart and optimistic, every single member of his family was a delight to the neighbors.*

13 **For them, the Danube is an unmissable attraction partly because of its lasting connection with Strauss's most famous work. (Para. 9)**

**Notes:** Here "unmissable" is used to describe how important the attraction is to tourists. We can also use the verb form of "miss" and say "It is something you shouldn't miss."

14 **It matters not how many other cities the river flows through, nor how far from blue the water actually is. In people's minds the world over, as the tender melody plays, the Danube becomes forever a river of dreams in Vienna, shining with history and possibility. (Para. 9)**

**Notes:** Initially, the author disregards the concrete, physical attributes of the Danube, such as its actual color or the number of cities it traverses. Following this, the narrative shifts as the river surpasses its objective qualities and evolves into a symbol of dreams, historical significance, and potential. This evolution is predominantly propelled by its connection to Vienna and the evoking melody associated with it. Essentially, the author portrays a transformation of the Danube from its real, tangible existence to a deeper and more symbolic interpretation.

### Getting the ideas

**Reference answers**
1

**Teaching suggestions**
This activity is a matching task that aims to help Ss grasp the main ideas of the text. It requires Ss to locate the relevant paragraphs and sentences where the correspondent message has been conveyed. A related task can be developed for working out the logical development / organization of the main ideas. T can ask Ss to summarize the main idea of each paragraph and tell which paragraphs form a section to express a more general idea. Text interpretation can be T's reference.

1 c
2 d
3 e
4 b
5 a

2

**Facts:**
1) the country's mood
2) attachment
3) a line in a poem
4) choral piece
5) a sensation

**Influences:**
1) a masterpiece
2) across the world
3) style of music
4) national anthem
5) classical musical performance
6) personality

3

1 During the 18th and 19th centuries, a multitude of esteemed composers and musicians were drawn to Vienna with the ambition of honing their skills and establishing their renown, resulting in this timeframe being recognized as the Viennese Classical Period. Other than Strauss, some of the most distinguished composers who resided in the city include Beethoven, Mozart, and Haydn. These maestros composed many of their noteworthy pieces in Vienna itself, thereby reinforcing the city's stature as a hub for classical music.

2

Vienna developed into a center of classical music basically for the following reasons:
• Its political influence may have played a role, as it was the capital of the Austro-Hungarian Empire from 1867 and of the Austrian Empire before that.
• The city's well-informed and educated audiences may also have contributed to its success, as members of the aristocratic court of the time saw it as their duty to encourage and support musicians.
• Vienna's social atmosphere also had a significant effect on the musicians there. The composer Schubert, for example, was part of a group that met in the city's atmospheric cafés to discuss art and music. Brahms was influenced by the folk music in local coffee houses.
• Vienna's unique setting served as a source of inspiration for its creative people. The city, with hills around it and parks in it, has a natural beauty that many artists have loved. The scenery had an emotional influence that further motivated the famous composers to create their masterpieces.
In my eyes, the social atmosphere is the most important reason. Art needs a breeding ground and when the social atmosphere is very suitable for artists to communicate and inspire one another, more original works will be created. As a result, more artists will be attracted there, which will help further enhance a more productive social atmosphere.

### Analyzing writing techniques

**Reference answers**
1) About a well-known example

Merely hearing the name of the city evokes the melodies of waltzes, the best known of which is surely "The Blue Danube."

It is an unofficial national anthem in Austria and always marks the conclusion of Vienna New Year's Concert.

For people across the world, even those of us with no interest in this style of music, its rhythm is instantly recognizable.

Strauss had always loved his city and its river, the Danube. He wanted to create a musical piece expressing his attachment to them.

The tune's joyful melody, possibly the catchiest piece of classical music ever written, won over the public. It captured the carefree personality of Vienna.

It has become a famous part of Austrian musical history and an enduring symbol of Vienna.

The music remains deeply rooted in Viennese life and invariably features in any classical musical performance in the city.

2) **About the historical influence**

The city has a rich and productive musical history that few others can rival.

Classical music is so strongly related to Vienna because of its status as a musical hub in the 18th and 19th centuries.

Many famous composers and musicians flocked to the city at this time in order to build their reputations and learn from other masters, leading this era to be known as the Viennese Classical Period.

Beethoven, Mozart, and Haydn are the most prestigious of the many composers who lived in the city and composed many of their remarkable works there, strengthening Vienna's reputation as a classical music hot spot.

3) **About the present-day position**

Today, visitors to Vienna can enjoy its musical legacy by taking in a concert at one of the numerous traditional and modern music venues.

Every night, musical performances take place at a great many stages across the city, delighting listeners and allowing them to remember the glorious age of classical music.

Crowds of tourists are drawn to this center of culture, and many of the music lovers.

The famous song perfectly summarizes the longing to visit this most musical of cities that is felt by so many.

### Building your language

**Reference answers**

2 create masterpieces
3 (a) strong attachment
4 flock to
5 delight visitors

3 The well-known story can be traced back hundreds of years and has been associated with the city's lake ever since.
4 Seen from above, the vast sea is decorated with islands that look just like pearls on a silver plate.

Located in southwest China, Chongqing is a city that played a significant role in history, with its Bayu culture dating back more than 3,000 years. It is a highly recognizable city. Being surrounded by mountains, Chongqing is known as Mountain City. It is also rich in water. The Yangtze River flows through the city and the magnificent scenery along its banks showcases the charm of nature, which deeply touches the tourists and has served as a source of inspiration for the literati to create numerous masterpieces. Today, shining with history, Chongqing is undergoing rapid development towards the future.

**Reference answers**
"Eating in Guangzhou"（食在广州）may be one of the first phrases that comes to mind when one mentions Guangzhou. The saying dates back to the late Qing Dynasty. However, the practice of investing in the refinement of food delicacy started quite early, with notable advancements occurring in the 16th century during the Ming Dynasty. During this period, Guangzhou established itself as a significant trading port. Cuisine development accompanied the business boom. Cantonese cuisine has gained its fame. It attaches great importance to mouth-feel and is famous for the taste of lightness, freshness, crispiness, and smoothness. In addition to being the birthplace of Cantonese cuisine, Guangzhou is also a melting pot of diverse and well-established cuisines. It is recognized as a city of gastronomy and has attracted visitors from all over the world. Here they can enjoy food both at time-honored restaurants with a history of more than 100 years and at popular food courts with trendy fusion styles.

## Further exploration

The task in *Scenario* asks Ss to give a presentation speech introducing their own cities. The text in this part provides a model of introducing a place by describing both its historical features and contemporary development. The video presents the basic principles to follow for a city to become attractive, which can provide Ss with certain perspectives when trying to dig out the attractive characteristics of their cities. T can compare the text in *Active reading* and the text in *Further exploration* in order to help Ss learn the different ways of introducing a city: to focus on one characteristic and to present features both from history legacy and from modern development. T can also encourage Ss to analyze their own cities by referring to the principles introduced in the video.

## Wuzhen: Past and present (Text)

### Language points

1 **It has retained many features of its long history, including both physical details and folk customs. (Para. 2)**

**Meaning:** Many characteristics developed over the long history have still been kept in Wuzhen. These include both material cultural heritage and nonmaterial cultural heritage such as folk customs.

**Notes:** "Retain" and "sustain" are both verbs that relate to maintaining or preserving something, but they have slightly different meanings and contexts. When we use "retain," we typically mean to keep or hold onto something, often for an extended period. It implies the act of preserving or not allowing something to be lost or taken away, e.g., "retain information / customers / control, etc." "Sustain" refers to the act of supporting, upholding, or keeping something in existence or functioning properly. It often implies the idea of providing necessary resources or the continuation of a process or condition. For example, "sustain livelihood / growth / energy, etc."

2 **Visitors walking around the town may feel as though they have traveled back to a simpler age. (Para. 3)**

**Notes:** Here "a simpler age" is used to refer to some time in history, especially ancient times, when people lived in a simpler way. This is an indirect way to say that classical construction styles are prevailing in Wuzhen so that it can cause people to feel like they are in a historical period.

3 **The old houses, supported by ancient beams and painted pillars, and topped with tiled roofs, look like details in an ink drawing. (Para. 3)**

**Notes:** "Details in an ink drawing" is used to describe the images of old houses in a vivid way. Such descriptions can help readers to visualize the scenes without truly seeing them.

4 **While Wuzhen is deeply steeped in history, it is far from being an open-air museum. (Para. 4)**

**Meaning:** Although Wuzhen has a rich historical background, it goes beyond being a mere outdoor museum.

**be steeped in history / tradition / politics, etc.**: have a lot of a particular quality 有悠久的历史，根植于传统，深陷政治等

*The Great Wall of China is a monumental structure that is steeped in history, serving as a symbol of China's rich past and engineering marvels.*

**Notes:** The phrase "an open-air museum" is used to refer to a place whose only function is to exhibit historical and cultural heritage. By pointing out that Wuzhen is not an open-air museum, this sentence leads to the following introduction to its contemporary development.

5 **The annual Wuzhen Theater Festival is one of the town's many cultural festivals, and has become a phenomenon in China. (Para. 5)**

**phenomenon**: *n.* [C] a person or thing that is very successful or impressive 杰出的人；非凡的人（事物）

*The professor's lecture has become a phenomenon, as has the book he wrote based on the same theory.*

**Notes:** When something is described as a "phenomenon," it suggests that it has captured the interest and fascination of many people, creating a significant impact or influence. This sentence indicates that the Wuzhen Theater Festival has gained a reputation and popularity that extends beyond the local town and has become renowned on a national scale.

6 **These demonstrate Wuzhen's technology-centered approach to tourism, public affairs, and daily life. (Para. 6)**

**approach**: *n.* [C] a way of dealing with sb. or sth.; a way of doing or thinking about sth., such as a problem or a task （待人接物或思考问题的）方式，方法，态度

*The representative proposed a number of approaches to the pressing problem.*

**Notes:** It should be noted that "approach to" should be followed by a noun or a gerund. The possible mistake of saying "an approach to do sth." should be guarded against.

7 **With its fusion of traditional beauty and cutting-edge development, Wuzhen is a one-of-a-kind attraction. (Para. 8)**

**Meaning:** In Wuzhen you can both appreciate heritage from history and observe achievements from advanced technology development. By combining these two, Wuzhen has become a unique place attracting visitors.

**one-of-a-kind**: *a.* you use one-of-a-kind to describe sth. that is special because there is nothing else exactly like it 独特的；独一无二的

*This antique shop is a one-of-a-kind store, offering unique and rare treasures that you won't find anywhere else.*

**Reference answers**
1
1) triangle
2) Charming Chinese Towns
3) National Tourist Attraction
4) long history
5) Jiangnan-style residences
6) a phenomenon

7) cultural centers

2
1 It helps readers visualize the scenery and the beauty of the place using descriptions of sounds, colors, and shapes.
2 Local and international plays, competitions, and panel discussions in various elegant venues and pop-up street performances are included in the annual Wuzhen Theatre Festival.
3 Wuzhen benefits from developments in technology, such as the intelligent facilities for the elderly, information booths, and public bikes. It has developed an intelligent hi-tech approach to tourism, public affairs, and people's daily lives.
4 On the one hand, Wuzhen has Jiangnan-style residences, which date back to the Ming and Qing dynasties. Visitors can enjoy the traditional beauty by walking around the town appreciating hand-paddled wooden boats creaking along the canals under stone bridges, and the old houses supported by ancient beams and painted pillars, and topped with tiled roofs. On the other hand, Wuzhen has developed to be a center of contemporary culture and latest technology. The annual Wuzhen Theater Festival has become a phenomenon in China, attracting global performers. Wuzhen is also the permanent site for the annual World Internet Conference and has made a name in the field of technology. Therefore, it combines the beauty of both tradition and modern development and demonstrates the glamour of past and present.

## How to make an attractive city (Video)

### Scripts

1 With a growing urban population, it's important that cities are planned correctly.

2 First, cities should be neither too chaotic nor too ordered. People like order. Order means balance, symmetry, and repetition. If a city is chaotic, it seems a mess. There is no order: a pitched roof next to a flat roof, high-rise towers placed here and there like teeth in an open mouth. However, excessive order can be a problem. Too much order feels dull and charmless. What an ideal city needs is variety and order. This is the idea behind a square in Telč in the Czech Republic, where every house is the same width and height, but within that ordered pattern, every house has been allowed freedom in its form and color.

3 Second, cities need to show signs of life. People are attracted to busy streets where they can see residents going about their lives and enjoying themselves. The most popular European cities have a strong café culture, where people are content to sit outside and watch the comings and goings of people as they enjoy their coffee. This is a lively scene in Venice. In the 18th century, the painter Canaletto specialized in painting pictures of cities, which were popular because the cities were full of life.

4 Finally, cities should be local and unique. Although we don't expect there to be a uniquely Venezuelan telephone or a distinctively Icelandic bicycle, we don't want every city in each country or around the world to look the same. It's disappointing to travel hours away from home to find you could be anywhere. Cities need to have strong characters connected to the use of distinctive local materials and forms. How a city looks, feels, and works should reflect the culture, climate, and lifestyle of its people. This makes a city unique and full of charm.

5 The principles above may be easy to present but actually require a great deal of effort to digest and carry out. As a matter of fact, very few cities out of many thousands are really beautiful. We can create more attractive cities, but we need to develop a better understanding of the principles behind this process, and put them in action.

### Language points

1 However, excessive order can be a problem. (Para. 2)

**excessive**: *a.* greater than what seems reasonable or appropriate 过分的；过度的

*The book was published in 1960 to criticize the excessive use of chemicals at that time.*

2 People are attracted to busy streets where they can see residents going about their lives and enjoying themselves. (Para. 3)

**go about**: do sth. in the way that you usually do it （以通常的方式）做某事

*His supervisor pointed out that he was not going about the project in the right way.*

3 ... where people are content to sit outside and watch the comings and goings of people as they enjoy their coffee. (Para. 3)

**Notes:** "They" and "their" refer to the first "people" – people who are sitting outside.

4 In the 18th century, the painter Canaletto specialized in painting pictures of cities, ... (Para. 3)

**specialize**: *vi.* limit all or most of your study, business, etc. to a particular subject or activity 专门研究；专门从事；专攻

*I know more than one person who figured out too late what they wanted to specialize in.*

5 It's disappointing to travel hours away from home to find you could be anywhere. (Para. 4)

**Meaning:** Visitors will be disappointed if they go to a place very far away from their home city and only find that there is nothing unique or characteristic.

6 The principles above may be easy to present but actually require a great deal of effort to digest and carry out. (Para. 5)

**Notes:** There is a contrast between "to present" and "to carry out." The sentence argues that it's easy to introduce the principles as this video does, but it is more demanding that we truly understand such principles and follow them in practice.

**Reference answers**

1

Principle 1
ordered
a, e

Principle 2
life
c

Principle 3

unique

2
1) variety
2) form and color
3) comings and goings
4) full of life

**Additional activity**

The video introduces three principles to follow for a city to be attractive. T could also invite Ss to think what other factors may help a city attract visitors. Although Ss are supposed to introduce a city as required in the scenario, switching their perspective from a presenter to a visitor could be of some help. Ss may think about and discuss the following questions:

To take a traveler's perspective, what would attract you to a city?

**Reference answers**

The enchanting folk tales or myths in a city could be very attractive. Both traditional myths and urban myths will do.

For example, there is this Foolish Old Man, or Yugong in a traditional Chinese fable. The story takes place in Jiyuan City in the east part of China. Two huge mountains lie directly in front of the Yugong's house. The Foolish Old Man, who is almost 90 years old, has to take a long detour whenever he goes out. To get rid of this inconvenience, he leads his sons and grandsons in chipping away at the mountains year after year. They will not give up despite doubts and jeers from neighbors, and eventually move the Immortal, who sends heavenly spirits to move the mountains away. The story contains the underlying message about courage and perseverance in the face of challenge and has gained increasing appreciation over the years. The "Foolish Old Man Who Moved the Mountains" has become a synonym for forging ahead in the face of difficulties and persevering to the very end.

There is also the well-known urban myth about London. London has the most extensive network of underground tunnels in the world. But for some inhabitants, the tunnels are more than just convenient – they live in them. The London Subterraneans are a race of people who live beneath the streets. They're human, but they don't speak English, and they have their own customs. They only appear at night through the drains in dark backstreets, and if they hear footsteps, they quickly hide, so very few Londoners have seen them. But this myth may create a sense of mystery and attract visitors to explore.

### Sharing your ideas

**Reference answers**

• One remarkable feature of Xi'an related to its development and planning is the preservation of its ancient city walls. In the past, Xi'an was a capital city of several dynasties surrounded by massive and

（页底截断，接下页）

robust defensive walls. These walls stood as a symbol of power and provided protection for the city and its inhabitants.

• In modern times, Xi'an has retained and beautifully restored a significant portion of the city walls, creating a unique blend of historical charm and modern functionality. The walls now serve as a prominent tourist attraction and a recreational space for local residents and visitors alike. The city has also implemented a well-planned transportation system that allows people to cycle or walk along the walls, providing a panoramic view of the cityscape.

• This preservation effort not only showcases Xi'an's rich history but also demonstrates its commitment to urban planning and heritage conservation. The combination of ancient walls with modern amenities and recreational spaces adds an attractive element to the city's planning. It invites people to experience the grandeur of the past while enjoying the conveniences of the present, making Xi'an an enchanting destination for history enthusiasts and urban explorers.

**Suggestions for evaluation**

For this activity, Ss' discussion can be evaluated according to the following criteria:

• whether Ss can clearly state the attractive features of their city and fully illustrate with some details
• whether Ss can clearly state the city's development and planning

## Project

**Sample**

(*Words and expressions in red are from the input materials.)

If you are interested in ancient cities with a long history and splendid culture, you may already know some famous cities in China such as Beijing and Xi'an. However, there is a city that will satisfy every longing to be attached to ancient beauty but is not so popularly mentioned.

Lying in the west of Henan Province, Luoyang was the capital city for the longest period, the most dynasties, and the earliest time compared with the other ancient capital cities in China. It is located in the Central Plain surrounded by mountains, which were natural barriers against invasions. Apart from its favorable geographical location, Luoyang had an agricultural advantage as several rivers flow through it. Therefore, 13 dynasties set their capitals in Luoyang in China's history. Luoyang was a center of politics, economy, and culture in China for a total of over 1,500 years, which left rich world heritages.

One that you can't miss is Longmen Grottoes, comprising more than 2,300 caves and niches carved into the steep limestone cliffs over a 1-kilometer-long stretch. The largest Buddha statue stands 17.14 meters high with 2-meter-long ears. The grottoes and niches of Longmen present the largest and most impressive collection of Chinese art of the late Northern Wei and Tang Dynasties, representing the high point of Chinese stone carving.

A starting point of the ancient Silk Road, Luoyang once stretched its cultural and commercial reach from the Yellow River to the Golden Horn. An unprecedented connection between the East and the West was established in Luoyang 1,854 years ago when the first Roman Empire envoy visited the city and was captivated by its thriving international trade and vibrant fusion of culture.

Millenniums later, Luoyang still retains many features of its long history. Its world-class museums and leading cultural industries are attracting visitors from all over the world. Dubbed "the oriental capital of museums," Luoyang has more than 100 museums. In the exhibition hall of the Luoyang Museum, advanced technologies and cultural innovation have injected a few raisins of modernity into the dough of history. Visitors can study ancient artworks by using 3D screens and VR glasses.

In addition to museums, Luoyang has also breathed new life into ancient history through its themed cultural festival and tourism activities. The 1,500-year history of peony cultivation has made Luoyang a unique and distinctive city among many ancient capitals. The Peony Cultural Festival has become a phenomenon. The annual festival was established to celebrate the blossom of the beautiful national flower of China. Aside from flower exhibitions, there are various folk performances staged in the gardens. Local artists also use ancient porcelain-making techniques to create modern peony-themed decorations, which can serve as China's national gift to foreign leaders as well as trendy jewelry.

Luoyang is a city where you can hear stories about 13 dynasties, observe masterpieces of stone carving from 1500 years ago, and appreciate the grace of China's national flower. Come and be drowned in the ancient beauty of modern times.

### Suggestions for evaluation

| Components | Criteria / Traits |
| --- | --- |
| **Content** | • Relevance: Addressing the assigned task by introducing a city that focuses on a prominent characteristic of the city and its development and planning<br>• Appeal: Including relevant messages, using visual aids effectively, being informative and persuasive |
| **Organization** | • Structure: Organizing ideas in chronological / spatial / topical order based on appropriate focus of the speech<br>• Coherence: Using effective sign-post expressions according to the structure<br>• Clarity: Arranging the points with a clear logic<br>• Technique: Highlighting a characteristic of the city by establishing a strong association between them |
| **Language** | • Diversity: Using an adequate range of vocabulary and sentence structures<br>• Acquisition: Using as many as possible of the new words, expressions and grammatical structures learned in the unit |
| **Delivery** | • Fluency: Making the presentation as fluently as possible<br>• Engagement: Using tones appropriately to attract audiences<br>• Visual aids: Using suitable visual methods, such as images, videos, and objects and models to help the audience better understand the attractiveness of the city |

## Extended reading

In the previous parts, Ss have learned to introduce a city by selecting a focus. They have been presented with different approaches, including emphasizing a single characteristic of the city, demonstrating features both from history legacy and from modern development, and working on attractive characteristics by referring to some principles. In this part, Ss will explore an essay that traces the evolution of urban cities and discusses the prospective paths for urban growth. It helps Ss further explore the topic of this unit by reflecting on the origin of cities, the factors contributing to their development, and how to solve some problems for future urban life. Ss can be guided to think about cities not just as individual existence but as a lifestyle for mankind and develop insights on how human beings can strive for a better future in general.

## Cities: Past and future

### Language points

1 In the early days of human history, humans were hunter-gatherers, often moving from place to place in search of food. (Para. 2)

**hunter-gatherer**: *n.* [C] a member of a group of people who live by hunting and looking for plants that can be eaten, rather than by keeping animals for food or by growing crops 采猎者（以打猎和采集植物为生的人）

*Until recently, people in this area retained a hunter-gatherer lifestyle, foraging in the forests for food and eating wild meat.*

2 For the first time, people could raise food rather than search for it, and this led to the development of semi-permanent villages. (Para. 2)

**semi-permanent**: *a.* relating to or extending over a relatively long time 半永久性的

*They finally arrived at the coast where they had semi-permanent settlements.*

**Notes:** The prefix "semi-" means "half or not totally." It can be added to some words to form new ones, such as "semicircle," "semiautomatic," "semicommercial," and "semimature."

3 This allowed the development of other specialized trades, and, by extension, cities. (Para. 2)

**by extension**: used when you want to mention sth. that is naturally related to sth. else 自然地；当然地

*The advancements in manufacturing and production during the Industrial Revolution paved the way for urbanization and, by extension, the development of modern cities.*

**Notes:** The preposition "of" is followed by two objects in this sentence, one is "other specialized trades," and the other is "cities."

4 And as trade flourished, so did technologies and infrastructures that facilitated it, like carts, ships, roads, and ports. (Para. 3)

**Meaning:** As commerce or trade thrived, the technologies and infrastructures that supported and made this trade possible also developed and flourished. These supportive elements include things like carts and ships (used for transporting goods), and roads and ports (that provided the necessary route and location

（页底截断，接下页）

for the exchange of goods).

**Notes:** The structure "so did ..." is used to mean technologies and infrastructures flourished, too. The structure can connect the two clauses while avoiding repetition. It also helps to emphasize the correlation between trade, technologies, and infrastructures, highlighting the interconnected nature of their growth and development.

5 ... some cities in 2,000 B.C. had population densities nearly twice as high as that of modern Shanghai. (Para. 4)

**Meaning:** The population density of some cities in 2,000 B.C. doubled the population density of contemporary Shanghai.

6 And the land area of the city was further restricted by the need for walls to defend against attacks. (Para. 4)

**restrict**: *vt.* limit or control the size, amount, or range of sth. 限制，控制（大小、数量或）

*For efficiency, let's restrict attention to the issue under discussion for today's meeting.*

**Notes:** The noun form of "restrict" is "restriction," meaning a rule or law that limits or controls what people can do. For example:

*It is not surprising that the general public warmly welcomes the increased restriction placed on smoking.*

7 ... most modern cities didn't really get their start until the Industrial Revolution, when new technology allowed cities to expand and integrate further. (Para. 4)

**Meaning:** Most modern cities only truly started after the Industrial Revolution. During this period, new technology provided the condition for cities to grow larger and combine with one another.

8 The cities of 2045 will be home to more than six billion of us. (Para. 5)

**Meaning:** In 2045, there will be more than six billion people living in cities.

**be home to sth.**: be the place where a person, animal, or plant lives 是……的居住地；是……的产地；是……的生长（栖息）地

*Paris was home to some of this century's most important artists.*

9 So how will cities need to change to accommodate this growth? (Para. 6)

**accommodate**: *vt.*

1) if a room, building, etc. can accommodate a particular number of people or things, it has enough space for them （房间、建筑物等）容纳

*According to the expansion plan, the county will accommodate a projected population of over 7 million by 2030.*

2) accept sb.'s opinions and try to do what they want, esp. when their opinions or needs are different from yours 迎合；迁就

*Please notice that our proposal has evolved to accommodate a bigger vision.*

**Notes:**

1) The noun form of "accommodate" can be "accommodation (esp. BrE)" and "accommodations (AmE)," meaning a place for someone to stay, live, or work. For example:

*The council promised to place more homeless people into private rented accommodation.*

2) The noun "accommodation" can also mean an agreement between people or groups who have different views or opinions, that satisfies everyone. For example:

*The leaders are working very hard to seek a new accommodation between the two parties.*

10 ... towering skyscrapers discourage a sense of community and promote anonymity. (Para. 6)

**Meaning:** Very tall buildings make people living there rarely know one another and do not help to form a sense of community.

11 In a hotel project, the green area within the building is 200 percent of the ground area covered. (Para. 7)

**Meaning:** In a hotel project, lots of plants are grown in the building. Compared with the ground area covered by the building, the total area covered by plants in the building is twice as large.

12 As a result, the building is very different to most. (Para. 7)

**Notes:** The three prepositions typically follow "different" are "from," "to," and "than." The phrase "different from" is the most commonly used structure in both British English and American English. The phrase "different to" is also used in British English. In American English, it is common to hear people say "different than."

13 In addition, experts also aim to create self-sufficient cities – generators of food, energy, and water. (Para. 8)

**Meaning:** Besides, experts also want to make cities capable of producing such necessities as food, energy, and water by themselves.

**self-sufficient**: *a.* able to provide all the things you need without help from other people 自给自足的

*The local construction industry is self-sufficient with abundant supplies of stone, cement, sand, and lime.*

**Reference answers**

a-2
b-3
c-8
d-4
e-7
f-2
g-5
h-6
i-7
j-5

## Self-reflection

In this part, T can ask Ss to refer back to *Try it out* in the Student's Book and reflect on what they have learned to complete the task. Ss can categorize their gains according to the same four aspects, i.e., content, language, structure, and communication. They can also reflect on how learning the unit may influence their view on cities and their action in daily life. Then they can write down their specific gains in the self-reflection table in the Student's Book.

## Translation of the texts

## Active reading

美丽的蓝色多瑙河

1 "维也纳，只有你，你是唯一，维也纳，我的梦中之城……"这首著名的歌曲完美地概括了人们对参观这座音乐之城的渴望，这种渴望很多人都很有。对于不少音乐爱好者来说，哪怕只是听到这个城市的名字，耳边都会响起华尔兹的旋律，这其中最出名的一首就是《蓝色多瑙河》，它是由维也纳最受欢迎的音乐之子小约翰·施特劳斯创作的曲子。

2 时至今日，施特劳斯的这首宏伟巨作成为公认的西方古典音乐杰作。它既是奥地利的一首非正式国歌，也总作为维也纳新年音乐会的压轴结束曲目。世界各地的人，即使对这类音乐风格兴趣不浓，也能立即分辨出它的旋律。然而，很少有人知道这首作品的由来。

3 在 1865 年，维也纳男子合唱团委托施特劳斯写一曲赞美诗，但因为有其他任务在身，他没能开始创作。第二年，奥地利的一次军事失败摧毁了维也纳人的信心，施特劳斯决定完成这项委托，创作一首乐曲，重振人们的精神。施特劳斯出生在维也纳的一个郊区，他也一直深爱着这座城市和这里的河流，多瑙河。因此，他想创作一首音乐作品来表达对它们的依恋，并以一句诗"在那美丽的蓝色多瑙河边"来命名。

4 1867 年 2 月，《蓝色多瑙河》作为带歌词的合唱曲目首次登台。或许是由于其歌词平平无奇，观众并没有对它表现出多大兴趣。但当施特劳斯把曲子改编成交响乐版本，并于当年晚些时候在巴黎向世界展演时，这首曲子引起了轰动。

5 这首曲子的欢快旋律赢得了公众的青睐，也成就了可能是有史以来最悦耳易记的古典音乐作品。它抓住了维也纳那无忧无虑的特性。自此，《蓝色多瑙河》不仅成为了奥地利音乐史的一部分，也成为了维也纳永恒的标志。这首乐曲依然深深地扎根于维也纳人的生活，并且总是出现在维也纳的古典音乐演出中。新年伊始，当午夜的钟声在奥地利首都渐渐消散，人们准备在新年起舞时，《蓝色多瑙河》的音符就会回荡在广场和庭院中。

6 然而，《蓝色多瑙河》并非唯一与维也纳有联系的音乐作品。这座城市还有着富饶多产的音乐历史，很少有其他城市能与之媲美。维也纳之所以与古典音乐关系如此密切，得益于它曾是 18 和 19 世纪欧洲的音乐中心。那时，许多著名的作曲家和音乐家汇聚在这个城市，建立声望，向其他大师学习。这个时代被称为"维也纳古典主义时期"。除施特劳斯外，贝多芬、莫扎特和海顿也都是当时最负盛名的作曲家。他们在此生活，创作出许多杰出作品，为维也纳古典音乐中心的声誉添砖加瓦。

7 我们应该试着了解维也纳起初能成为这样一个古典音乐中心的缘由是什么。其政治影响可能是一个原因，它从 1867 年起就是奥匈帝国的首都，而在这之前，它是奥地利帝国的首都。维也纳那些见多识广、受过良好教育的观众也可能助推了这座城市的成功，因为当时的贵族宫廷成员将鼓

（页底截断，接下页）

励和支持音乐家视为自己的责任。

8 维也纳的社会氛围也对当地的音乐家产生了重要影响。艺术家们常常聚在有情调的咖啡馆里讨论艺术和音乐，例如，作曲家舒伯特就是其中的一员。勃拉姆斯也受到了当地咖啡馆中民间音乐的影响。此外，维也纳独特的地理环境也是这些有创造力的作曲家的灵感来源。这里群山环绕，并有公园点缀，它的自然魅力吸引了许多艺术家。这里的风景引发情感上的共鸣，愈发激励着这些著名的作曲家们创作杰作。

9 如今，游客可聆听维也纳数百个或传统或现代的音乐场所的任何一场音乐会，欣赏其音乐传奇。每晚，全市多个舞台上都有音乐演出，为听众带来欢愉，令人们重温古典音乐的辉煌时代。这个文化中心吸引了大批游客，其中不少都是音乐爱好者。对他们来说，多瑙河是一个不容错过的景点，部分原因就在于它和施特劳斯最著名的作品之间那永恒的连接。无论这条河流经多少其他城市，也无须纠结它的河水果真湛蓝与否——在全世界人民的心中，只要那温柔的旋律响起，多瑙河就是维也纳永恒的梦幻之河，闪耀着历史与无限可能。

## Further exploration

乌镇：过去与现在

1 浙江乌镇是长江以南的六个水乡古镇之一。这些镇子以密集的运河与河流网闻名。乌镇隶属嘉兴市，位于由上海、杭州和苏州这些顶级旅游胜地形成的三角区中心。这座宜人的小镇被正式列入中国历史文化名镇、中国十大魅力名镇、全国环境优美乡镇、国家 5A 级旅游景区的名单中。

2 乌镇始建于 1,300 多年前，是历史上许多名人的出生地。它保留了许多物质文化和民俗风情特征，展现了它悠久的历史。乌镇的江南风格民宅可以追溯到明清时期，是其独特遗产的进一步佐证。

3 游客走在镇上，会感觉仿佛回到了更古朴的时代。手划的木板在运河上吱呀作响，穿过高脚屋，横过石桥下。这些古老的房屋，以古老的梁柱支撑，彩绘的柱子装饰，顶部是瓦片屋顶，看起来像水墨画中的细节。在建筑物之间的狭窄巷道里，小贩们延续着他们历史悠久的买卖。

4 虽然乌镇有着深厚的历史底蕴，但它并不是一座露天博物馆。近年来，这个水乡小镇持续发展，吸引了追求当代文化和最新科技的人们。

5 一年一度的乌镇戏剧节是这里众多的文化节之一，已成为中国的现象级节日。优美的风景，来自世界各地的演员，以及街头快闪表演，一时将乌镇变为中国的文化中心。除了本土和国际戏剧演出，节日的竞赛活动和座谈会也会在各种高雅的场所举办。戏剧节不仅为成千上万的参与者提供了娱乐活动，还巩固了乌镇在国际戏剧界的地位，为这里带来了繁荣。

6 乌镇在科技领域也声名鹊起。这里的日常生活处处融入了智慧特色。为长者提供的高科技设施、遍布的交互式信息亭、以及便民共享单车计划，都展现了乌镇在旅游、公共事务和日常生活中以技术为中心的做法。

7 或许有些出乎意料，但这个历史悠久的小镇也是年度世界互联网大会的永久会址。自 2014 年始，来自世界各地的互联网专家、学者、政府官员和科技企业家都齐聚乌镇，分享他们对网络空间发展的观点，探索前沿技术，讨论《世界互联网发展报告》。

8 融合了传统之美和前沿发展的乌镇，是一处独一无二的旅游景点。

## Extended reading

城市：过去和未来

1 今天，全世界有一半以上的人口生活在城镇地区。到 2050 年，这一比例将增加至 70%。但仅在 100 年前，每十个人中只有两个人在城市生活，在那之前，这个比例甚至更低。我们是如何达到如此高的城市化程度的？这对我们的未来又意味着什么？

2 在人类历史早期，人们以游猎采集为生，经常从一个地方迁徙到另一个地方寻找食物。但大约一万年前，我们的祖先学会了选择性育种和早期农业技术。有史以来第一次，人类能够生产食物，而不仅是寻找食物，这促成了半永久性村庄的发展。"为什么只是半永久性的？"你可能会问。起初，由于土壤资源枯竭，村庄仍不得不每隔几年就搬迁一次。大约 5,000 年前，灌溉和土壤耕作等技术得到发展。因而，人们能够依靠稳定长期的食物供应，这使得永久定居成为可能。当生产出足够的食物后，就不再需要每个人都参与到耕作中了。这给予了其他行业发展空间，也由此衍生出了城市。

3 有了城市生产盈余的食物以及工具和其他物品，商业和远距离交往就有了可能。随着贸易的繁荣，推车、船只、道路和港口等促进贸易的技术和基础设施也在不断发展。当然，建造和维护这些需要更多的劳动力。更多职业和机会的出现也吸引了更多的人从农村来到城市。

4 如果你认为现代城市过于拥挤，那你可能会很惊讶地发现公元前 2000 年的一些城市人口密度几乎是当今上海的两倍。这主要是因为当时的交通并不发达。所有设施都必须在步行范围内，包括为数不多的清洁水源。此外，城市的占地面积又因需要城墙抵御攻击而受到进一步限制。罗马帝国能够通过发展基础设施来克服这些限制，但除此之外，直到工业革命时期，新技术使城市能够进一步扩大和整合时，现代城市才真正开始出现。

5 20 世纪初，伦敦的居民已接近 700 万。随着城市居住者人数的增加，全世界城市人口密度急剧上升。新的建筑技术让人口密度变得更高。在 20 世纪 20 年代初，摩天大楼让纽约拥有了比伦敦还多的市民。21 世纪初，全球已有大约 30 个巨型都市，并且还在增加，这一趋势不会停止。到 2045 年，城市将会成为 60 多亿人的安家之所。

6 那么，城市需要如何改变来适应这种增长呢？首先，世界要寻找为每个人提供足够的食物、卫生设施和教育的途径。其次，城市增长应以一种不损害土地资源的方式进行，因为土地提供支撑人类发展的物资和服务。许多人认为，城市不应向横向扩张、占用更多的土地。那么，向上纵向扩张是理想的解决方案吗？一些人认为高耸的摩天大楼削弱社群归属感，助长个体身份隐匿。然而，未来的垂直型城市很可能会惊艳我们，甚至大自然都可以在其中占据一席之地。

7 在拥有约 600 万居民的岛国新加坡，两位建筑师正试图将自然、人口密度和高质量的生活结合起来。他们研发了一系列非凡的项目，以创造一个被自然覆盖的、生机盎然的美丽城市，而向上纵向发展是唯一的解决方案。花园、公园，甚至原始森林都被复制到了建筑物的高处。在一个酒店项目中，建筑内的绿化面积是建筑物占地面积约 200%。正因如此，该建筑十分与众不同。比起一个由玻璃、钢铁或混凝土构造的建筑，它更像是一个自然的归宿。住客的反馈非常鼓舞人心："当我清晨醒来，向窗外看去，我看到了一整片红色的海洋——由红色蜻蜓组成的海洋。"该项目成功地将生物多样性带入城市和建筑中。

8 此外，专家们还以创建自给自足的城市为目标，让城市成为食物、能源和水的生成者。例如，用太阳能电池板覆盖建筑物的顶层来发电。而蔬菜可以种在下一层。城市和农场之间将不再有区别，因为城市可以自给自足生产食物。

9 希望通过这些努力，我们可以在城市的密度不断增大的同时，创造更为宜居的环境。更美好的城市即将到来。

# Unit 5 Journey to the stars

## Unit overview

This unit focuses on human space exploration and the experiences of astronauts in space. Its purpose is to assist Ss in recognizing the importance of space exploration for humanity and the significant role that China has played. The text in *Active reading* describes China's achievements in space exploration, emphasizing the motives behind such endeavors and the positive impact they have on scientific discovery, technological advancement, and daily life. The text in *Further exploration* narrates the difficulties encountered by the astronauts involved in the Apollo 13 mission throughout their space voyage. Then the audio introduces some tips on staying healthy in space, further informing Ss about the work and life of astronauts. In *Project*, Ss are asked to give an interactive talk at an exhibition about China's space exploration, focusing on China's achievements and how Chinese astronauts deal with challenges in space. The essay in *Extended reading* is a revised excerpt from Jules Verne's novel *From the Earth to the Moon*, describing the launch of a projectile to the moon. By learning this unit, Ss are expected to embrace human qualities of curiosity, innovation, and courage etc., and take pride in China's achievements in space exploration.

（本页为单元结构流程图，按方框层次转录如下）

**Scenario**
在中国航天博物馆，学生以志愿讲解员的身份为参加夏令营的国际学生介绍中国航天成就以及宇航员在太空中所遇到的挑战。

↓

**Active reading**（Sailing the ocean of stars）
阅读介绍人类航天成就的文章，了解人类航天发展历史，航天科技应用，以及中国航天所取得的伟大成就。

→ **Further exploration – Text**（Apollo 13）
阅读关于美国登月飞船阿波罗13号的太空历险故事，了解宇航员在执行太空任务时所面临的巨大挑战。

→ **Further exploration – Audio**（Staying healthy in space）
听音频，了解太空旅行对人体的影响及应对方法。

↓

**Project**
介绍中国航天成就及中国宇航员所面对的太空挑战，并以问答的形式与参观者积极互动。

↓

**Extended reading**（Fire!）
阅读儒勒·凡尔纳科幻小说《从地球到月球》选段，了解19世纪人类对探索太空的想象，激发对科学知识和技术创新的追求。

↓

**Self-reflection**
回顾整个单元的学习，反思在内容、语言、结构、交际和行为上的收获。

↓

结合所学知识和信息查阅，介绍中国航天成就和宇航员的太空生活，认识科学探索对人类发展的多重意义，了解中国航天的开拓进程，培养民族自豪感。

## Scenario

The scenario in this unit can be analyzed from the following aspects:
**Setting:** The China Space Museum
**Topic:** China's achievements and the challenges Chinese astronauts face in space
**Identity:** Speaker – Volunteer guides
　　　　Audience – International visitors
**Purpose:** To welcome the visitors to enjoy the exhibition, inform them about China's space exploration and the life of taikonauts, and invite them to engage in discussions

**Teaching suggestions**
T can help Ss generate ideas about how to give an interactive talk by asking them to search online for China's achievements in space exploration and the challenges that Chinese astronauts face while in space. Remind Ss to use credible sources and gather relevant statistics and success stories. Then divide Ss into small groups, ask them to exchange ideas on the content they plan to include in their talk, prompt them to discuss the historical background, key milestones, technological advancements, and notable missions in China's space program, and encourage them to brainstorm engaging and interactive ways to present this information to the international visitors.

After the tryout, T can ask Ss to reflect on their difficulties in four aspects, i.e., content (how to introduce China's achievements and how Chinese astronauts deal with challenges in space), language (the expressions and sentence structures they have used and feel hard to use), structure (how to organize a talk clearly and logically), and communication (how to make the talk interactive), and write them down in the table in the Student's Book.

## Active reading

In *Scenario*, Ss are expected to give an interactive talk on China's achievements and the challenges Chinese astronauts face in space. The text provides a historical review of human exploration of space, with special highlights on the achievements made by China. It opens with a description of the Tiangong class hosted by Chinese astronauts, which serves as an example of the writing technique of using a specific lead-in to introduce a grand theme. The main points of this text are as follows: the driving force behind space exploration, human findings about the universe and the solar system, the latest development in manned spacecraft, and the benefits of space technology in daily life. The text concludes with an exciting prospect of future space exploration. On the whole, this part offers multiple examples of China's achievements which will facilitate Ss' preparation for the task in *Project*.

### Before you read

**Reference answers**
1 The first picture depicts the ancient legend of Chang'e, a Chinese goddess, who flew to the moon. The second picture showcases Chinese astronauts on board the Tiangong space station, representing China's human space exploration endeavors.

2 The two pictures are related and symbolize the enduring fascination of the Chinese people with the mysteries of the universe: from ancient times, when celestial exploration captivated their imaginations, to the present, when modern China has achieved remarkable milestones in space exploration. The first picture showcases China's fascination with lunar exploration, while the second picture represents the nation's ability to independently build and operate a space station. These images embody the continuous realization of China's space aspirations and the culmination of centuries of dedication and progress. China's remarkable achievements in space exploration reflect the nation's collective strength and unwavering commitment to pursuing the dream of space.

## Sailing the ocean of stars

### Text interpretation

本文采用说明文体，讲述了人类太空探索取得的成就和中国的伟大贡献。文章第一部分（第一段）由中国宇航员"天宫课堂"开篇，以事例讲述的方式引入人类太空探索这一宏大主题。第二部分（第二段）讲述人类对宇宙的好奇心由来已久。第三部分（第三至第五段）按照时间顺序，介绍了人类在太空探索中取得的成就，从能够回答关于宇宙的基本问题，到通过太空探测器探索太阳系和其他行星的奥秘，再到发射载人航天器、进行太空行走和建设空间站。这些进展拓展了人类活动的范围，为长期太空旅行积累了宝贵经验。其中，中国的航天成就为人类太空探索作出了重要贡献。第四部分（第六至第七段）介绍了航天技术民用化方面取得的诸多成就。最后一部分（第八段）呼吁国际社会共同努力推动太空探索的发展。

从写作手法上看，本文最突出的特征是通过有趣的案例引入太空探索话题，充分利用了视觉效果、情感共鸣和具体化的手法，使抽象的主题更具体、更有趣、更能吸引读者的注意力。这种写作手法不仅提高了文章的可读性和吸引力，还让读者更深入地了解和感受太空探索的魅力。教师可结合写作技巧部分的讲解和练习引导学生分析这种写作手法的优点，并在产出任务中有效应用。

从知识层面，本文梳理了不同时代、不同国家在多个领域所取得的航天成就。从价值观层面，文章意图展现人类的好奇心、想象力、意志力、合作精神在科学发展中的作用，同时通过介绍中国航天所取得的成就，培养学习者的民族自信心和参与国际合作的积极性。

教师可以借助课文中的词汇、句子进行隐性的课程思政，例如：
例1（第一段）：解读宇航员王亚平对祖国年轻一代的寄语，激发学生投身科学探索和祖国建设的热情；
例2（第二、五、六段）：通过讲解、分析中国航天器命名中的中国文化渊源（天宫、嫦娥、玉兔、神舟、北斗等），培养学生的文化认同感与自豪感；
例3（第一、五段）：查阅中国航天员人物事迹，讨论德才兼备的高素质人才对建设科技强国的重要性；
例4（第八段）：分析中国空间站对外开放、合作共赢的态度所折射出的中国大国外交精神及对世界和平发展的影响。

### Photo interpretation

本单元课首图片为中国文昌航天发射场。它是中国首个滨海航天发射基地，也是世界上为数不多的低纬度发射场之一。该发射中心可以发射长征五号系列火箭与长征七号运载火箭，主要承担地球同步轨道卫星、大质量极轨卫星、大吨位空间站和深空探测卫星等航天器的发射任务。

主课文配图一描绘了中国古代传说中"嫦娥奔月"的场景，代表远古时期人类对外太空展开的丰富想象，图二为当代中国航天员实现飞天梦想的情景。古今对比，喻示中国新时代高质量发展取得的成就。

教师可以在导入活动中充分利用课首图和这两张图片，让学生围绕图片讨论问题，引导学生关注我国航天事业伟大发展，隐性融入育人元素。比如，教师可以设计以下问题：
• What is the significance of the Wenchang Spacecraft Launch Site in China's space exploration efforts?
• What are the main capabilities and advantages of the Wenchang Spacecraft Launch Site compared to other launch sites?

### Culture notes

1　**Tiangong space station**
The Tiangong space station is constructed by China and operated by China Manned Space Agency (CMSA) in low Earth orbit between 340 and 450 km above the surface. It is China's first long-term space station, part of the Tiangong program, and the core of the "third step" of the China-manned space program. The construction of the station is based on the experience gained from its precursors, Tiangong-1 and Tiangong-2. The first module, the Tianhe ("Harmony of the Heavens") core module, was launched on April 29, 2021, followed by multiple crewed and uncrewed missions and two more laboratory cabin modules Wentian ("Quest for the Heavens") launched on July 24, 2022, and Mengtian ("Dreaming of the Heavens") launched on October 31, 2022. The research conducted on the station aims to improve researchers' ability to conduct science experiments in space.

2　**Chang'e-5 mission**
The Chang'e-5 mission was launched from the Wenchang Spacecraft Launch Site. It successfully landed on the Moon on December 1, 2020, and completed its mission by returning 1,731 grams of lunar rock and soil to Earth on December 17, 2020. This historic achievement marked the first time in 44 years that lunar samples were collected and brought back to Earth. With this feat, China became the third country in the world to retrieve lunar samples, joining the ranks of the United States and the Soviet Union. The mission lasted for 23 days, showcasing China's advancements in space exploration and its commitment to lunar exploration.

3　**The Milky Way**
The Milky Way is the galaxy that contains the Sun, the Earth, and other objects in our solar system. It also includes hundreds of billions of stars besides the sun. Huge clouds of gas and dust lie throughout the galaxy, and they constantly form new stars. The Milky Way is so massive that about 10 smaller galaxies orbit it like satellites revolving around a planet. The diameter of the Milky Way is about 100,000 light years. The galaxy is about 10,000 light-years thick at the central bulge. Toward the edges of the disk, it is much flatter, with a thickness of only about 1,000 light years. Our solar system is on the outskirts of the galaxy, about 25,000 light years from the center.

4　**Voyager 1 & Voyager 2**
The National Aeronautics and Space Administration (NASA) launched Voyager 1 on September 5, 1977. The probe made its closest approach to Jupiter on March 5, 1979, encountered Saturn on November 12, 1980, and then headed toward interstellar space (the space between the stars). Voyager 2, launched on August 20, 1977, made its closest approach to Jupiter on July 9, 1979, Saturn on August 26,
（页底截断，接下页）

1981, Uranus on January 24, 1986, and Neptune on August 25, 1989, then traveled toward interstellar space.

5　**Mars rover Zhurong**
Zhurong is China's first Mars rover. On May 22, 2021, the Zhurong rover arrived on the surface of Mars and began its exploration. Zhurong is tasked with surveying Mars' landforms, geological structures, soil characteristics, potential locations of water and ice, and atmospheric and environmental characteristics, as well as magnetic and other physical fields.

6　**Shenzhou spacecraft**
The Shenzhou spacecraft is a series of China's manned spacecraft, with research and development beginning in 1992, when they were first listed in the national development plan. The Shenzhou spacecraft consists of an orbital module, a reentry module, and a service module.

### Language points

1　**You are all budding flowers of our country. (Para. 1)**
**budding:** *a.* (only before noun) beginning to develop 开始发展的；成长中的
*As a budding artist, you will have to make a special effort for people to take you seriously.*

**Notes:** In Chinese, the young generation are often compared to "budding flowers"（含苞待放的花朵）, which represents the expectations the older generation hold for the youngsters. In English, the term "budding artist / actor / writer, etc." is commonly used to describe someone who is in the early stages of their creative journey, such as painting, acting, writing, etc., and shows potential for future success.

2　**Space exploration arose from our desire to know about the universe, ... (Para. 2)**
**arise from:** if sth. arises from or out of a situation or an event, it is caused or started by that situation, etc. （由……）产生；（由……）引起
*Such misunderstandings may have arisen from language barriers and cultural differences.*

3　**This curiosity has inspired myths since time immemorial. (Para. 2)**
**since time immemorial:** for an extremely long time 自古以来
*Since time immemorial, storytelling has served as a fundamental component of human culture and communication.*

**Notes:** In this sentence, the word "immemorial" means "starting longer ago than people can remember, or than written history shows." The word is often used in the phrase "from / since time immemorial," when referring to a time beyond the reach of memory, record, or tradition.

4　**..., and the Earth is located on the outer fringe of the one known as the Milky Way. (Para. 3)**
**on the fringe (of sth.):** at the part of sth. that is farthest from the center in （……的）边缘；在（……的）外围
*To get a better view of the stage from afar, we remained on the fringe of the crowd.*

**Notes:** When we describe something as being "on the fringe" of something else, it is marginal, additional, or secondary in comparison to the center. This can apply to spatial relationships or serve as metaphorical representation of social circles.

5　**Similarly, the lunar samples brought back by China's Chang'e-5 mission have shed new light on the evolution of the moon. (Para. 4)**
**Notes:** When lamp sheds light on your desk, it lights up the area around it. This expression is also used metaphorically to mean "to make something easier to understand by providing new or better information." The human sense of vision is such a rich source of metaphor that we could find many other examples related to "light," like the verb "enlighten," which means "to explain something to someone." Likewise, when you feel uninformed about something important because you have not been told about it, you can say "I'm kept in the dark."

6　**..., they expanded the scope of human activities ... (Para. 5)**
**scope:** *n.* [U] the range of things that a subject, activity, book, etc. deals with （学科、活动、书籍等的）范围
*The scope of the project includes both the design and implementation of the new software system.*

**Notes:** There are several verbs that can be used with "scope," depending on the context, like "expand / widen / broaden / extend / narrow / limit the scope." For example:
*The professor had to limit the scope of the lecture due to time constraints.*
*Before the work begins, we should outline the scope of the project.*

7　**Chinese astronauts, also known as taikonauts, have devoted themselves to developing the space industry and made their mark in the history of space exploration. (Para. 5)**
**make one's mark:** attain recognition or distinction 得到公认；获得名声
*As a young artist, she quickly made her mark in the world of abstract painting, standing out with her unique style.*

**Notes:** In the phrase "make one's mark," the word "mark" refers to a noticeable effect or influence. It denotes a significant impression or contribution that a person has made in a particular field, area, or activity. When we describe someone becoming successful or famous, we could say "make / leave one's mark as ..." or "make / leave one's mark on / in ..." The preposition "as" is used when we want to specify the role or profession in which someone has excelled, e.g., "He made his mark as a great writer." On the other hand, "on" or "in" is used when we want to highlight the area, field, or domain that has been significantly influenced or transformed, e.g., "She left her mark on the fashion industry."

8　**Technologies developed in the 1960s to monitor astronauts' vital signs have found their way into every hospital. (Para. 7)**
**Meaning:** These technologies initially developed for astronauts in the 1960s have eventually been implemented and become widely used for medical purposes.
**way into sth.:** a method of getting involved in a particular activity or type of work （做某事的）方法；（进入……的）途径
*Through diligent study and practice, she found her way into the prestigious orchestra as a first violinist.*

9　**With its infinite possibilities, space exploration is a cause which will enrich and strengthen humanity's future. (Para. 8)**
**Meaning:** Space exploration, because of its unlimited potential and opportunities, is a pursuit that will greatly contribute to and fortify the future of human beings. This might be through technological advances, scientific knowledge, resource discovery, or other potential benefits that we can gain from exploring space.

**infinite:** *a.* very great, and seeming to have no limit 巨大的；极大的；无限的
*Her love for her children was infinite, boundless beyond measure.*

10　**Without doubt, the vast ocean of stars will remain a source of information and inspiration ... (Para. 8)**
**Notes:** Alliteration (押头韵) is a rhetorical device that uses several words together that begin with the same sound or letter in order to make a special effect, as is found in "information and inspiration." Other classic examples include Peter Pan, Mickey Mouse, etc. Another common example is "She sells seashells by the seashore," where the "s" sound is repeated. It's a popular technique in poetry, advertising, and other forms of writing because it draws the reader's attention to a particular section of the text.

**Teaching suggestions**
T may encourage Ss to research the latest advances in space technology so that they can find evidence for their discussion. One way is to do some follow-up research on issues mentioned in the text, such as new findings about Mars, human settlement on the moon, scientific experiments at the space station, etc. Another approach could be question-oriented, where Ss make a list of global issues and find out how space technology might help solve these problems, such as climate change, the energy crisis, world peace, etc.

**Reference answers**
1 Space exploration enriches and strengthens humanity's future through scientific discovery, technological advancements, and global collaboration. Exploring space uncovers new knowledge about the universe, leading to breakthroughs in various fields and improving our understanding of the Earth. It drives the development of innovative technologies that have practical applications in communications, navigation, and more. Additionally, international collaboration in space missions promotes cultural exchange, strengthens diplomatic relations, and fosters peaceful cooperation on a global scale. Overall, space exploration creates opportunities for progress, making a positive impact on our lives and the world.

2 The Wenchang Spacecraft Launch Site holds significant importance in China's space exploration endeavors due to its favorable geographical location near the equator, which facilitates efficient launches into various orbits. Its strategic coastal position enables the safe disposal of rocket stages over the ocean. The site boasts modern infrastructure and is situated remotely, ensuring minimal disturbances to surrounding communities. This prioritizes the smooth execution of missions while emphasizing environmental safety. In summary, Wenchang serves as a crucial asset for China's space exploration program.

### Analyzing writing techniques

**Reference answers**
1
1 The lead-in connects to the theme of space exploration by providing a real-life example of space exploration and its impact on our understanding of the universe. It highlights the experiments conducted by Wang Yaping aboard the Tiangong space station. This example serves to spark curiosity and fascination about space exploration and sets the stage for the subsequent discussion on the origins of our ancient dreams, the development of technology, the discoveries made through space probes, the achievements of human spaceflight, and the practical applications of space technologies.

2 In the lead-in, the author uses the following details:
• Wang Yaping's demonstration during a live class aboard the Tiangong space station
• The buoyancy experiment with a table tennis ball in a glass of water, where the ball remained in the middle of the water instead of floating to the surface like on Earth
• The creation of a thin film of water within a metal ring by adding water from a bag
• The placement of a folded paper flower on the water film, causing it to "magically blossom" and spread out across the water
• Wang Yaping's statement about the flower reminding her of her daughter and her hope for the dreams of the students to blossom in the vast universe
These details illustrate the behavior of water in the weightless environment of space and evoke a sense of wonder and inspiration about space exploration.

3 The lead-in serves as an intriguing introduction that sparks interest and primes the reader for the subsequent space exploration. It smoothly transitions from a specific example to the broader theme of space exploration, drawing the readers deeper into the main content.

2
In the old city of Jiuquan, China, a brave team of adventurers stand in front of a gigantic rocket, ready to begin a journey that will change the way we see the universe. With excitement and hopes of learning new things, they take off into the sky, capturing incredible pictures and conducting groundbreaking experiments. Through their courage and dedication to exploring space, we are reminded of the endless marvels that wait for us in the vast unknown. Get ready for an amazing ride as we uncover the mysteries of Chinese space exploration, where an extraordinary adventure beyond our wildest dreams awaits.

For thousands of years, human beings have been attracted to the universe far out of reach. Thanks to the development of science and technology, we have sent machines and later humans to space, expanding the scope of human activities. In 2003, a Chinese astronaut went to space for the first time, which opened a new chapter in China's space dream. Cutting-edge technologies developed in space exploration have found wide practical applications in other fields. The spirit of China's space exploration keeps encouraging people from all walks of life to bravely explore the unknown, and inspiring Chinese people to contribute more and better Chinese wisdom, Chinese solutions, and Chinese strength.

### Sharing your ideas

**Reference answers**
**Satellite systems:**
China has made remarkable advancements in satellite systems. It has developed a variety of satellites for Earth observation, communications, navigation, and scientific research purposes. The Beidou Navigation Satellite System is China's answer to GPS and has gained global recognition for its high precision and reliability.
**Lunar exploration:**
China's lunar exploration program, also known as the Chang'e program, has achieved significant milestones. It successfully landed unmanned spacecraft on the moon and conducted multiple missions to collect data on lunar topography, mineral resources, and geological structure. The Chang'e-4 mission made history by becoming the first mission to successfully land on the far side of the moon.
**Exploration of other planets:**
China's Mars mission, Tianwen-1, successfully entered Mars orbit and deployed a rover to explore the Red Planet's surface.
**Space station:**
Tiangong space station has been visited by three batches of Chinese astronauts, or taikonauts, already, and it will continue to serve as a major hub for microgravity research.
**Manned space program:**
China's achievements in manned space flight have been substantial as well. The Shenzhou program has launched multiple manned missions, advancing knowledge of long-duration space flight. The most recent, Shenzhou-16, carried a crew of three taikonauts to the Tiangong station for a five-month mission.

**Suggestions for evaluation**
For this activity, Ss' answer can be evaluated according to the following criteria:
• whether Ss list achievements based on reliable sources of information
• whether Ss explain the achievements with specific details

## Further exploration

In *Active reading*, Ss have learned about the achievements made by humankind. Those achievements did not come easily. For one thing, space presents a dangerous and inhospitable environment for human beings. For another, there are inherent risks involved in both space travel and work. In this part, Ss will read a short passage on the "successful failure" of the U.S. Apollo 13 mission. They will also listen to an audio material about "staying healthy in space," which describes the specific challenges human body faces in a space environment and how the astronauts deal with them with the aid of science and technology. These materials are in real use to the second subtitle in *Project*. T can encourage Ss to pay special attention to the vocabulary concerning space facilities and physiological conditions.

## Apollo 13 (Text)

### Culture notes

**The Apollo program**
Apollo was the U.S. space program that carried people to the moon. The program took place between 1961 and 1975 and was carried out by NASA. Each Apollo mission carried three astronauts. In moon-landing missions, two of the astronauts landed on the lunar surface. The third astronaut stayed with the orbiting capsule. The program landed a total of 12 astronauts on the lunar surface. Three Apollo missions circled the moon without landing. The first landing occurred during Apollo 11 mission on July 20, 1969. Astronauts Neil Armstrong and Buzz Aldrin sat down on the flat feature known as the Sea of Tranquility. The third Apollo 11 astronaut, Michael Collins, stayed aboard the orbiting command module.

### Language points

1　**Things started to go wrong several days before the scheduled launch, when one of the backup astronauts developed German measles. (Para. 3)**
**backup:** *n.* [C, U] people or equipment that can be used when extra help is needed 后备人员；支援人员；备用设备
*When she saw the dangerous situation unfolding, she immediately called for backup from her team.*
**develop:** *v.* if you develop a disease or illness, or if it develops, you start to have it 患（病）；得（病）
*As his unhealthy habits continued, he began to develop a disease that significantly affected his quality of life.*
**German measles:** *n.* [U] 风疹
*Despite being vaccinated, my brother unfortunately contracted German measles during our trip to Europe.*

2　**As if these obstacles were not enough, disaster struck two days after the rocket was launched. (Para. 4)**
**Notes:** The phrase "as if these obstacles were not enough" implies that the previously mentioned obstacles were already challenging, and the subsequent disaster made the situation even worse.

3　**As the astronauts neared the Earth's atmosphere, they climbed back into the command module, ditched the lunar module in space, and prepared for a splashdown in the Pacific Ocean. (Para. 5)**
**ditch:** *vt.* (*infml.*) stop having sth. because you no longer want it 扔掉；抛弃；丢弃
*Due to the unexpected circumstances that arose, they had to ditch their initial plan of backpacking across Europe.*
**splashdown:** *n.* [C, U] a landing by a spacecraft in the sea （航天器在海上的）溅落
*The spacecraft is expected to make an ocean splashdown this Sunday.*

4　**They were weary and chilled, but all of them managed to survive. (Para. 6)**
**weary:** *a.* very tired, esp. after you have been working hard or doing sth. for a long time （尤指长时间努力工作后）疲劳的，疲倦的，疲惫的
*After a long day of meetings and problem-solving, John arrived home feeling utterly weary and ready for a good night's sleep.*

**Notes:** In everyday English, people usually say "tired" rather than "weary."

5　**This mission, a "successful failure," did not achieve its goal, but all of its crew members survived despite almost insurmountable odds. (Para. 7)**
**insurmountable:** *a.* (*fml.*) an insurmountable difficulty or problem is too large or difficult to deal with （困难）难以克服的，不可逾越的；（问题）难以处理的
*The professional obstacles in her path seemed insurmountable, yet she remained determined to overcome them and reach her career goals.*
**odds:** *n.* [pl.] difficulties which make a good result seem very unlikely 困难；不利条件
*The soldiers' job was to hold on despite impossible odds.*

**Notes:** In the word "insurmountable," the verb "mount" refers to the action of getting up onto something. The term "mount" originates from the same Latin root as "mountain," and we keep that imagery in mind when using "surmount." Climbing up or over a mountain symbolizes achievement. The word is used in relation to human effort and typically carries a positive connotation. Therefore, we often speak of surmounting difficulties, problems, or hurdles.

**Reference answers**
1
1) exposed to the virus
2) replaced
3) two oxygen tanks
4) moon landing
5) a lifeboat
6) extreme cold
7) the Earth's atmosphere
8) a splashdown
