## Staying healthy in space (Audio)

### Script

1  More than 60 years ago, the first human flew into space. But what does a trip like that actually do to the human body? What do astronauts do to offset the hazards of trips to space? Let's find out!

2  On the Earth, our lower body and legs carry our weight. This helps keep our bones and muscles strong. In space, astronauts float. They do not use their legs much. Their lower backs begin to lose strength, and their leg muscles and bones become weak. Within only a single month, an astronaut's bone density can decrease by one to two percent, which can increase the risk of bones breaking when they return to the Earth. That's why people in space must exercise every day. For example, at the Tiangong space station, fitness equipment includes a spin bike and resistance bands, along with a treadmill.

3  Another important thing is food. In space, where astronauts confront drastic environmental and physical changes, the need for safe and nutritious food is vital to overall health. It was once to some extent challenging to produce and transport food for those traveling far from the Earth. Due to recent efforts, astronauts are now provided with food that does more than simply meet their nutritional requirements. The food is tasty, attractive, and convenient. For example, China's astronauts can choose from more than 120 dishes during their stay in orbit. The foods are usually solid, boneless, in small pieces, and selected to meet the astronauts' personal tastes. Meals include thin pieces of pork in garlic sauce, kung pao chicken, black pepper beef, pickled cabbage, and many more. There's also a kitchen and a microwave, so the astronauts can have hot food whenever they need to.

4  There are some other negative effects of trips to space. For example, some astronauts' eyes show flattening of the eyeballs after long-duration space flight. An astronaut's heart may also lose mass. When astronauts come back to the Earth, they might experience a reduction in blood pressure, making them dizzy and even causing them to faint after standing up. Scientists are working to prevent these negative changes, which will make space travel a lot safer.

### Language points

1  What do astronauts do to offset the hazards of trips to space? (Para. 1)

**offset**: *vt.* if the cost or amount of sth. offsets another cost or amount, the two things have an opposite effect so that the situation remains the same 抵消；补偿

*During the competition, his speed offset his opponent's greater weight.*

**hazard**: *n.* [C] sth. that may be dangerous, or cause accidents or problems 危险；隐患

*Be aware of the economic hazards of running a small farm.*

2  Due to recent efforts, astronauts are now provided with food that does more than simply meet their nutritional requirements. (Para. 3)

**Meaning:** Because of recent endeavors, astronauts now receive food that goes beyond merely fulfilling their nutritional needs.

**Notes:** T can remind S of the bare infinitive "meet" in the expression "does more than simply meet." Ss can learn this usage and make a new sentence. For example:

*Thanks to advancements in technology, online education does more than simply provide students with course materials; it also fosters a highly interactive learning environment.*

3  An astronaut's heart may also lose mass. (Para. 4)

**mass**: *n.* [U] (technical) the amount of material in something （术语）质量

*The scientist carefully measured the mass of the chemical compound before proceeding with the experiment.*

**Reference answers**

1  T

### Sharing your ideas

**Reference answers**
**Interviewer:** We are truly delighted to have you here. How do you feel about the trip?
**Astronaut:** It was an incredible feeling. There's nothing quite as surreal as viewing Earth from space. It was both intimidating and awe-inspiring at the same time. Being in zero gravity is an incredibly unique experience that is hard to describe.

**Interviewer:** It indeed sounds otherworldly. Moving on, what challenges or difficulties did you come across during your journey?
**Astronaut:** There were several challenges. For starters, the physical strain on the body both during take-off and re-entry is immense. Adapting to the zero-gravity environment was a challenge, too. It changes everything, from the way we move to the way we eat and sleep.
**Interviewer:** Those challenges do seem daunting. Can you tell us how you tackled these challenges or difficulties?
**Astronaut:** All astronauts undergo intensive training that prepares us to handle many of the physical challenges we might face. We are trained to function under high g-force conditions. In terms of the zero-gravity environment, it's really a matter of adjustment and acclimation. Our bodies and minds have an amazing ability to adapt to new circumstances, and with time, we learn to navigate and perform our tasks effectively in such environments. Most importantly, the mission team on the ground provides constant support and guidance, which is invaluable.

**Suggestions for evaluation**
For this activity, Ss' role-play can be evaluated according to the following criteria:
• whether the interview covers the three questions listed in the book
• Whether students maintain a natural and consistent conversation flow

## Project

**Sample**
(*Words and expressions in red are from the input materials.)

**Speaker 1:**
Hello, everyone! Welcome to the China Space Museum! My name is Chen Tao, and this is my colleague, Fang Yu. We are the guides for the tour this morning, and we are both glad to see you here today!

What have we brought us together at this museum must be something we have in common. So let me ask you this – when was the first time ever you found yourself truly intrigued by the unknown in space? Was it back in the kindergarten when you first learned to sing "Twinkle, Twinkle, Little Star"? Or was it the illustrations in children's books about stars and the universe? Or in sci-fi movies such as *Apollo 13* or *The Wandering Earth*? To me, it was a camping trip in the mountains during the summer vacation when I was a second grader. That was my first time to see the Milky Way. That breathtaking view, with all the stars far out of reach, made me feel connected to the myth-makers since time immemorial and the artificial satellites, spaceships, and astronauts orbiting and working out there, as I had seen on TV. We all had our moments, didn't we?

Today, you will see an exhibition on China's Space Dream. Fang Yu and I will introduce to you the achievements made by China and the challenges that Chinese astronauts face while working in space. Are you ready? (Use slides to demonstrate images and key words if possible.)

China launched its first satellite, the Dong Fang Hong 1, in 1970. It took us another two decades to embark on the China Manned Space Program in 1992. In this program, there are three steps:
1)  Crewed spacecraft launch and return
2)  Space laboratory, spacecraft rendezvous and docking procedures
3)  Long term modular space station

As you can see here, in November 2022, with the launch and docking of Shenzhou-15 with the Tiangong space station, China successfully completed all three of the above steps, thus opening a whole new chapter in space exploration. Does anyone know the meaning of Shenzhou and Tiangong in Chinese? (Wait for response from audience.) Yes, Shenzhou means "heavenly boat" since the spacecraft are like ships navigating the ocean of stars. And Tiangong? In Chinese mythology, it is a palace in the heaven, exactly like the space station itself, composed of the core module Tianhe ("Harmony of the Heavens") and two laboratory cabin modules Wentian ("Quest for the Heavens") and Mengtian ("Dreaming of the Heavens"). As you might have heard, Tiangong station welcomes international collaboration in space research.

During the completion of the "three steps," China landed rovers on both the moon and Mars. Here you can see images of Yutu (known as the Jade Rabbit in Chinese) collecting lunar samples on its mission, and Zhurong, named after the god of fire in Chinese mythology, exploring the surface of the red planet. They both shed light on our understanding of those remote celestial bodies. China is also operating the Beidou Navigation Satellite System, named after the Big Dipper constellation, which has found practical applications in various fields.

Now, my colleague Fang Yu is going to take over, and she will tell you about the taikonauts. Buddy, the floor is all yours! (The second speaker takes over.)

**Speaker 2:**
Thanks, Chen Tao! Hi everyone. Some of you might be wondering, what does the word "taikonaut" mean? Anyone having any idea? (Wait for the audience to respond.) This is a name combining "Taikongren" which means spaceman in Chinese and astronaut in English. As their counterparts in all the other nations, these brave men and women have devoted themselves to the cause of space exploration and made their mark in history.

So how does it feel like to live and work in the space? What do taikonauts do to offset the hazards of trips to space? Let's find out!

A common challenge to life in space is the decrease in bone density. At Tiangong space station, thanks to the fitness equipment, taikonauts exercise every day, by using the spin bike, resistance bands, or a treadmill. They also need safe and nutritious food to stay healthy. The taikonauts now can choose from a menu consisting of 120 dishes!

Now, here is a list of food. Can you guess which ones are available at Tiangong space station? What do you think? How about kung pao chicken, black pepper beef, shredded pork in garlic, and pickled cabbage? The truth is, these dishes can all be found on the Tiangong menu. And with a microwave, the taikonauts could always enjoy hot meals! Actually, I heard they are growing vegetables in the space lab, too!

I hope our talk has aroused plenty of interest in you. There is so much more to see and even try your hands on at the exhibition. Shall we now start our tour to find out more about the China space dream? Please join us! This way, everyone.

### Suggestions for evaluation

| Components | Criteria / Traits |
| --- | --- |
| **Content** | • Relevance: Addressing visitors to the museum as a guide, providing information about China's achievements in space exploration and challenges Chinese astronauts face in the space<br>• Reasoning: Explaining key concepts clearly, using well-researched and specific examples<br>• Appeal: Including up-to-date information, being informative, educational, and entertaining |
| **Organization** | • Structure: Including opening remarks, main points, and conclusion<br>• Coherence: Using transitional devices to ensure coherence and unity<br>• Clarity: Highlighting key words in the main points |
| **Language** | • Diversity: Using an adequate range of vocabulary and sentence structures<br>• Acquisition: Using as many as possible of the new words and expressions learned in the unit |
| **Delivery** | • Fluency: Presenting in a natural, smooth, and clear way with a natural colloquial flow<br>• Engagement: Using body language to engage the audience and arousing interaction<br>• Visual aids: Using slides, pictures, etc. to help the audience better understand the ideas |

## Extended reading

In the previous parts, Ss have learned about the advances in space exploration in modern times. But the ambition to probe into the universe started way before Yuri Gagarin or Apollo missions. In the *Extended reading* text, Ss will read a revised excerpt from a classic work of science fiction, Jules Verne's novel *From the Earth to the Moon*. It is about the launch of the rocket to the moon. Ss will get a glimpse into the early visions and dreams of space travel that laid the foundation for the scientific and technological advancements we see today. It is hoped that this exploration of literature and imagination will further motivate students in their own pursuit of scientific knowledge and innovation.

## Fire!

### Culture notes

1  **Jules Verne (1828 – 1905)**

Jules Verne was a French novelist, poet, and playwright. His collaboration with the publisher Pierre-Jules Hetzel led to the creation of the Voyages extraordinaires, a series of bestselling adventure novels including *Journey to the Center of the Earth* (1867), *Twenty Thousand Leagues Under the Seas* (1870), and *Around the World in Eighty Days* (1873). His novels, always well documented, are generally set in the second half of the 19th century, taking into account the technological advances of the time. In addition to his novels, he wrote numerous plays, short stories, autobiographical accounts, poetry, songs and scientific, artistic and literary studies. During the 20th century, Verne's works were translated into more than 140 languages, making him one of the world's most translated authors. His work has been adapted for film and television since the beginning of cinema, as well as for comic books, theater, opera, music, and video games. Verne's influence extends beyond literature and film into the world of science and technology, where he inspired generations of scientists, inventors, and explorers. In 1954 the United States Navy launched the world's first nuclear-powered submarine, named for Verne's *Nautilus*.

2  **Yankee Doodle**

The national hymn of the United States very likely refers to the national anthem of the country. "Yankee Doodle" is a traditional song and nursery rhyme, the early versions of which predate the Seven Years War and American Revolution. It is often sung patriotically in the United States today. It is the state anthem of Connecticut. The melody is thought to be much older than both the lyrics and the subject, going back to folk songs of Medieval Europe.

### Language points

1  For, if the projectile were not discharged that very night at 10h. 48m. 40s. p.m., more than eighteen years must roll by before the moon would again present herself under the same conditions. (Para. 1)

**Meaning:** Because if the projectile had not been launched precisely at 10:48:40 p.m. that night, people would have to wait for another 18 years for the right lunar conditions so the rocket could shoot for it.

2  Every quarter of an hour the railway brought fresh sightseers … not less than five million spectators had flocked to this part of Florida.

**Notes:** The phrase "every quarter of an hour" highlights the repeated occurrence of the railway bringing fresh sightseers. This creates a sense of rapid and continuous movement. The verb "flocked" conjures a vivid and dynamic image of a large number of people gathering or converging in a specific location. It suggests a sense of excitement and energy as people are drawn to this part of Florida.

3  Welcome shouts greeted her on all sides, as her pale beams shone gracefully in the clear sky. (Para. 6)

**Notes:** Across cultures, the moon carries connotations of femininity and is often compared to a goddess. For instance, in Greek mythology, Artemis was the goddess of the moon. In Chinese mythology, Chang'e is the moon goddess. This has led to cultural connotations where the moon is often compared or linked to female attributes.

4  Barbicane, the inventor, cool and collected, was giving his final directions. (Para. 7)

**collected**: *a.* in control of yourself and your thoughts, feelings, etc. 镇定的；泰然自若的

*The chairperson seemed completely cool, calm and collected during the speech.*

5  The moon climbed upward, outshining the stars. (Para. 10)

**outshine**: *vt.* be better or more impressive than sb. or sth. else 优于；使黯然失色

*The young violinist outshone the other musicians at the concert.*

**Notes:** "Out-" is a prefix to go with nouns, adjectives, and verbs. In verbs, it means to become bigger, further, greater, etc. than someone or something else. For example:

*At 5 feet and 8 inches, he had outgrown his clothes.*
*Dying at the age of 82, she outlived her brother.*

6  An immense spout of fire shot up from the bowels of the earth. (Para. 14)

**the bowels of sth.**: the lowest or deepest part of sth. 某物的最深处

*The engine room is deep in the bowels of the ship.*

7  The earth heaved up, and with great difficulty a few spectators caught a brief glimpse of the projectile victoriously cutting the air in the midst of the fiery vapors. (Para. 14)

**Meaning:** The ground surged upwards, and with considerable effort, a small number of onlookers managed to briefly see the projectile triumphantly slicing through the air amidst the fiery fumes.

**Notes:**

1)  In the sentence, "heave up" refers to the sudden upward movement or rise of the earth. It suggests a forceful and noticeable action, as if the ground was lifted or pushed upwards. This movement likely occurred as a result of a powerful event or action taking place.

2)  "The fiery fumes" refers to the gases or smoke that are filled with fire or intense heat. It implies the presence of flames or a burning substance, creating a visual image of smoke or gases that are hot, glowing, or producing fire. The use of "fiery" emphasizes the intense or vibrant nature of the fumes, adding to the dramatic effect of the scene.

8  Men, women, children, all lay prostrate like ears of corn under a storm. (Para. 16)

**ear**: *n.* [C] the top part of a plant such as wheat that produces grain 谷穗

*After the harvest, she picked up the ears of corn left in the field.*

**Notes:** This sentence is a use of metaphor to express the extreme impact of the event on the people. The phrase "like ears of corn under a storm" creates a visualization of how cornfields are flattened by the powerful winds during a storm, suggesting the people too were knocked over forcefully by the earthquake.

9  They looked in vain! (Para. 17)

**Meaning:** However hard they tried, they could not see the rocket because it had gone too far.

**in vain**: without success in spite of your efforts 徒劳；无结果

*In bad need of money, they searched in vain for the missing purse.*

**Reference answers**
1  B
2  A
3  C
4  D
5  C

They can also reflect how the knowledge and skills they learned may influence their beliefs and attitudes regarding space exploration and their ability to communicate about this topic. Ss then can write their specific gains in the self-reflection table in the Student's Book.

## Translation of the texts

## Active reading

星海遨游

1  在失重的太空环境中，水的状态会发生什么样的变化？在中国天宫空间站，"神舟十三号"航天员乘组通过直播课堂为我们揭开了谜底。在"浮力消失"的实验中，王亚平将乒乓球放进装满水的杯子里。乒乓球没有像在地球上那样浮到水面，而是停留在了水杯的中央。她还通过从一个袋子小心翼翼地向金属环中添加水制作出一张水膜。这张水膜看上去就像一个放大镜。接着她将一朵在执行航天任务之前与女儿一同制作的纸折花朵放到了水膜上。折纸花在水上神奇地舒展绽放。"看到这朵花，我就想到了我的女儿，"王亚平对收看天宫课堂的学生们说，"你们都是祖国的花朵，含苞待放。希望你们的梦想都能在这广袤的宇宙中绽放。"

2  实际上，我们通往星际之海的旅程发源于我们远古的梦想。太空探索源于我们对宇宙的渴望，而对于人类历史的大部分时间来说，宇宙都是神秘而遥不可及的。千百年来，人们仰望天空，好奇夜空中闪烁的物体究竟是什么。这样的好奇激发了远古时代的神话传说。例如，在中国，关于天宫（天上的宫殿）、嫦娥（中国的月亮女神）和玉兔（陪伴嫦娥女神的兔子）的美妙故事，在我们的想象中构建了美丽的场景，将我们吸引向星辰大海。

3  到了现代，随着火箭技术、电子及其他科技的发展，探索未知宇宙成为可能，这有助于解答一些根本性的问题，比如：宇宙有多大？它是何时形成的？在我们视力无法到达的地方还存在着什么？现在，我们知道，宇宙的跨度大约有 900 亿光年，它有近 140 亿年的历史。它包含着数千亿个星系，而地球坐落在其中之一中的银河系的外围边缘。

4  太空探测器也帮助我们更多地了解地球家园所在的太阳系。美国航空航天局于 1977 年发射的"旅行者一号"和"旅行者二号"探测器一直在传回关于其他行星的照片和数据。火星探测器，包括以中国神话中火神命名的"祝融号"，帮助人类了解了这颗红色行星的地表及天气等方面的信息。同样，中国"嫦娥五号"任务所带回的月壤样本为分析月球演变提供了新的依据。

5  除了通过发射太空探测器所取得的重要发现，载人航天也为太空探索翻开了新的篇章。当载人飞船克服地球引力，人类活动范围得到拓展，不再局限于陆地、海洋以及大气层。中国宇航员积极投身于航天工业的发展，并在人类太空探索的历史上留下了光辉的篇章。杨利伟乘坐中国的第一艘载人飞船"神舟五号"造访太空。翟志刚则搭乘"神舟七号"升空，成为第一位完成太空漫步的中国宇航员。"神舟十三号"飞行乘组在"天宫空间站"生活和工作了六个月，为长期宇宙航行积累了宝贵的经验。

6  太空探索不仅涉及那些远离地球的事物；它也为我们的日常生活带来了影响深远的益处。太空探索所激发的新型技术极大地推动了其他领域的发展。以在地球轨道中运行的人造卫星为例，它们所提供的通讯、导航和地球观测服务为我们提供了更为详尽的风暴预警和宇宙天气预报。中国独立建造并运行的北斗卫星导航系统，以汉语中的"北斗七星"命名。它被应用于多个领域，满足了重要的国家基础设施需求，并创造了令人瞩目的经济和社会效益。

7  同样，许多最初为航天项目开发的设备在地球上也得到了广泛应用，并深刻改变了我们的日常生活。20 世纪 60 年代用于检测宇航员关键体征的技术被运用于每一所医院。美国航空航天局也利用宇航服技术帮助改进了消防员保护的方法。基于太空任务所使用的机械臂，中国科学家设计出了安全检测机械狗。一旦发现例如像起火这样的危险情况时，它会发动警报并向指挥系统发送报告。

8  因为拥有无限的可能性，太空探索成为了一项丰富和拓展人类未来的伟大事业。在这项创举中，中国正扮演着越来越重要的角色。为了和平利用外太空，中国欢迎其他国家参与在天宫空间站进行的研究项目。毋庸置疑，广袤无垠的宇宙星海将始终是我们追求美好生活以及世界繁荣的信息及灵感之源。

## Further exploration

阿波罗十三号

1  1969 年，"阿波罗十一号"的两名宇航员踏上月球，这大概是人类历史上最著名的登月任务之一。然而，对很多人来说，1970 年的"阿波罗十三号"任务更为惊心动魄。

2  "阿波罗十三号"任务也计划搭载宇航员登陆月球，为后续研究采集土壤和岩石，并探索月球表面。

3  但是这项任务在发射前就遇到了问题。由于最后一刻的人员变动，飞行乘组并非原定人选。在计划发射的前几天，问题就出现了。一名候选宇航员出现了风疹症状，这导致控制舱宇航员也接触了病毒。由于他对这种病毒没有免疫力，不得不临时被替换。

4  祸不单行，火箭发射后两天灾难降临。电路问题导致服务舱发生爆炸，并破坏了两个氧气存储罐。很显然，没有氧气和电是无法完成登月的，宇航员不得不放弃计划。但更令人担忧的是，宇航员们并不确定他们是否能够安全返回地球。

5  在宇航员决定使用登月舱作为求生船之后，他们增添了信心。按照原计划配备的饮水和食物量，登月舱可维持两名宇航员两天的需求。然而，现在他们需要利用这些物资维持三名宇航员四天的生存。除此之外，他们还必须应对极端的寒冷和氧气的匮乏。当宇航员们接近地球大气层时，他们爬回了控制舱，将登月舱丢弃在太空中，并为飞船在太平洋的溅落做好了准备。

6  当宇航员进入地球大气层时，有一段通信中断期，无法与地面指挥中心取得联络。指挥中心的人们陷入了极度的紧张，无法预知宇航员们是否能安全返航。幸运的是，转机出现，这些人成功地将宇宙飞船降落在距离回收舰仅 6.5 公里的海面上。他们疲惫不堪，感到寒冷，但他们都成功地活了下来。

7  "阿波罗十三号"任务吸引了全球数百万人的注意，这段故事也被广为传颂，其中最著名的是 1995 年改编的同名电影。此次登月任务被视作一个"成功的失败"——虽然未能完成原定计划，但飞船乘组克服了几乎难以战胜的困难，最终全体安全返回。

## Extended reading

点火！

1  12 月 1 日终于到了！这是决定性的时刻！因为如果登月发射器没有在当天晚上 10 点 48 分 40 秒发射，那么要等月球再次出现在同样位置，还需要十八年的时间。

2  天气非常好。尽管冬季来临，阳光仍然很明媚。许多人在前一天晚上根本无法入睡！黎明时分，大量人群聚集在位于乱石岗附近的火箭发射地。每隔一刻钟，铁路就会带来新的观光者。据《坦帕观察家报》报道，至少有 500 万观众涌入了佛罗里达州的这一地区。

3  在此前的整整一个月里，人们在这里安营扎寨，用小木屋、草房和帐篷打下了一座城镇的基础。世界上的每一个国家都有代表在那里；同时可以听到用各种语言进行的交谈。美国社会各阶层的人们都聚集在一起，成为了一个整体。

4  用餐时间这里通常非常喧闹。但在 12 月 1 日这一天，四下却很安静。没有人想到吃喝。到了下午四点，许多围观者甚至还没有吃午饭。

5  直到夜幕降临，一种无声的焦虑贯穿人群。每个人都盼着它赶快结束。

6  然而，在大约七点钟时，令人压抑的沉默消失了。月亮从地平线上升起。当她从晴朗的夜空优雅地洒下淡淡的光芒时，四面八方都有欢呼的声音。就在这时，三个旅行者出现了。这是一个信号，让人们再次发出更加激动的呼喊。人们很快唱起了美国国歌，还有"扬基歌"。五百万人的歌唱汇集在一起，直冲云霄。然后，人群再次陷入深深的寂静。

7  法国人阿尔当和两位美国人巴比康和尼科尔这时已经进入了人群中央的场地。陪同他们的是大炮俱乐部的成员，以及欧洲各天文台派来的代表。发明家巴比康，冷静而镇定，正在做最后的指示。同为发明家的尼科尔是巴比康的长期竞争对手。他双手背在身后，步履坚定。法国冒险家阿尔当，则总是很轻松。他穿着宽松的天鹅绒西装，嘴里叼着雪茄，正在一边和朋友开玩笑，一边哈哈大笑。

8  十点钟声响起！他们在发射舱中各就各位。是时候说"再见了！"这一景很感人。

9  片刻之后，三位太空同行者已在发射舱中安顿停当，并用盖板封住了出口。哥伦比亚德这个巨大的发射器，其发射口完全指向天空。

10  月亮向上攀升，光芒盖过了星星。整个场面陷入了可怕的寂静之中！大地上没有一丝风！无数观众都屏住了呼吸！他们的心脏似乎不敢跳动！所有的眼睛都盯哥伦比亚德的发射口。

11  离出发的时刻还有四十秒，但每一秒钟都似乎很漫长！

12  "三十五！- 三十六！- 三十七！- 三十八！- 三十九！- 四十！点火！！！"

13  瞬间，工程师按下了电池按钮，点燃了哥伦比亚德发射器。

14  一声惊天动地的巨响瞬间响起，雷声的轰鸣和火山爆炸都无法与之相提并论，任何语言都无法描述这惊天巨响的分毫！一股巨大的火舌从地底射出。大地猛然一震，一些观众好不容易才在短瞬间看见那枚"炮弹"在炽热的蒸汽中胜利地划破天空！

15  火焰的强光照亮了整个佛罗里达，在一百英里外的海面上也能被看到，多位船长在航海日志中记录了这颗大型流星的出现。

16  发射的同时还发生了地震。从佛罗里达州的地底都能感受到震动。观看者们都跌倒在地！男人、女人、孩子，所有人都像暴风雨中的麦穗一样躺在地上。很多人受了重伤。多达三十万人在一段时间里失去了听力。

17  最初的冲击一过，受伤的、失聪的，然后是整个人群，都在醒来后疯狂地叫喊，响彻云霄："为阿尔当欢呼！为巴比康欢呼！为尼科尔欢呼！"成千上万的人，仰着头，拿着望远镜，向太空望去。他们什么也看不到！搭载着探险家们的发射舱已经飞出了天际。

# Unit 6 Path to prosperity

## Unit overview

This unit focuses on the significance of ending poverty worldwide and China's achievements in targeted poverty alleviation. It aims to help Ss understand the multiple dimensions of poverty in human society and the various measures being taken to tackle the issue. The text in *Active reading* discusses what poverty means, what forms it takes, and how it can be addressed. The text in *Further exploration* introduces some innovative ways of helping the poor and Ss are encouraged to think of more ways for poverty alleviation. The audio then highlights the impact of targeted poverty alleviation policies on the life of a villager named Uncle Li. In *Project*, Ss are asked to give a brief talk on poverty alleviation to an international audience in a workshop and answer their questions. The essay in *Extended reading* tells the story of a young flower farmer who uses live-streaming to make his flower business thrive. By learning this unit, Ss are expected to understand the importance of poverty alleviation in building a shared future for humankind and embrace qualities of empathy, innovation, and courage in tackling complicated social issues.

<!-- 本页为单元结构流程图（第 2 页），按版面自上而下逐框转录 -->

**Scenario**

一个摆脱了绝对贫困的村庄将迎来一批想了解中国扶贫措施的国际访客。这些访客将参与村庄定期举办的扶贫研讨会。学生将在此次研讨会开场时发表一场介绍性演讲。

**Active reading**
(Meeting poverty head-on)
阅读讨论贫困问题的文章，理解贫困的含义、形式以及解决贫困问题的方法，理解解决贫困问题的重要意义。

**Further exploration – Text**
(Global innovations for poverty alleviation)
阅读介绍全球范围内减贫方法的文章，了解多个领域的创新解决方案，激发更多创新性举措以帮助贫困群体。

**Further exploration – Audio**
(A case of China's targeted poverty alleviation)
听音频，了解中国农村贫困家庭如何通过精准扶贫政策获得帮助，认识精准扶贫政策的效果和重要性。

**Project**
讲述扶贫工作的重要性，中国脱贫攻坚战中采取的措施和取得的伟大成就，有效回答访客提问。强调巩固提升脱贫攻坚成果，弘扬脱贫攻坚精神的重要意义。

**Extended reading**
(Live-streaming helped a flower farmer thrive)
阅读关于一个花农通过直播销售花卉，在疫情期间获得商业成功的故事，思考如何通过认识并挖掘自身优势，通过直播销售等创新方法实现个人和农村的经济发展。

**Self-reflection**
回顾整个单元的学习，反思在内容、语言、结构、交际和行为上的收获。

讲述中国新时代脱贫攻坚的伟大成就，认识扶助贫困人口对构建人类命运共同体的多重意义，了解人类扶贫事业的创新策略，培养解决现实社会问题的意识和能力。

<!-- 第 3 页：表格 -->

**本单元课程思政元素备选清单**

| 素材 | 课程思政参考点 | 二十大精神 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Active reading**<br>(Meeting poverty head-on) | • 了解贫困问题的复杂性，如气候变暖、国际局势、种族关系、性别问题等与贫困存在直接或间接关系。<br>• 了解世界多层面贫困状况及减贫干预措施的选择。 | 通过本单元的学习，学生能够深入理解我国经历的具有重大现实意义和深远历史意义的三件大事之一：完成脱贫攻坚、全面建成小康社会的历史任务，实现第一个百年奋斗目标。我国精准扶贫打赢了人类历史上规模最大的脱贫攻坚战，历史性地解决了绝对贫困问题，为全球减贫事业作出了重大贡献。学生要坚定信念，不断学习掌握有关脱贫和社会发展的知识与技能，身体力行，为人类脱贫事业作出自己的贡献。 |
| **Further exploration – Text**<br>(Global innovations for poverty alleviation) | • 了解解决贫困这一世界性难题应集思广益，相互借鉴。<br>• 了解扶贫措施应针对实际情况，精准帮扶，方能达到良好的效果。 | （本列四行合并，内容同上） |
| **Further exploration – Audio**<br>(A case of China's targeted poverty alleviation) | • 了解精准扶贫、精准脱贫基本方略，以及扶贫减贫理论创新和实践创新，思考如何了解中国农村发展现状，心系家国，躬身实践。 | （本列四行合并，内容同上） |
| **Extended reading**<br>(Live-streaming helped a flower farmer thrive) | • 了解直播带货作为一种创新的销售模式，不仅在推动农副产品的销售和群众收入增加方面发挥了重要作用，还对脱贫攻坚工作起到了积极的助力作用。 | （本列四行合并，内容同上） |

## Scenario

The scenario in this unit can be analyzed from the following aspects:
**Setting**: A workshop on poverty alleviation held by a Chinese village
**Topic**: The significance of poverty alleviation and China's measures and achievements in tackling poverty
**Identity**: Speaker – Interns
            Audience – International visitors
**Purpose**: To explain the significance of poverty alleviation in the sustainable development of humankind, inform the audience about China's solutions to the issue, and invite them to ask questions

**Teaching suggestions**
T can help Ss generate ideas about how to give a talk in the workshop by asking them to search online for background information about poverty alleviation in China, the significance of this issue, and its impact on individuals and communities. T should remind students to pay attention to the sources they use and gather statistics and success stories while searching. Then T can ask Ss to work in groups to exchange thoughts on what content they plan to include in their talk, what presentation structure they will use, and anticipate potential questions from the international visitors.

After the tryout, T can ask Ss to reflect on their difficulties in four aspects, i.e., content (how to explain the significance of poverty alleviation and introducing China's poverty alleviation measures and achievements), language (what difficulties they have in expressions and sentence structures), structure (how to organize a talk clearly and logically), and communication (how to handle listeners' questions effectively), and write them down in the table in the Student's Book.

## Active reading

In *Scenario*, Ss are expected to give a talk on the significance of poverty alleviation and China's measures and achievements in tackling poverty. The text is about the global problem of poverty, its significance, and its current status worldwide. It highlights the need to understand poverty from a multidimensional perspective and discusses the efforts and measures taken to alleviate poverty, including health interventions, education policies, and improving living conditions. It can help Ss better understand the theme of the unit, i.e., the strategies for poverty alleviation. Ss can learn the writing technique of improving credibility by using authoritative sources.

### Before you read

**Reference answers**
All the ten indicators should be included because each of them concerns the very basic needs of life in modern times. However, these needs might enjoy different orders of priority. For example, given that life is the most important issue, nutrition and child mortality should be at the top of the list, and assets are slightly less essential than sanitation and drinking water.

**Meeting poverty head-on**

### Text interpretation

本文整体采用说明文体，站在全人类共性的角度，介绍贫困的多维度表现形式及应对方法。文章第一部分（第一段）通过引用查尔斯·狄更斯经典小说《雾都孤儿》中的一段描述，引出主题并突出贫困问题由来已久。作者指出，尽管现代社会在不断发展与进步，贫困仍是一个普遍存在的问题。文章第二部分（第二段至第四段）以联合国所提出的"2030 可持续发展目标"为基础，详细阐述了（页底截断，接下页）联合国设立全球"多维度贫困指数"分析框架的原因（第二段）及方法（第三段），进一步定义了贫困的概念并描绘了贫困在全球范围内的现实情况，这部分辅以贫困家庭实例（第四段），以增加读者理解。第三部分（第五段）介绍了全球各地的发展项目如何通过多种方式帮助贫困人口，如改善卫生条件、加强教育以及改善住房条件等，这些成功案例有助于学生理解解决贫困问题的可能性。文章最后（第六段）指出尽管我们已经取得了一些成功，但贫困问题还可能受到诸如气候变化、全球事件以及种族和性别问题等复杂因素的影响。对于如何在具体背景下找到解决贫困的方案，人类需要勇气和智慧。

从知识层面看，本文介绍了具有国际视野的、可操作性强的贫困多维度分析框架。并通过数据和案例使问题得到清晰的描述。从价值观层面看，文章意图展现同理心、分析能力、实践精神在人类可持续发展中的作用。

作为关于世界性话题的说明文语篇，本文采用了"问题——解决方案"的宏观结构，并采用了内隐的时间顺序，从 19 世纪 30 年代英国社会底层生活，过渡到 21 世纪重新审视气候变化问题、国际政治与贫困之间的复杂关联。为提升内容的可信度和清晰度，作者在讨论贫困主题时，引用了联合国（UN）和世界卫生组织（WHO）等权威信息源。对于全球贫困的现状和如何评价贫困问题，作者引用了联合国多维贫困指数（MPI），以及世界卫生组织关于儿童死亡率降低的数据。教师可结合写作技巧部分的讲解和练习，引导学生分析如何利用权威来源增强内容的可信度，并在产出任务中有效应用。

基于课文，教师可深入挖掘并运用思政教育元素，引导学生思考解决人类贫困问题的创新方法，鼓励学生设想从社会工作、公共政策、商业创新等不同角度提供解决方案。另外，教师还可以带领学生关注其他与贫困相关的全球性问题，引导学生思考贫困问题的复杂性、全球性，并鼓励他们主动融入建设更公平、更美好的世界的过程中。这对培养学生的全球视野和社会责任感具有积极影响。

可以融入课文讲解中的思政案例：
例 1：（第 1 段）从狄更斯笔下 19 世纪孤儿院的情景，分析贫困在人类历史不同历史阶段的普遍存在，以及改善民生对建设现代文明国家的重要意义；
例 2：（第 2 段）查阅消灭贫困在"联合国 2030 可持续发展目标"中的首要地位，理解解决贫困问题对于构建更好的人类未来的意义；
例 3：（第 3-5 段）学习解决现实问题的清晰思路——厘清贫困的多维度表征（见下图），基于人物实例分析贫困成因，最后引出扶贫的多种可行措施。

<!-- 下图（Structure of the global Multidimensional Poverty Index）为图示，按结构还原 -->

**Structure of the global Multidimensional Poverty Index**

Three dimensions of poverty:
- **Health**
  - Nutrition
  - Child mortality
- **Education**
  - Years of schooling
  - School attendance
- **Standard of living**
  - Cooking fuel
  - Sanitation
  - Drinking water
  - Electricity
  - Housing
  - Assets

### Photo interpretation

本单元课文首页图为宁夏回族自治区闽宁镇原隆村。2021 年 8 月，闽宁镇入选第一批全国乡村旅游重点镇（乡）名单。2021 年 9 月，闽宁镇入选"2021 年全国千强镇"。2021 年热播电视剧《山海情》即以闽宁为故事发生地，讲述了村民从搬迁到脱贫的艰辛历程。
教师可以在导入活动中充分利用这张课文首页图，让学生围绕图片讨论问题，引导学生关注我国脱贫攻坚所取得的伟大成就，隐性融入育人元素。比如，教师可以设计以下问题：
- Can you use three adjectives to describe the picture?
- What do you know about the history of Minning Town?
- Which methods adopted by Minning Town in the fight against poverty are worth promoting in other regions across the country?

### Culture notes

1 **Charles Dickens (1812 – 1870)**
Charles Dickens was an English novelist, generally considered the greatest of the Victorian era. His many volumes include such works as *Oliver Twist*, *A Christmas Carol*, *David Copperfield*, *Bleak House*, *A Tale of Two Cities*, *Great Expectations*, and *Our Mutual Friend*. Dickens enjoyed a wider popularity during his lifetime than any previous author.

2 **The 2030 Agenda for Sustainable Development**
Officially known as the Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs) or Global Goals, the 2030 Agenda for Sustainable Development is a collection of 17 interlinked objectives designed to serve as a "shared blueprint for peace and prosperity for people and the planet now and into the future." The SDGs emphasize the interconnected environmental, social, and economic aspects of development by putting sustainability at their center. The SDGs were formulated in 2015 by the United Nations General Assembly (UNGA). Most targets are to be achieved by 2030.

### Language points

1 **Meeting poverty head-on (Title)**
**head-on**: *ad.* if sb. deals with a problem head-on, they do not try to avoid it, but deal with it in a direct and determined way 面对面地；毫不回避地（应对问题）
*We have developed new policies which address the issue head-on.*

**Notes**: Expressions with "head-on" include "tackle a problem head-on," "meet (or face) something head-on," etc. For example:
*Despite the complexity of the situation, the team decided to tackle the problem head-on, demonstrating their commitment and determination.*
*When the company faced unprecedented financial difficulties, the CEO chose to meet the challenge head-on, implementing rigorous cost-cutting measures while seeking new growth opportunities.*

2 **The bowls never wanted washing. The boys polished them with their spoons till they shone again … (Para. 1)**
**Meaning**: There was no need to wash the bowls. The boys were so hungry that they would finish every bit of food, and therefore the bowls would look very clean and shiny …

3 **…, as poverty alleviation demands far more effort than imagined. (Para. 1)**
**Meaning**: It takes more effort than we might have imagined to reduce poverty.

4 **This is why "ending poverty in all its forms everywhere" tops the 2030 Agenda for Sustainable Development. (Para. 2)**
**top the agenda / list / chart**, *etc.*: come first in a list or in an order of things 在议程、名单、列表等上面排第一位
*The urgent matter of cost-cutting has topped the agenda, prompting the management to brainstorm innovative ways to streamline expenses.*

5 **… to build a more just future for humanity, … (Para. 2)**
**just**: *a.* morally right and fair 公正的；正义的
*There have been leaders pushing to make health care a more just space.*

**Notes**: Synonyms for "humanity" include "mankind," "humankind," "the human race," etc.

6 **…, let us first define the concept of poverty and map the reality on a global scale. (Para. 3)**
**map**: *vt.* discover or show information about sth., esp. about its shape or arrangement, or how it moves or works 描述；呈现
*It brings the technical assistance necessary to map the future that can hopefully change the community.*

7 **For health, nutrition and child mortality are the major considerations, … (Para. 3)**
**mortality**: *n.* [U] (also ~ rate) the number of deaths during a particular period of time among a particular type or group of people 死亡数；死亡率
*The virus led to an appalling high infant mortality.*

**Notes**: The adjective form of "mortality" is "mortal," which means "not able to live forever." The antonym of "mortal" is "immortal," meaning "living or continuing forever."

8 **A person's deprivation score is the sum of the weighted deprivations they experience. (Para. 3)**
**deprivation**: *n.* [C, usu. pl., U] the lack of sth. that you need in order to be healthy, comfortable, or happy 缺少；匮乏；贫困
*Research has shown that sleep deprivation can result in mental disorders.*

**Notes**: Words that collocate with "deprivation" often reflect the necessary aspects of life that someone might be lacking or denied. For example, "sleep deprivation," "nutrient deprivation," "deprivation of rights," and "economic deprivation."

9 **Although they are sometimes food insecure, they are not deprived of nutrition. (Para. 4)**
**insecure**: *a.*
1) capable of being lost or taken from you at any time 无保障的；不可靠的；不稳定的
*Many families in the poverty-stricken region are food insecure, struggling to access and afford adequate nutritious food.*
2) not confident about yourself 无把握的；不自信的
*Despite the success his business was experiencing, John felt insecure about its future, knowing that it could potentially be lost in the ever-changing market trends.*

**Notes**: The noun form of "insecure" is "insecurity." Adjectives that often go before "insecurity" are "deep," "great," "growing," "increased," "general," "financial," etc. The preposition often used before "insecurity" is "about." The word is also often used in phrases, e.g. "a feeling / sense of insecurity."

10 **…, with access to affordable interventions in health and sanitation, child mortality in under-fives worldwide has been reduced dramatically, … (Para. 5)**
**Meaning**: Thanks to the practical measures, which are not too costly, to improve health and sanitation conditions, the global child mortality rate for children under the age of five has greatly decreased.

**Intervention**: *n.* [C, U] the act of becoming involved in an argument, fight, or other difficult situation in order to change what happens 干涉；干预；介入（某种困难处境）
*According to the doctors, surgical interventions are needed in this type of emergency.*

11 **…, relocating poverty-stricken people away from inhospitable areas can be an effective way to improve their lives. (Para. 5)**
**Meaning**: Moving people who are living in extreme poverty away from areas that are not conducive to human life (for instance, areas with harsh climates, food scarcity, or deficient basic services such as water, health care, and education) could improve their living conditions and overall well-being.

**inhospitable**: *a.* an inhospitable place is unpleasant or difficult to visit or live in 不适宜居住的；荒凉的
*The Sahara is one of the world's most inhospitable regions.*

**Notes**: The antonym of the word "inhospitable" is "hospitable", which is used to describe an environment in which things can grow. For example:
*The coastal town's hospitable climate and welcoming community make it an ideal place to retire.*
The word "hospitable" also means "friendly, welcoming, and generous to visitors." For example:
*The hotel is run by a hospitable couple who are from Sichuan.*

12 **… the fight against poverty must be integrated with strategies that build economic growth and address social needs. (Para. 6)**
**Meaning**: Efforts to combat poverty should be combined with approaches that foster economic development and meet societal requirements. Society should not only focus on helping the poor financially in the short-term but also create long-term solutions such as job opportunities, education, healthcare, etc., that would nourish economic prosperity and social welfare, which can sustainably lift people out of poverty.

13 **This is no easy task, but it is a cause that we, with the aim of building a human community with a shared future, need to fight for. (Para. 6)**
**Meaning**: The goal of alleviating poverty, promoting economic growth, and addressing social needs is difficult to achieve, but it's an important endeavor we must undertake in order to build a shared, inclusive, and prosperous future for the entire human community.

**Notes**: In this sentence, "This is no easy task" is a common way of expressing that something is difficult or challenging. For example:
*Implementing solutions for climate change is no easy task, but it's a crucial responsibility we owe to future generations.*

1
**Teaching suggestions**
T may guide Ss to relate the two key words "courage" and "wisdom" to the issue of poverty alleviation. Ss could start by considering the definition of each word. Then they could illustrate the qualities embedded in these two words. For example, "courage" may stand for the willingness and determination to confront difficult problems, and "wisdom" could be explained as the awareness of the complicated nature of a social issue and the practical tactics when tackling it. Finally, T could encourage Ss to use facts and examples regarding the poverty issue to support their own understanding.

**Reference answers**
Addressing poverty requires individuals and policymakers to possess both courage and wisdom. To figure out effective solutions, they need the courage to face the challenging issues related to health, education, and living standards, as well as the factors that complicate the issue of poverty, such as climate change, world events, and issues relating to race and gender. This may involve taking bold action and making tough decisions. Additionally, wisdom is needed to make informed and well-thought-out choices that consider the specific factors influencing poverty in a specific context, such as nutrition, child mortality, schooling, access to basic necessities, and overall living conditions. The combination of courage and wisdom enables a comprehensive understanding of the complex factors contributing to poverty and helps in formulating appropriate strategies to combat it.

2
**Reference answers**
Relocation can contribute to poverty alleviation in several ways:
- Access to better opportunities: Relocation often means moving from rural areas to urban or semi-urban regions where there are often more job opportunities. This can assist individuals to secure steady employment and increase their income.
- Improved infrastructure: Relocating to areas with better public infrastructure can yield access to quality health care, education, sanitation facilities, and housing, all of which can significantly improve one's quality of life.
- Skill development: More developed areas can provide access to institutions for skill development. People can thereby improve their employment prospects and ability to earn a better income.
- Access to social services: In many cases, social services are more readily available or accessible in urban areas or regions being targeted for poverty alleviation. This can include things like food assistance, childcare assistance, educational grants, and more.

### Analyzing writing techniques

**Teaching suggestions**
Using the main types of reliable sources from the Student's Book, T can give some examples for each type. If possible, T can also compare the information from different sources to make sure they understand the differences. Explain to Ss why the source of information may make a big difference when used in one's writing or speech. Then Ss check the sources of information in the text and comment on their credibility.

**Reference answers**
1
The author uses several authoritative sources:

The quote from Charles Dickens' novel *Oliver Twist* is used to detail the living conditions of poverty-stricken children in the 19th century. This provides a historical perspective of poverty.

The United Nations (UN): This source is used multiple times throughout the text to establish some key statistics related to global poverty. For instance, it's stated that 1.2 billion people still live in acute multidimensional poverty. The UN is also referenced when explaining the global Multidimensional Poverty Index (MPI), which helps define and measure poverty in three key dimensions: health, education, and standard of living. In addition, the details of the 2030 Agenda for Sustainable Development serve to highlight poverty eradication as a primary goal.

The anecdotal account of Nyawala, a refugee from southern Sudan, serves as a real-life example to provide an insight into the reality of multidimensional poverty. Although it may not seem like an "authoritative source," such real-life situations help authenticate the argument being made by bringing in human interest and personal connection to the topic of poverty.

The World Health Organization (WHO): The text cites WHO saying that meaningful interventions in health and sanitation have significantly reduced child mortality rates over the last three decades. This supports the statement about the effectiveness of development projects across various dimensions to tackle poverty.

2
Given the subject of the talk and the context of China's achievements in this field, here are some potential sources that may prove helpful:

United Nations Development Programme (UNDP): The UNDP provides numerous reports and data dealing with poverty alleviation globally and can offer detailed studies concerning China's advancements.

The World Bank: This organization produces a lot of research on global poverty and has documented China's efforts and success in lifting hundreds of millions of people out of poverty.

National Bureau of Statistics of China: This agency provides comprehensive and updated statistics about various social and economic aspects in China, which include poverty reduction.

The State Council's White Papers on Poverty Alleviation: China's State Council periodically releases white papers on different topics. The white papers on poverty eradication can provide a comprehensive overview of China's strategies, measures, and achievements as told by the Chinese government.

Academic and research papers: Scholarly research articles from reputable journals provide comprehensive analyses and studies about China's strategies regarding poverty alleviation, their impact, and the challenges faced.

### Building your language

1 - c
2 - e

**Making sentences**
1 **flee a crisis**: Many families were forced to flee a crisis in their home country due to political instability.
2 **demand far more effort**: Achieving sustainable development and poverty alleviation will demand far more effort from both government entities and private sectors.
3 **be enrolled in a school**: Due to the government's efforts, a significant number of children from impoverished families are now being enrolled in a school, shifting their life's trajectory toward a more promising future.
4 **get access to flush toilets**: As part of the poverty alleviation initiative, rural households in underprivileged regions have been able to get access to flush toilets, dramatically improving their living standards and hygiene conditions.
5 **be deprived of nutrition**: Before the poverty alleviation measures were implemented, many impoverished children in rural areas were sadly being deprived of nutrition, but comprehensive social programs have since greatly improved the situation.
6 **be complicated by other factors**: The success of a marketing campaign can be complicated by other factors like consumer preferences, market competition, and unpredictable external events.

3
The late 20th century witnessed rapid development in China's big cities, but poverty remained a problem in some remote rural areas. To win the battle against poverty, achieve common prosperity for all, and advance the great rejuvenation of the Chinese nation on all fronts, China has taken a series of targeted poverty alleviation measures, through which the incomes and welfare of the poor have improved dramatically. Close to 100 million poor rural residents have been lifted out of poverty, and, among them, more than 9.6 million poverty-stricken people have been relocated from inhospitable areas. China has resolved the problem of absolute poverty, making significant contributions to the cause of global poverty reduction and building a human community with a shared future.

### Sharing your ideas

**Reference answers**
Today, according to the United Nations (UN), 1.2 billion people still live in acute multidimensional poverty. It restricts individuals from basic necessities, inhibits potential growth, and promotes socioeconomic disparity, impacting societal stability. Hence, alleviating poverty is a vital step toward building a human community with a shared future. By ensuring a fair distribution of resources, poverty alleviation not only unlocks individual potential but also fosters opportunities for broader economic growth. Conclusively, eliminating poverty is a paramount necessity for global development and peace, contributing significantly to achieving the global Sustainable Development Goals.

**Suggestions for evaluation**
For this activity, Ss' discussion can be evaluated according to the following criteria:
- whether Ss describe clearly the current situation of poverty and its influence
- whether Ss explain convincingly the significance of poverty alleviation

## Further exploration

The text in *Active reading* helped Ss understand the multiple dimensions of poverty and some conventional measures to tackle it on a global scale. Given that poverty alleviation is an ongoing process and the strategies very often need to be adapted to the local contexts, the text in this part helps Ss better understand poverty alleviation measures that have proved successful in other parts of the world. The audio in this part introduces an interview of a Chinese villager who talks about the changes his home village has witnessed as a result of targeted poverty alleviation. Materials in this part will not only expand the vision of Ss about ways of tackling poverty, but also prepare them to confidently address questions posed by workshop participants, by using the cause-effect analysis skills. T can further encourage Ss to reflect on the significance of poverty alleviation efforts and the impact they can make on individuals and societies.

**Global innovations for poverty alleviation (Text)**

### Culture notes

**Doctors Without Borders**
Doctors Without Borders, also known as Médecins Sans Frontières (MSF) in French, is a humanitarian organization that provides emergency medical assistance to people affected by conflicts, epidemics, natural disasters, and exclusion from health care. It was founded in 1971 in France by a group of doctors and journalists in the wake of war and famine in Biafra, Nigeria. Their aim was to establish an independent organization that focused on delivering emergency medical humanitarian aid quickly, effectively, and impartially. Over the past five decades, MSF has grown from a group of a few hundred volunteers to an international movement of more than 63,000 staff providing over 10 million medical consultations in more than 70 countries every year.

### Language points

1 **… the Hippo Roller improves people's access to water sources. (Para. 2)**
**Notes**: The Hippo Roller is named after this large African animal (Hippo) mostly for its shape and size as a water container (as shown in the picture below). It is a clever invention since the round barrel could serve as a wheel at the same time.

<!-- 原图此处为图片：村民推着 Hippo Roller 取水的照片 -->

2 **Another innovative water solution is the solar pump, which enables farmers in remote locations to pump water to their crops using sunlight as an energy source. (Para. 2)**
**Meaning**: Another creative way of transporting water is the solar pump, which turns sunlight into an energy source. (Check the illustration below)

<!-- 原图此处为图片：太阳能水泵（solar pump）示意图 -->

3 **For poor children who suffer from malnutrition, Plumpy'Nut offers possible solution. (Para. 3)**
**malnutrition**: *n.* [U] a poor condition of health caused by a lack of food or a lack of the right type of food 营养不良
*If malnutrition worsens, their hair becomes fragile and falls out.*

**Notes**:
1) The prefix "mal-" usually signifies something bad, wrong, inadequate, or abnormal when attached to a root word. So words like "malpractice (improper or illegal professional activity or treatment)," "malfunction (failure to function normally)," and "malnourished (insufficiently or improperly nourished)" are all examples where "mal-" indicates a negative or adverse meaning.
2) Plumpy'Nut is a therapeutic food product that is used to treat severe acute malnutrition in children. It is a ready-to-use paste made from a combination of peanuts, milk powder, sugar, vegetable oils, and a mix of vitamins and minerals. Plumpy'Nut is designed to provide a high-calorie, nutrient-dense source of food that does not require refrigeration or cooking, making it highly effective for use in emergency situations or resource-limited settings. Plumpy'Nut has been widely recognized and utilized by humanitarian organizations and has played a significant role in the fight against malnutrition globally.

4 **Apart from water and food, housing also plays a critical role in providing stability to poor families. (Para. 4)**
**apart from**: *prep.* as well as 除了……还
*Apart from his earnings as a tennis coach, he also runs a sports shop.*

5 **…, providing microloans that enable people to repair, renovate, and complete their houses. (Para. 4)**
**microloan**: *n.* [C] a small amount of money that is lent at a low rate of interest to a new business 小额贷款
*They specialize in providing micro-loans to the very poor to start small businesses.*

**renovate**: *vt.* repair a building or old furniture so that it is in good condition again 修复；翻新
*To welcome its new tenants, the apartment has been entirely renovated.*

**Notes**: The prefix "micro-" means something that is extremely small or microscopic in size. It is often used in scientific terms and measurements, and it can go with nouns, adjectives, and adverbs. Some common nouns include "microphone," "microscope," "microelectronics," etc.

For a piglet to grow big enough for the market, the children and their family would have to take good care of it all year round, and their hard work will pay off in the end. This may boost the kids' self-esteem and their confidence to strive for a better life through hard work.

Livestreaming marketing has been helping many farmers find effective distribution channels on the internet. Sometimes a whole village may benefit from the successful sales of their produce.

Mobile banking: In regions where banking infrastructure is lacking, mobile banking offers a path for financial inclusion. Services like M-Pesa in Kenya have opened access to safe, secure, and cheap financial services enabling savings, loans, money transfers, and insurance.

Microinsurance: Low-cost insurance plans protect the vulnerable against certain risks (illness, death, crop failure) to avoid spiraling deeper into poverty.

Digital education: Online platforms providing free, quality education help to bridge the education gap. For example, Khan Academy offers a broad range of subjects that can be accessed by anyone with internet access.

Agricultural innovations: Drought-resistant crops, precision farming techniques, and bio-composting innovations can increase food security and limit risk for farmers.

**A case of China's targeted poverty relief (Audio)**

### Script

J=Journalist;   I=Interviewee

J: Uncle Li, what was your life in the village like before?

I: It was hard. Before 2013, my family didn't have reliable access to electricity and I couldn't afford my children's schooling. My wife and I each made no more than 1,700 yuan in a whole year.

J: That can't have been easy! So what happened in 2013?

I: That was when the targeted poverty alleviation policy was proposed. In the following year, all the households in the village were assessed and our living conditions were recorded. Officials came to see whether we lacked food, shelter, or work and whether the children were in school. That way, poor families in need of help could be identified.

J: What kind of help did you get?

I: We received help tailored to our needs. For example, some old houses were repaired, and we have a constant electricity supply now. With the newly built roads, our produce could be taken to markets for sale. And we received loans for starting small businesses. Some of us seized the opportunities offered by e-commerce. We started to grow kiwi fruit and sell them online, which has been really successful. Others who have practical skills, such as embroidery, were trained to make commercially profitable goods. Old people who were living alone and in poor health received benefits, too.

J: That is really impressive! Are you satisfied with your life now?

I: Yes, I am. I earned about 18,000 yuan last year. That would have been unthinkable before. My family has rebuilt our house so now it is more comfortable to live in. My children are in school. I hope I can continue to provide a better life for my family. And I hope all my children will go to college!

J: Wow, I am really happy for you and your family! By the way, did the officials ever evaluate the outcome of the poverty alleviation project in your village?

I: Yes, they did. The government sent an evaluation team to the village. They assessed the welfare of every family as well as the whole village to get an accurate picture of the results of the project. Plans for further assistance were proposed.

J: Thank you for sharing your story. Best wishes to you and your family!

### Language points

1  ... all the **households** in the village were assessed ...

**household:**

1) n. [C] all the people who live together in one house  一家人；同住一幢房子的人
At that time, not many households had telephones.
2) (only before noun) relating to looking after a house and the people in it  家庭的；家用的；家务的
Today, I deep-cleaned my house using a variety of household products, such as dish soap, laundry detergent, and a multi-purpose cleaner.

2  We received help **tailored** to our needs.

**tailor:** vt. make sth. so that it is exactly right for sb.'s particular needs or for a particular purpose  根据特定需要制作；定做
In the years to come, they will tailor the show for younger audiences.

3  We started to grow kiwi fruit and sell them online.

**Notes:** In English, "fruit" can be both singular and plural when referring to various types of fruit collectively or to fruit in general. However, "fruits" is used when speaking about different kinds of fruit distinctly or individually. For instance, "I like to eat a lot of fruit" means fruit in general. "I enjoyed the different fruits on my trip to the tropics" refers to different kinds of fruit.

4  They assessed the welfare of every family as well as the whole village to get an accurate picture of the results of the project.

（页底截断，接下页）

（接上页）**Meaning:** A detailed assessment has been performed to accurately understand the impact of the poverty alleviation policy. Thus, they are trying to see how effective the policy was by analyzing its outcome at both micro (individual families) and macro (whole village) levels.

**Reference answers**

1

1) electricity
2) schooling
3) 1,700 yuan
4) assessed
5) needs
6) 18,000 yuan
7) in school

2

1  It refers to targeted measures to ensure that assistance reaches poverty-stricken villages and households, particularly those tailored to the needs of poor families. It is a concept opposite to extensive assistance to the rural areas. The targeted poverty alleviation policy involves accurate identification, precise assistance, and specific management of the poverty alleviation work.

2  Actions include assessment of household living conditions, identification of specific needs, and assistance tailored to villagers' needs, such as road construction, housing renovation, training in farming or handicrafts combined with e-commerce, etc.

3  The government sent an evaluation team to the village. They assessed the welfare of every family as well as the whole village to get an accurate picture of the results of the project. Plans for further assistance were proposed.

4  It helps the truly needy rural families in poverty-stricken areas, upgrades the welfare of the rural population, boosts the national economy, promotes social stability, sets examples of Chinese solutions to a common problem worldwide, and fosters a better-shared future for mankind.

### Sharing your ideas

**Reference answers**

China's poverty alleviation efforts and its significant achievements are recognized internationally. Here are some strategies it has implemented:

- Targeted poverty alleviation: The government identifies the root causes of poverty for each family and formulates tailored poverty alleviation plans accordingly.
- Infrastructure development: By improving infrastructure such as transportation, irrigation, and energy facilities, it's easier for goods to reach markets, for services to be provided, and for businesses to flourish.
- Agricultural assistance: The government boosts the agricultural sector through techniques, such as providing quality seeds, modern farming equipment, and training on innovative farming methods.
- Education programs: To break the cycle of poverty, substantial investments are made in education, particularly in rural areas. The measures include waiving tuition fees, providing subsidies, and building better schools.
- Healthcare coverage: Expanding healthcare coverage helps protect people from health-related impoverishment. Providing affordable and good-quality healthcare services reduces the financial pressure on poor families.
- Relocation: For regions with harsh geographical or climatic conditions, people are relocated to areas with better livelihood conditions.
- Job creation: The government stimulates local economies in poverty-stricken areas to create job opportunities. It includes the development of rural businesses based on local resources.

（接上页）- Social security programs: Throughout the country, the social security system has been improved. More residents, specifically those in rural areas, get coverage for pension and medical insurance.

The benefits of these strategies are numerous:

- Better access to education improves literacy rates and employment prospects.
- Improved health care keeps individuals and families healthier, thus, enhancing their productive capabilities.
- Infrastructure development assists in the economic growth of regions.
- With job creation and business support, more people are able to earn a decent living.
- Agricultural support improves food security, boosts income for farmers, and contributes to the rural economy.
- Social security coverage helps alleviate the financial pressure on the most vulnerable and provides aid in times of need.
- The relocation strategy leads to improved living conditions for many families.

**Suggestions for evaluation**

For this activity, Ss' list and discussion plan can be evaluated according to the following criteria:

- whether the list covers the strategies that Ss learned in this section and from other sources
- whether Ss give a clear statement of the possible benefits of the strategies

## Project

**Sample:**

(*Words and expressions in red are from the input materials.)

Good afternoon, everyone! Welcome to this workshop held in our village. My name is Li Hua, a sophomore on my summer internship. It is my honor to speak here today! Let me first tell you a little bit about the village itself. Its origin can date back to Qing Dynasty. By the early 2000s, dozens of settlements around this mountainous area were combined into one village, which has been well-known for its Miao ethnic tradition. The villagers used to live at the bottom of the mountains, but natural disasters such as landslides forced them to move uphill. So now we are here at an altitude of 700 meters. Living in the mountains often hinders the economic well-being of the villagers. We are here today to find out how those disadvantages were offset and how poverty alleviation measures helped change the lives of the villagers.

Poverty is always an important issue in human society. Given that 1.2 billion people still live in acute multidimensional poverty all over the world, ending poverty remains the top goal in the UN 2030 Agenda for Sustainable Development, and it is an essential starting point for building a more just future for mankind. To better tackle the problem, the UN has created a framework for identifying poverty, breaking it into three dimensions containing 10 indicators. We now measure poverty not just by income, but by considering many aspects of a person's well-being. These include health, education, and living conditions. And because of that, various measures have been taken to help the poor, targeted at one or more dimensions of their well-being.

Here in China, we have also figured out some solutions to rural poverty by tailoring the assistance to the specific needs of poor households. We call it "targeted poverty alleviation." As in this village, the project started in 2014, with a detailed assessment of all the households. Officials came to see whether each family lacked food, shelter, or work and whether the children were in school. Then aids were provided accordingly. Old houses were repaired, and a constant electricity supply was provided. To improve connection with the outside world, new roads were built so the villagers could take their produce to the markets. They also received loans for starting small businesses like kiwi fruit farming, or training in handicrafts such as embroidery so everybody could make some commercially profitable goods. With the booming of e-commerce and tourism, the income of the villagers dramatically improved, and the village shook off poverty in 2016. In 2022, the average annual income reached 18,000 yuan.

In order to consolidate the achievements of the poverty alleviation project, the government sent an evaluation team to the village, so the outcome could be accurately assessed.

Now, I wonder if you have any questions. I would be glad to answer them.

**Suggestions for evaluation**

| Components | Criteria / Traits |
| --- | --- |
| Content | • Relevance: Addressing the assigned task by providing information about the significance of poverty alleviation and China's measures and achievements in tackling poverty<br>• Reasoning: Using well-researched, specific examples, better with citation of sources<br>• Appeal: Including up-to-date information, being informative and educational |
| Organization | • Structure: Including well-organized introduction, body, and conclusion<br>• Coherence: Using transitional devices to ensure coherence and unity |
| Language | • Diversity: Using an adequate range of vocabulary and sentence structures<br>• Acquisition: Using as many as possible of the new words, expressions, and grammatical structures learned in the unit |
| Delivery | • Fluency: Presenting in a smooth and clear way with a natural colloquial flow<br>• Engagement: Using gestures and eye contact to engage the audience; arousing interaction<br>• Visual aids: Using slides, pictures, etc. to help the audience better understand the ideas |

## Extended reading

In the previous parts, Ss have learned about the multiple dimensions of poverty and a variety of measures taken by governments and institutions to help the poor. They have also heard a middle-aged Chinese villager talking about the changes he experienced thanks to the targeted poverty alleviation policy. What about the younger generation, and those whose lives were greatly affected by the pandemic? In the *Extended reading* text, Ss will read about an inspiring story of a young flower farmer from Yunnan who struggled and then flourished over a highly challenging time. This hopefully will inspire the Ss to ponder on how multiple social issues may relate to each other, and how they will confront challenges in their own lives in the future.

**Live-streaming helped a flower farmer thrive**

### Language points

1  Every day he rose early and **tended to** the blossoms ... (Para. 1)

**tend to sb. / sth.:** look after sb. or sth.  照看（照料）某人或某物
One of the parents came down to tend to the baby.

**blossom:** n. [C, U] a flower or the flowers on a tree or bush  花；花簇
By early April, the apple tree will be covered in blossom.

2  Without any way to carry out **deliveries**, Li watched as his flowers dropped in price and still couldn't be sold. (Para. 2)

**delivery:** n. [C, U] the act of bringing goods, letters, etc. to a particular person or place, or the things that are brought  递送；交付；递送的物品
Upon delivery of the new furniture, the living room took on a cozier atmosphere.

**watch:** vi. to keep sb. or sth. under close observation  密切关注
The security guard was instructed to watch the surveillance cameras closely to ensure the safety of the building.

**Notes:** Verbs that go well with "delivery" include "arrange," "schedule," "track," "confirm," etc. For example:
They decided to schedule the delivery for next Tuesday.
With the tracking number, you can track your delivery online.

3  In the end, tens of thousands of blossoms **spoiled**. (Para. 2)

**spoil:** vi. start to decay  （开始）变质，变坏，腐败
If you leave the fruit in the sun for too long, it will spoil.

4  Weeks passed before he received a message from an old friend ... Did Li want to join in? (Para. 3)

**Notes:** The two sentences are found at the beginning and the end of Paragraph 3, with other sentences in between explaining the larger background of the message. The question "did Li want to join in" not only sets the stage for the potential transformation of Li's business but also invites readers to anticipate what his response might be. This question, as well as the detail about the company's offer, thus serves as a narrative device to engage the readers' interest and keep them invested in the story's unfolding.

（接上页）5  Growers who had once sold 90 percent of their products offline have now **flipped** to selling 90 percent online. (Para. 5)

**flip** v. move sth. with a quick sudden movement so that it is in a different position  （使）快速翻转
He had to flip the pancake skillfully to ensure it was cooked evenly on both sides.

6  Li and his friend were **on board**. The first week of live-streaming was largely a blur. (Para. 6)

**Meaning:** Li and his friend were enthusiastic about live-streaming. The first week of their live-streaming experience went so fast that it was hard to clearly recall or make sense of it.

**on board:** involved with sth. or working for an organization  参与（某事）的；为（某组织）工作的
He came on board in his thirties and spent two decades with the agency.

7  ... he knew they were **onto something**. (Para. 6)

**be onto something:** have discovered or produced sth. new and interesting  发现新事物；制作出有趣的事物
Despite initial challenges in her research, she kept working and eventually felt she was onto something significant.

8  More orders came **flooding in**, and Li began to gain his own following. (Para. 7)

**Meaning:** A large number of orders started coming in quickly and in great volumes, like a flood, and Li started to attract a group of people who appreciated his work.

**flood in:** arrive or go somewhere in large numbers  大量涌入
Donations have been flooding in since we launched the project.

9  ... the flowers he sold were a rare **breed** ... (Para. 8)

**breed:** n. [C] a type of animal or plant that is kept as a pet or on a farm  （动植物）品种
She and her friends favor different breeds of dogs for pets.

10  He downplays the figure and admires the influencers who have orders of magnitude more followers than he has. All the same, he's hooked. (Para. 9)

**Meaning:** He looks up to the popular influencers who have many more followers than he does. But even though he knows this, he still really likes what he is doing and can't get enough of it.

**Notes:**

1) The phrase "orders of magnitude" is used to express a significant difference between two quantities or states. For example:
   The computer processing power of today is orders of magnitude greater than it was just a decade ago.
2) The phrase "all the same" means despite the previous statement or situation, it doesn't change the fact that Li is still hooked or deeply engrossed in his own pursuits. For example:
   Despite the rain, Sarah decided to go for a run. She knew it would be difficult, but she was determined to stay fit and healthy all the same.

**Reference answers**

a-4
b-3
c-8
d-9
e-7
f-5
g-6
h-1
i-2
j-8

## Translation of the texts

## Active reading

**直面全球贫困问题**

1  "这些碗从来不用洗。孩子们用汤匙把它们刮得闪闪发亮；刮完碗，他们仍坐在那里，眼巴巴地盯着那口铜锅，恨不得把刮锅灶的砖头也吞下去。"这是查尔斯·狄更斯的小说《雾都孤儿》中，19 世纪一所孤儿院里的情景。这段文字生动地描述了旧时代贫困儿童的悲惨生活。这样的场景比较符合我们的心理预期，因为它来自一个人们依靠马和马车出行的时代，那时蒸汽机仍是一种尖端技术。然而，差不多过了 200 年，尽管社会不断发展，人类已经拥有了人工智能和载人航天等技术，但贫困仍然是一个普遍存在的问题，因为扶贫所需要的努力远超普通人的想象。

2  目前，根据联合国的数据，全球仍有 12 亿人生活在严重的、不同形式的贫困中，其中大约一半是儿童及 18 岁以下的青少年。这个问题与影响人类福祉的许多其他问题相关。这就是为什么在联合国 2030 年可持续发展议程中，"终止全世界一切形式的贫困"被列为首要任务。也就是说，想要为人类建立一个更公正的未来，解决贫困问题是首要的出发点。

3  在找到解决方案之前，让我们首先定义贫困的概念，并描绘全球范围内的贫困现状。联合国创建了全球多维贫困指数（MPI），旨在通过健康、教育和生活水平三个维度的 10 个指标来评估资源的短缺情况。这三个维度的权重相同，因此每个维度的权重为 1/3。在每个维度中，所有指标的权重也是相等的。在健康方面，营养和儿童死亡率是主要的考虑因素，每个因素的权重为 1/6。在教育方面，受教育的年数和入学率作为衡量指标各获得 1/6 的权重。对于生活水平，评估的因素为烹饪燃料、卫生设施、饮用水、电力、住房和资产，每一项均得到 1/18 的权重。换句话说，多维贫困不仅仅是指生活在低收入中，而是指无法拥有被认为是基本生活必需品的东西。一个人的贫困得分是指他所经历的各种资源匮乏的权重总和。在全球多维贫困指数框架中，如果一个人的贫困得分达到或超过 1/3（33.3%），那么他就会被认定为遭受着多方面的贫困。

4  通过观察一个个体案例，我们可以更好地理解这个问题。52 岁的纳瓦拉是一名从南苏丹一次危机中逃离的难民，她居住在乌干达北部的一个难民营中。纳瓦拉和她 9 岁的孙女现在住在一间没有铺地板、墙壁的房子里，有两张床、一盏太阳能灯和一个由低成本太阳能板充电的电源插座。每天，安置点的妇女都要步行 15 分钟到一个公用的自流井取水。厕所是与其他八个家庭共用的。她们用柴火烧饭。虽然她们偶尔会遇到食物短缺，但没有发生营养不良。纳瓦拉祖孙俩没有什么财物，但她们很庆幸能有一部手机和一盏太阳能灯。由于动荡，小姑娘错过了几年的学业。纳瓦拉希望能够尽快让孩子进入小学，这样孙女就能赶上并达到她自己没有机会享受的教育水平。根据上述条件，纳瓦拉和孙女被认为符合多维贫困的标准，因为在 10 个指标中她们有 7 个都未达标，在本案例中这相当于 61.1% 的贫困得分。

5  基于对贫困的多个维度的理解，世界各地的开发项目旨在以多种不同方式帮助穷人。根据世界卫生组织的数据，在经济能够承担的情况下，实施健康、卫生干预措施后，全世界五岁以下儿童的死亡率已大幅下降，从 1990 年到 2020 年的 30 年间，每年的死亡人数从 1260 万下降到了 500 万。

（接上页）至于入学率，义务教育法在各个国家都发挥着重要作用。这些政策有助于最大限度地减少城市和农村地区之间的教育差异，减少因经济压力而辍学的儿童人数，并提高人口的总体教育水平。关于对一个人的生活水平有重大影响的住房问题，将贫困人口迁离荒凉地区可以成为改善他们生活的有效途径。易地搬迁后，他们往往可以获得自来水、冲水马桶和许多其他必要的生活设施。

6  尽管上述扶贫策略取得了成功，但气候变化、国际关系以及与种族和性别有关的问题会使贫困问题进一步复杂化。全球社会所有共识，要想取得消除贫困的胜利，必须将其与经济增长、解决社会需求的战略相结合。想要针对具体情境，找到解决方案，人类既需要勇气也需要智慧。这不是一项简单的任务，而是我们为建设人类命运共同体而为之奋斗的事业。

## Further exploration

**全球扶贫创新**

1  贫困有多种形式，世界不同地区的扶贫战略也有所不同。以下是一些例子，希望能激发出更多帮助贫困人群的新想法。

2  在严重缺水的地区，"河马滚桶"（Hippo Roller）改善了人们获取水源的途径。它是一个桶状的容器，可以利用内置的滚轴，便于在地面上拖动。对于那些不得不拿着沉重的罐子走很远的路去取水的人来说，这个设备很容易移动。另一个创新性的取水方式是太阳能泵，它帮助偏远地区的农民利用太阳能抽水灌溉。由于这项技术，印度的许多农民摆脱了贫困。

3  对于患有营养不良的贫困儿童来说，"胖胖坚果"（Plumpy'Nut）提供了一个可能的解决方案。它是一种即食食品，由花生酱、糖、植物油、奶粉制成，并富含维生素和矿物质。它不需要加水或烹饪，一天的供应量只需花费一美元。人道主义组织如"无国界医生"已经使用"胖胖坚果"来救治非洲严重营养不良的儿童。

4  除了水和食物，住房对贫困家庭的生活稳定也起着关键作用。在坦桑尼亚，一个组织正在通过建造适宜的、便宜的房屋来缓解住房困难，从而改善低收入家庭的生活水平。该组织还启动了一个小型住房信贷项目，提供小额贷款，资助人们维修、翻新和建成他们的房屋。

5  另外，教育也是扶贫的一个关键因素。一个非营利组织向包括大洋洲和美洲国家许多地方的贫困儿童提供笔记本电脑，使他们能够轻松获得信息以及建立与世界的联系。这些笔记本电脑是专门为儿童设计的，它们小巧、耐用，可接收无线信号。

6  中国也实施了教育方面的扶贫措施。除了通过提供书籍和文具来满足偏远地区贫困儿童的学习需求外，中国还努力提高儿童学习和勤奋的积极性。一所云南的乡村小学给 20 名学习成绩优异的学生每人奖励了一只小猪。这是对孩子们努力学习的认可，同时也是帮助他们的家庭改善经济条件的实用礼物。学校的一位老师说，他们希望孩子们懂得，自力更生比依赖别人的帮助更重要。据报道，几个月后，其中一只猪的重量超过了 150 公斤，并以 4000 多元的价格售出，这增加这个养猪家庭的收入。

7  上面的例子有没有启发你想出更多帮助贫困人群的方法呢？通过发挥我们的想象力，我们有望建立一个没有贫困的世界。

## Extended reading

**直播助力花农致富**

1  大学毕业几年后，小李回到农村老家，成为一名花农。每天他都会早起照料花朵；下午修剪和包装鲜花；夜间装箱完毕，准备发货。

2  这些花的最终去向，小李那时并不清楚。从中国云南的花田里，他把花卖给全国经销商，经销商再卖给花店，最后由消费者购买。他想象着自己美丽劳动的成果，在不同的城市里给许多家庭带去了亮丽的色彩。花草种植是他的家族数代相传的营生。然而，随着 2020 年新冠疫情的到来，他们的生意变得岌岌可危。由于无法运输，他只能眼睁睁地看着鲜花的价格不断下跌，却依然卖不出去。最后，数以万计的花都烂掉了。"它们都变成了垃圾，只好扔掉，"小李如是说。

3  几个星期后，他收到了一个在鲜花分销公司工作的老朋友发来的信息。她说，中国最大的一家在线零售商正在帮助农民通过直播来找到买家。卖家需要每天花几个小时在其应用程序上，推销不同的产品并回答潜在买家的问题。该公司将提供物流服务，并从销售额中提取少量抽成。她问小李愿不愿意加入。

4  在那时的中国乡村，小李所面临的现实是一种常态，因为当时市场几乎陷入了停顿。在之前的那几年里，互联网的影响尚未触及农业领域。农民们虽然习惯于在日常生活中通过智能手机上网，但仍然严重依赖线下市场销售他们的商品。但当集市关闭、运输瘫痪，即使人们对农产品的需求上升，他们也不得不扔掉变质的产品。

5  但是危机也提供了机会。中国政府采取了一系列措施，支持贫困地区农产品的销售，提高农村居民的收入，包括开展"消费扶贫"行动。为了响应这一号召，大公司迅速推出了农村直播项目。他们帮助农民和商户建立网上商店，并指导他们如何设计直播内容。大公司把其应用程序变得更直观，并提供物流网络将农产品直接运送到购买者手中。过去种植者有 90% 的产品在线下买卖，现在已经变成了 90% 在线上销售。直播不仅帮助该行业渡过了危机——它还建立了一种全新的商业模式，并且这种商业模式很可能会在疫情结束后依然持续下去。

6  小李和他的朋友决定加入。直播的第一个星期如今回忆起来有些模糊。他们为顾客建立了一个线上商店，并准备了每天一到两小时的内容脚本。小李就站在自己的花田里直播。他带领大家参观种花的苗圃，展示各种鲜花，并讲解如何照料它们。他的工作时间比以前更长。但是，当他在第一天卖出了 100 份订单时，他知道这事儿有戏。

7  花农们还与愿意免费帮助他们推广鲜花的网红们建立了合作。花农提供专业知识，向网红传授鲜花知识以及插花的技巧。有一次，一位网红的直播吸引了超过一百万的观众。更多的订单蜂拥而至，小李也开始有了自己的粉丝。他还记得，他一度没有足够的人手来完成订单。到收获季节结束时，他已售出几十万朵花。他的生意渡过了难关。

8  小李并不是唯一一个因为新趋势而改变生活的人。即使生活和工作已经恢复正常，消费者和种植者都继续依赖着直播。对于小李来说，这一趋势代表了他的生活方式早已该得到的迭代。这曾是疫情中的一线希望。他也喜欢现在与客户之间的直接联系。他们彼此不再是素未谋面的陌生人。当然，并非每件事都是完美的。他承认，在满足客户要求方面他有了额外的压力。在疫情期间，他卖的花是一种罕见的品种，很少有人认得。他因此收到了很多信息，询问如何打理这种花——这是他以前从未经历过的售后服务环节。

9  同时，小李也为自己拥有粉丝感到既羞涩又自豪。"我今天早上刚看过，"他说，"差不多超过了 3000 人"。他对这个数字轻描淡写，并对网红们吸引的粉丝数量表示了羡慕。即便如此，他也感到了有些欲罢不能。他说："一旦你开始直播，你真的停不下来。因为现在你有了粉丝。"
